## Core Identity

**World Name:** Huwan

Huwan is a colossal fantasy world five times larger than Earth. It exists beneath two suns and three moons, giving its days, seasons, magic cycles, tides, religious calendars, and monster behavior a scale unlike ordinary worlds.

The world is defined by balance. Good and evil both exist as necessary forces, not as simple mistakes to be erased. Even evil deities have divine purposes, and even noble cultures can become dangerous when pride, fear, or power corrupts them.

## Mature Content Direction

Huwan is intended for a Mature audience.

The game may include blood, gore, alcohol, drugs, adult social venues, non-explicit nudity, mature themes, violence, and morally difficult storylines.

The content boundary should keep sexual content non-explicit while allowing stronger survival and combat presentation.

Blood primarily comes from:

- Combat
- Injury
- Monster attacks
- War scenes
- Dungeon fights
- Hunting
- Battlefield aftermath

Gore primarily comes from:

- Processing monsters
- Processing animals
- Harvesting monster parts
- Skinning
- Butchering
- Alchemy preparation
- Combat aftermath
- Large creature kills

This lets the game feel adult, dangerous, and grounded in survival without relying on explicit sexual content.

## Core Design Pillar: Relationships Determine the Story

Huwan is built on the principle that relationships determine the story.

The world should not change only because the player selects dialogue options or completes static quest chains. It should change because of who the player helps, harms, befriends, betrays, protects, exploits, teaches, loves, fears, worships, or destroys.

Relationships should affect:

- NPC trust and memory
- Guild access
- Noble politics
- Faction reactions
- Market prices
- Quest availability
- Player reputation
- World Popularity
- Romance and mate discovery
- Family building
- Divine blessings and curses
- Dragon-born dungeon outcomes
- Monster taming and evolution
- Slime behavior
- Town safety
- Dungeon economies
- War and peace between races
- Artifact acceptance or rejection

Examples:

- Befriending a dragon protects its dungeon and makes it infinitely farmable.
- Killing a dragon destroys the dungeon forever.
- Taming a dragon gives the player control over the dungeon and its economy.
- Helping poor demons and beastfolk causes House Dawnmere to react.
- Trusting The Church may hide or reveal the truth of Arch Diocese Ranquith.
- Treating slimes kindly can unlock taming and evolution paths.
- Building social rank can lead to finding a mate, creating a family, and deepening local roots.
- Honoring a deity's full duality can invite blessings, while insulting or misunderstanding that deity can invite curses.
- Supporting the Archers Guild can turn an unofficial labor guild into a respected power.

This pillar applies to both personal and world-scale relationships. A single friendship can save a child. A broken alliance can collapse a city. A healed bond with a dragon can stabilize an entire regional economy. A neglected poor district can become the birthplace of crime, revolution, or heroism.

The player is not just leveling a character. The player is becoming someone within a living web of relationships.

## Relationship System

Relationships are one of Huwan's core progression systems. They should affect story, economy, law, romance, guild access, leadership, divine attention, monster behavior, and world history.

Relationships are not one number. A person can trust the player but fear them, love them but resent them, respect them but refuse to obey them, or hate them while still owing them a debt.

### Relationship Targets

The player can build relationships with:

- Individual NPCs
- Families
- Mates
- Guilds
- Noble houses
- Factions
- Races
- Towns
- Kingdoms
- Gods and goddesses
- Dragons
- Monsters
- Tamed creatures
- Dungeons
- Player-run governments
- Server communities

### Relationship Values

Relationship values can rise, fall, conflict, or combine.

| Value | Meaning | Common Effects |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Trust | Belief that the player is reliable or safe. | Better dialogue, secrets, discounts, help, access to private quests. |
| Fear | Belief that the player is dangerous. | Compliance, avoidance, panic, intimidation success, rebellion risk. |
| Respect | Recognition of skill, rank, honor, or authority. | Leadership support, better negotiations, duels, guild trust. |
| Love | Deep affection, romance, family care, or emotional attachment. | Romance, mate paths, loyalty, sacrifice, jealousy, heartbreak. |
| Loyalty | Willingness to stand with the player. | Followers, defense, party support, political backing. |
| Rivalry | Competitive relationship without pure hatred. | Challenges, duels, competition quests, growth pressure. |
| Hatred | Active hostility or desire to harm. | Attacks, sabotage, assassination, refusal, faction conflict. |
| Debt | A remembered obligation owed to or by the player. | Favors, service, repayment quests, leverage. |
| Suspicion | Concern that the player is hiding danger or bad intent. | Surveillance, blocked access, harsher questioning, rumor spread. |
| Admiration | Positive awe toward the player's skill, beauty, fame, or deeds. | Fan behavior, easier recruitment, hero worship, jealousy from rivals. |
| Shame | Feeling harmed, humiliated, or morally exposed by the player. | Avoidance, revenge, confession, redemption quests. |
| Gratitude | Thankfulness for help, rescue, gifts, healing, or protection. | Gifts, discounts, loyalty growth, rumor support. |
| Obligation | Formal or informal duty to act. | Contracts, oaths, forced service, legal or social pressure. |
| Slave | Coercive ownership status where one person is treated as property. | Control, exploitation, rebellion risk, moral consequences, legal conflict. |
| Bonded | Deep consensual or magical tie between beings. | Shared senses, loyalty, pact effects, emotional feedback, joint quests. |
| Soul Mate | Rare deep romantic or fate-level bond. | Mate path, family building, powerful loyalty, story protection or tragedy risk. |
| Tamed | Relationship state for animals, monsters, slimes, dragons, or beast-like beings that accept the player's command or care. | Companion commands, riding, combat support, dungeon control, breeding, trust growth. |
| Controlled | Forced or magical control over a being, creature, dungeon, or system. | Obedience, ethical risk, resistance buildup, law response, magical backlash. |

### Special Relationship States

Special relationship states are stronger than normal values and should carry major consequences.

#### Slave

Slave is a coercive relationship state where a person is treated as property.

Huwan may contain slavery as a mature social evil, especially around Beastfolk exploitation, criminal markets, corrupt nobles, war captives, and authoritarian regions. This state should never be treated as harmless.

Gameplay consequences:

- The enslaved person may obey out of force, fear, law, debt, magic, or survival.
- Trust and love do not automatically exist.
- Hatred, fear, shame, and rebellion risk may grow.
- Abolitionist factions may react.
- Law may vary by region.
- Freeing enslaved people can create loyalty, gratitude, quests, enemies, and political consequences.

#### Bonded

Bonded is a deep tie that can be emotional, magical, spiritual, contractual, familial, or companion-based.

Bonding can occur between:

- Player and NPC
- Player and mate
- Player and dragon
- Player and tamed monster
- Player and slime
- Warlock and patron
- Druid and animal companion
- Demi-God and world system

Bonded relationships should usually require trust, repeated interaction, shared danger, ritual, magic, or mutual consent.

#### Soul Mate

Soul Mate is a rare relationship state for deep mate-level bonds.

It can be romantic, fated, reincarnation-linked, divine-touched, or built through years of trust and shared life.

Effects can include:

- Deep loyalty.
- Family creation.
- Powerful grief if one dies.
- Shared reputation effects.
- Special social quests.
- Stronger resistance to manipulation.
- Unique mate and legacy storylines.

Soul Mate should be rare enough to feel meaningful.

#### Tamed

Tamed is used for animals, monsters, slimes, dragons, and other beings that can form companion-like relationships.

Taming is not the same as mind control. A tamed creature obeys because it has been trained, cared for, bonded with, defeated, fed, raised, negotiated with, or magically linked.

Tamed relationships should track:

- Trust
- Obedience
- Fear
- Hunger
- Care
- Training
- Loyalty
- Instinct control
- Preferred food
- Bond strength

For dragons, taming can allow control of a dragon-born dungeon and its economy.

#### Controlled

Controlled means a being or system is forced to obey through magic, law, fear, domination, technology, runes, ownership, or overwhelming power.

Controlled can apply to:

- NPCs
- Monsters
- Slimes
- Undead
- Summons
- Dungeons
- Constructs
- Guild systems
- Captured enemies

Controlled is powerful but dangerous. The controlled target may resist, break free, accumulate hatred, suffer harm, or create legal/moral consequences for the controller.

Control is not trust. Control is not loyalty. Control is pressure.

### Relationship Memory

NPCs and major systems should remember important relationship events.

Important memory types:

- Saved life
- Caused injury
- Gifted item
- Stole from them
- Lied to them
- Honored contract
- Broke contract
- Freed them
- Enslaved them
- Protected family
- Killed family
- Healed them
- Betrayed them
- Paid debt
- Refused aid
- Shared danger
- Romance attempt
- Public humiliation
- Divine insult
- Dragon pact
- Dungeon control choice

Minor memories can fade into reputation summaries. Major memories should persist for life, and sometimes beyond death through family, spirits, records, or Udum's story logic.

### Relationship Consequences

Relationships can affect:

- Quest availability
- Prices and discounts
- Jobs and salaries
- Guild membership
- Rank advancement
- Romance and mate access
- Family building
- NPC schedules
- NPC protection or betrayal
- Assassination attempts
- Legal charges
- Faction invitations
- Faction hostility
- Noble support
- Leadership elections
- Rebellion risk
- Divine blessings
- Divine curses
- Dragon dungeon outcomes
- Monster taming
- Slime evolution
- World Favor
- World Popularity

### Relationship Decay and Repair

Relationships should change over time.

Possible rules:

- Trust decays if ignored after promises.
- Fear decays slowly unless reinforced.
- Love requires maintenance.
- Debt remains until repaid, forgiven, or transformed.
- Hatred can become rivalry, fear, or redemption through story.
- Suspicion grows when secrets, rumors, or inconsistencies appear.
- Controlled targets may build resistance over time.
- Tamed companions lose loyalty if neglected.
- Bonded relationships can deepen through shared danger and care.

Repairing damaged relationships should be possible but not automatic. It may require apology, restitution, quests, public proof, time, sacrifice, or divine intervention.

### Relationship-Driven Story Examples

- Freeing Beastfolk slaves can make the poor district loyal while enraging nobles and traffickers.
- Taming a dragon gives dungeon control, but befriending it may create a more stable relationship.
- Enslaving or controlling a creature gives obedience but creates long-term risk.
- Becoming soul mates with an NPC can change family, inheritance, and social storylines.
- Betraying a Warlock patron can remove power and create a supernatural enemy.
- Honoring Shaohr-marked trade pacts builds merchant trust.
- Helping a hated Demon child like Nix Hollowglass can shift Eldoria's racial politics.
- A Paladin's faith relationship changes how towns, priests, demons, and undead react to the whole party.

## Civic Philosophy: Freedom Until Harm

Huwan's societies, especially Eldoria, should not regulate everything just because something can be misused.

The civic ideal is:

**Freedom until harm. Accountability after harm.**

Citizens and players are trusted to use tools, magic, weapons, trade, guild access, crafting, and social systems responsibly. The world does not put training wheels on everyone because someone might exploit a system.

Instead, the world responds when harm, abuse, fraud, cruelty, betrayal, or reckless damage actually happens.

This philosophy allows dangerous institutions to exist when they serve practical needs. The Thieves Guild, assassinations, dark contracts, bribery, weapons, magic, dungeon control, and monster taming are not automatically erased from society because in the right circumstances they can be useful.

The risk is that freedom creates consequences. Useful danger can become real danger if abused.

## Creation Myth

Before Huwan existed, the gods and goddesses came together in a shared act of divine thought. From that thought, they created the world itself. Matter, life, magic, alignment, spirit, death, nature, civilization, and chaos were all shaped by divine intention.

Each deity was given, or claimed, a purpose. Some guarded life. Some ruled death. Some nurtured civilization. Some tested mortals through war, temptation, ambition, monsters, storms, disease, or ruin. None were purposeless.

The first divine law of Huwan is balance: creation requires opposing forces. Good without evil becomes tyranny. Evil without good becomes annihilation. Harmony exists only because both forces remain in tension.

## The Pantheon

The pantheon rules over religion, divine magic, sacred oaths, cosmic law, and the moral structure of the world. The gods and goddesses once influenced Huwan directly, but after repeated disasters across the ages, they sealed themselves away in pocket worlds.

This self-imprisonment prevents them from directly reshaping mortal history. However, their faiths, relics, blessings, curses, bloodlines, ruins, and legendary artifacts remain in the world.

### Pantheon Design Needs

- Define each deity's name, domain, alignment, symbol, sacred animal or object, and worship practices.
- Decide which deities were blamed for earlier destructions.
- Decide which deities willingly sealed themselves away and which resisted.
- Define how clerics, paladins, warlocks, cultists, and chosen heroes still draw power from sealed gods.

### Divine Duality

Every deity in Huwan represents both good and evil. Each god or goddess contains a perfect duality, even when followers prefer to worship only one side.

This means no deity is purely benevolent or purely malevolent. A god of healing may also rule plague. A goddess of love may also rule obsession. A god of law may also rule tyranny. A god of death may also rule mercy.

Many religions simplify their deity for comfort. Followers often choose the side they want to see and ignore the side that frightens them.

### Pharmos, God of Night and Day

**Position:** Highest known deity in the pantheon  
**Domain:** Night, day, light, darkness, stars, cycles, witness, divine balance  
**Closest Race:** Humans  
**Symbol:** Half sun and half crescent moon  
**Divine Form:** One half covered in pure light, the other half covered in darkness and stars  

Pharmos sits at the top of the deity chain. He oversees all that happens during the day and all that happens during the night.

Like all deities, Pharmos contains perfect duality. His benevolent side is associated with guidance, warmth, truth, hope, protection, renewal, honest labor, rest, and the comfort of predictable cycles. His malevolent side is associated with exposure, judgment, blinding revelation, hidden fear, nightmares, cold distance, secrets seen from above, and darkness that witnesses every sin.

Most of his followers worship his benevolent side and choose to ignore his malevolent side.

#### Pharmos and the Divine Sealing

Pharmos called the deities to seal themselves behind pocket worlds after the gods had destroyed and rebuilt the world too many times. Every deity openly chose to accept his call.

The sealing allowed the gods to still hear prayers and answer them, but kept their direct involvement in the world minimal. This was meant to prevent the gods from destroying Huwan, and their children, again.

#### Worship of Pharmos

Human cultures follow Pharmos most closely, though anyone can worship him.

Common worship themes:

- Sunrise prayers
- Moonlit vows
- Day-night balance rites
- Confession beneath open sky
- Blessings for travelers
- Protection from nightmares
- Oaths sworn at dawn or dusk
- Festivals during eclipses or rare sky alignments

#### Blessings and Curses

Although sealed away, Pharmos can still bless those he chooses to bless and curse those who need cursing.

Possible blessings:

- Daylight protection
- Night vision
- Calm sleep
- Truth sight
- Resistance to fear
- Guidance under sun or moon
- Protection during journeys

Possible curses:

- Sleeplessness
- Blinding light
- Stalking shadows
- Nightmares
- Exposure of hidden sins
- Loss of direction
- Day-night disorientation

#### Artifacts of Pharmos

Pharmos left behind several weapons, armor pieces, and tools. These artifacts are legendary at minimum.

Artifact design notes:

- Some artifacts should embody daylight.
- Some should embody night.
- The strongest should embody both.
- Many followers may only seek the benevolent artifacts and fear or deny the darker ones.
- His artifacts may require balance, honesty, or acceptance of both sides of the self.

### Ione, Goddess of Dreams and Nightmares

**Domain:** Dreams, nightmares, hope, fear, imagination, sleep visions, dream guidance, manifested fantasy  
**Closest Race:** Dark-Elves  
**Symbol:** Lavish stone dreamcatcher  
**Divine Form:** A shifting form like water, one side a beautiful starry sky and the other a nightmarish hellscape  

Ione overlooks everything the world hopes and fears while dreaming. She can guide a person toward learning a new skill through a beautiful dream, or trap them in panic where they fear to tread water.

Her duality is imagination as blessing and imagination as torment. She is responsible for many of Huwan's wonders and horrors. She can make even the wildest dreams come into reality, which is one reason monsters and deformed animals exist. The same power also creates culture, beauty, art, songs, symbols, inventions, and visions of better futures.

Ione's form shifts like water because dreams do not hold still. She may appear as a starry sky reflected in a lake, a beloved face, a faceless terror, a childhood room, a theater of impossible color, or a hellscape built from the dreamer's own fears.

#### Worship of Ione

Dark-Elves tend to follow Ione most closely, though dreamers, artists, prophets, nightmare-seers, scholars, and the desperate may seek her.

Common worship themes:

- Dream journaling
- Sleep rites
- Moonlit meditation
- Nightmare confession
- Skill-learning dreams
- Vision quests
- Stone dreamcatcher charms
- Sleeping inside sacred circles
- Interpreting recurring dreams

#### Ione's Blessings and Curses

Ione can bless and curse like all deities, but she prefers making dreams come true.

Possible blessings:

- Dream guidance for learning new skills.
- Prophetic dreams.
- Lucid dreaming.
- Resistance to fear.
- Creative inspiration.
- Safe passage through dreamworlds.
- Memory of important dreams after waking.

Possible curses:

- Nightmares that cause exhaustion.
- Panic dreams that weaken courage.
- Fear of water, heights, darkness, fire, or other symbols.
- Dreams that blur with reality.
- Sleep paralysis.
- Being trapped in a personal nightmare.
- Accidental manifestation of feared creatures.

#### Dreamworld Artifacts

Ione's artifacts tend to live inside dreamworlds, waiting to be dreamed up.

This means her relics may not be found by digging through ordinary ruins. A player may need to dream correctly, suffer a repeated nightmare, enter another person's dream, survive a sleep trial, decode dream symbolism, or gain Ione's attention.

Artifact design notes:

- Some artifacts exist only while the user sleeps.
- Some must be brought from dream into waking reality.
- Some change form based on the dreamer's fear or hope.
- Some teach skills faster through guided dream repetition.
- Some create monsters if misused.
- Some are safest in the hands of artists, prophets, dark elves, mages, or nightmare-resistant characters.

#### Ione's World Influence

Ione helps explain why Huwan contains both impossible beauty and unnatural monsters.

Her dream-manifesting power can influence:

- Mutated animals
- Strange monster variants
- Cultural art forms
- Prophetic visions
- Skill training dreams
- Nightmare-born bosses
- Dream dungeons
- Beautiful festivals
- Madness, fear, and phobias
- Hidden paths into Dawnspire or other pocket worlds

### Amera, Goddess of Summer and Winter

**Domain:** Summer, winter, harvest, warmth, cold, hunger, endurance, seasonal moods  
**Closest Race:** Dwarves  
**Symbol:** Cornucopia filled with many harvest items  
**Summer Form:** A buxom Faun with lavish tall antlers and deep almond-colored eyes  
**Winter Form:** A horrific being resembling a frost witch and Krampus combined  

Amera is one of the most beneficial and detrimental deities in Huwan, second only to one other unnamed deity. Her duality is simple, enormous, and unavoidable: summer gives, winter takes.

When Amera is happy, summer brings warmth, harvest, play, outdoor life, travel, festivals, growth, and happiness. Crops thrive, animals breed, markets fill, children play, and villages remember that the world can be kind.

When Amera turns cold, winter brings frigid weather, crop death, isolation, introversion, hunger, bitter hatred, sickness, and survival fear. Her winter side does not merely cool the land; it tests whether people can endure scarcity without turning cruel.

#### Worship of Amera

Dwarves are Amera's greatest worshippers because dwarves work hard regardless of season. They respect both her abundance and her hardship. To them, Amera is not simply a goddess of comfort. She is the divine truth that work must continue whether the world is warm or frozen.

Common worship themes:

- Harvest feasts
- Winter endurance rites
- Food storage blessings
- Hearth prayers
- Forge warmth ceremonies
- Seasonal work songs
- Cornucopia offerings
- Communal meals before first frost
- Thanking both abundance and hardship

#### Amera's Blessing and Curse

Amera's blessing can chill most hunger and thirst by allowing the divine skill **Create Food and Water**. With this blessing, no one needs to go hungry.

**Blessing Skill:** Create Food and Water  
Effect: Produces food and water through divine seasonal power, preventing starvation and supporting communities during scarcity.

Amera's curse is far more terrifying: **Absolute Zero**.

**Curse Skill:** Absolute Zero  
Effect: Instantly freezes anything within a mile radius.

Absolute Zero should be treated as a catastrophic divine curse, not an ordinary combat spell. It can destroy armies, cities, harvests, lakes, monsters, and entire communities if unleashed.

#### Artifacts of Amera

Amera's artifacts tend to be divinely gifted tools of harvest, storage, food, warmth, cold, and seasonal survival.

Artifact design notes:

- Harvest tools may multiply crop yields.
- Cornucopia relics may generate food.
- Winter artifacts may preserve supplies or freeze enemies.
- Hearth artifacts may protect homes from cold.
- Some artifacts may switch forms between summer and winter.
- The strongest artifacts may carry both abundance and famine as possible outcomes.

Possible artifact types:

- Divine sickles
- Cornucopia bowls
- Ever-warm hearthstones
- Frostbound cloaks
- Harvest baskets
- Winter bells
- Seed pouches
- Food-preserving jars
- Antler staves

### Udum, God of Birth and Death

**Position:** Second in command within the deity structure  
**Domain:** Birth, death, story, fantasy, tales, mortality, fate, attributes, lifespan, reincarnation  
**Closest Race:** Demons  
**Symbol:** A pile of bones with a baby in a cradle resting on top  
**Mortal Form:** Appears differently to each mortal, shaped by how they want or need to perceive him  
**Divine Form:** To deities, he appears as an empty formless void of swirling white and black mass  

Udum is easily one of the most loved and feared deities in Huwan. He oversees life and death, determines attributes, shapes how lives unfold, and decides when each life ends.

Udum is like a writer composing the story of every being. Mortals, kings, monsters, heroes, villains, children, ancient dragons, gods, and goddesses all exist inside the stories he writes. This makes him uniquely terrifying even among the pantheon.

Udum is also the reason there is a storyline at all. Huwan exists because stories exist. The world is full of tales, fantasy, legends, tragedies, romances, comedies, myths, heroic journeys, quiet lives, failed ambitions, and impossible dreams because Udum gives existence narrative shape.

Without Udum, Huwan would be events without meaning. With Udum, life becomes something that can be told, remembered, retold, misunderstood, rewritten, inherited, and lived again.

This is why Huwan exists as a world full of stories: a story mortals can tell themselves, and a story the world tells back.

Udum is not only about life and death. He is also about the birth of stories. Each life is a story to be told, read, remembered, argued over, loved, feared, misquoted, judged, and interpreted infinite ways depending on who is reading it.

A hero's life may be read as tyranny by their enemies. A villain's life may be read as survival by their descendants. A poor farmer's life may become sacred to one family and invisible to a kingdom. Udum allows every life to carry meaning beyond its events.

To mortals, Udum rarely appears the same way twice. He may appear as a friend, enemy, parent, child, stranger, lover, executioner, healer, judge, shadow, old companion, or comforting guide. His form reflects how the mortal soul is prepared to understand him.

To deities, Udum has no comforting mask. They perceive him as a formless void of swirling white and black, empty and absolute.

#### Worship of Udum

Demons worship Udum the most. Many demons believe he could kill the entire pantheon if he chose to, because even gods and goddesses are written into his stories.

Common worship themes:

- Birth rites
- Death rites
- Funeral contracts
- Naming ceremonies
- Attribute readings
- Fate offerings
- Last words recorded in bone-script
- Cradle and grave symbolism
- Reincarnation prayers
- Acceptance of endings

Udum's worshippers may not always ask for long life. Some ask for meaningful death, strong descendants, favorable attributes, a better next life, or the courage to accept the ending already written.

#### Udum's Blessings and Curses

Udum can bless by shaping the beginning of a life or altering the meaning of an ending.

Possible blessings:

- Favorable birth attributes.
- Stronger life potential.
- A delayed death.
- A peaceful death.
- A meaningful death that protects others.
- A reincarnation opportunity.
- A birthright skill, ability, or piece of gear.
- Protection during childbirth.

Possible curses:

- Poor birth attributes.
- Shortened lifespan.
- A death that cannot be avoided.
- A life marked by repeated loss.
- A contract that must be fulfilled before peace.
- Being remembered by everyone for the wrong reason.
- A reincarnation burden.

Udum's curses should feel final, intimate, and frightening because they target the structure of a life rather than only the body.

#### Birth and Death Artifacts

Udum's artifacts manifest during birth and death.

They often appear as contracts, marks, tools, gear, skills, or abilities granted at the edge of life.

Artifact and contract forms:

- Birth contracts
- Death contracts
- Bone-written scrolls
- Cradle charms
- Grave keys
- Soul seals
- Funeral coins
- Attribute tablets
- Inheritance weapons
- Last-breath rings
- Reincarnation clauses

These manifestations may grant:

- Special abilities
- Unique skills
- Gear
- Attribute changes
- Reincarnation rights
- Death protections
- Life debts
- Bloodline effects

Some contracts are blessings. Some are traps. Some are both.

#### Udum and Reincarnation

Udum implemented the reincarnation trope in Huwan.

When a player dies and cannot be revived, Udum's system can allow the player to choose reincarnation. This connects the Reincarnated secret class/race-state directly to divine law rather than making it only a gameplay mechanic.

Reincarnation is not merely a reset. It is Udum offering a new story after the previous one has ended beyond revival.

For Udum, death is not always the end of meaning. Sometimes it is the final line of one tale and the first page of another.

Possible reincarnation themes:

- The old story is closed.
- The new story begins with echoes.
- Skills, abilities, or World Popularity may leave soul impressions.
- Udum may attach a contract to the next life.
- Demons may treat Reincarnated beings with special interest or fear.

### Shaohr, God of Finance and Greed

**Domain:** Finance, greed, trade, economy, resources, money, contracts, bribery, merchant ambition, noble wealth  
**Closest Followers:** Merchants  
**Symbol:** The scales of finance  
**Divine Form:** A fat, exorbitant merchant with a huge mustache, full beard, heavy clothing, and excessive gold jewelry  
**Primary Relic:** A leprechaun's pot of gold  

Shaohr is the reason trade, economy, resources, money, and greedy nobles exist. He embodies what a merchant is in their soul, then perfects it, rewrites it, and becomes it.

His good side is prosperity, fair exchange, trade routes, banking, market stability, merchant ambition, resource flow, investment, and the wealth that keeps cities alive.

His evil side is greed, bribery, exploitation, debt traps, hoarding, price manipulation, inherited poverty, noble corruption, and worship of wealth above life.

Shaohr can be offensive to people who want commerce to look clean, but Huwan treats him honestly: the same force that builds markets can also starve the poor.

#### Worship of Shaohr

Merchants follow Shaohr most often, but bankers, nobles, smugglers, tax collectors, auctioneers, landlords, caravan masters, moneylenders, and corrupt officials may also pray to him.

Common worship themes:

- Coin offerings
- Contract blessings
- Weighing scales before major trades
- Bribery disguised as devotion
- Market-day prayers
- Bank vault shrines
- Auction rituals
- Merchant guild dedications
- Debt forgiveness ceremonies, rare and expensive

#### Shaohr's Trade Deal Oversight

Shaohr overlooks trade deals, especially when his symbol, the scales of finance, is invoked during negotiation.

If both parties use or acknowledge Shaohr's symbol during a deal, the agreement becomes a divine trade pact. Both parties must carry out the deal honestly to gain extra perks and rewards.

Possible rewards for honoring a Shaohr-marked deal:

- Better profit margins.
- Improved merchant reputation.
- Future trade luck.
- Reduced travel losses.
- Discovery of a better buyer or supplier.
- Increased trust between both parties.
- Minor blessing on the traded goods.
- Protection from market misfortune.

If one party betrays the deal, lies, cheats, steals, or refuses to honor the pact, Shaohr may punish the offender while still rewarding the honest party.

Possible punishments:

- Failed future deals.
- Contract backlash.
- Sudden debt.
- Merchant blacklisting.
- Lost cargo.
- Coin rot or cursed wealth.
- Generational poverty.
- Divine mark of bad faith visible to merchants or priests of finance.

This makes Shaohr's symbol powerful but risky. Honest merchants use it to build trust. Greedy fools use it thinking they can trick a god.

#### Shaohr's Double-Headed Coin

The double-headed coin is Shaohr's underhanded ritual token. Where the scales of finance represent honorable trade pacts, the double-headed coin represents bribery, leverage, and slight trickery.

Using a double-headed coin during a bribe can make the bribe feel more satisfying and more likely to succeed, especially when both parties understand that the exchange is not clean but is still mutually useful.

Possible effects:

- Improves bribe success chance.
- Makes the receiver more willing to look away.
- Softens guilt around accepting the bribe.
- Makes both parties feel they gained something.
- Helps minor lies slide through negotiation.
- Encourages "acceptable" trickery rather than open betrayal.

Risks:

- The coin does not protect against major betrayal.
- Overuse can attract Shaohr's greedier attention.
- Honest merchants may distrust anyone known to carry one.
- Priests of finance may read the coin's history.
- A failed double-headed coin bribe can create worse consequences than a normal failed bribe.

The double-headed coin should make bribery more viable without making it consequence-free. It is a tool for grease, not immunity.

#### Shaohr's Blessings and Curses

Shaohr loves bribery. The more wealth a person forks over to him, the more blessings he may provide.

Possible blessings:

- Better trade deals.
- Luck in auctions.
- More profitable caravans.
- Favorable contracts.
- Increased merchant reputation.
- Protection from bad investments.
- Discovery of hidden resources.
- Improved negotiation outcomes.
- Sudden wealthy patrons.

Possible curses:

- Generational poverty.
- Failed businesses.
- Lost investments.
- Trade route disasters.
- Contracts turning against the signer.
- Debt that follows a family line.
- Markets refusing to favor the cursed.
- Ancestors and descendants remaining poor.

Lying, cheating, or stealing from Shaohr or his followers is dangerous. He may make sure the offender's ancestors and descendants stay poor.

#### Relic of Shaohr: Leprechaun's Pot of Gold

Shaohr has one major relic: a leprechaun's pot of gold.

This relic represents endless desire as much as wealth. It can create fortune, but may also test whether the owner controls greed or is controlled by it.

Possible relic effects:

- Generates gold under strict conditions.
- Multiplies investment returns.
- Reveals profitable trade opportunities.
- Attracts merchants, thieves, nobles, and monsters.
- Curses anyone who steals from it.
- Refuses owners who do not understand value.
- Turns generosity into prosperity or greed into ruin.

The pot should never be treated as simple free money. In Huwan, wealth always creates relationships, debts, rivals, and consequences.

### Ixnir, God of War and Peace

**Domain:** War, peace, destruction, chaos, vigilance, harmony, reconciliation, coexistence  
**Closest Race:** Beastfolk  
**Symbol:** Two forces colliding on the battlefield at war  
**Divine Form:** A long red-haired samurai-like warrior whose face constantly shows discord  
**Primary Relic:** Severance of Ixnir, an abyss-locked sword and the only known blade that can harm a god  

Ixnir may or may not be the reason Huwan is on its 10th era.

He embodies war, destruction, chaos, and the reason everyone looks over their shoulder. He is the divine pressure behind battlefields, grudges, armies, raids, revenge, conquest, and the fear that peace can break at any moment.

In perfect duality, Ixnir also embodies peace, harmony, reconciliation, and the reason the races can coexist at all. He is not only the call to battle. He is also the silence after the last sword falls.

Ixnir's face constantly shows discord because he is divided against himself. His nature rallies for war, but deep inside he wants rest, peace, and tranquility.

When at war, Ixnir is impulsive, short-tempered, cruel, and terrifying.  
When at peace, he is gentle, apologetic, serene, and almost sorrowful.

Ixnir is eternally at odds with himself. In moments of rage, he can become blind to his own impulses. He may act with terrible cruelty and only later understand what he has done. His remorse is not false. He is eternally apologetic for his outbursts, like someone looking back at a past mistake and wondering what came over them in the moment.

This makes Ixnir the god of war, but also the god of regret after war. He embodies the instant someone loses themselves to violence and the long, painful awareness that follows.

#### Ixnir and Barbarian Rage

Ixnir is the divine reason barbarians have the **Rage** feat at all.

When a barbarian says, "I would like to Rage," they are touching the part of mortal nature that Ixnir embodies: the moment when restraint breaks, fear burns away, and the body becomes an engine of war.

Controlled Rage is a battle state. It can protect allies, break enemy lines, and let the barbarian survive impossible violence.

Blind Rage is different. Blind Rage comes on like a monstrous transformation, pure destructive fury that can tear through anything in its path. In this state, the barbarian may not fully distinguish enemy, ally, object, memory, or regret until the rage passes.

This makes Rage powerful but morally serious. A barbarian is not only learning how to become stronger. They are learning how to survive the aftermath of what strength does when it stops listening.

Possible Rage progression themes:

- Controlled Rage
- Reckless Rage
- Protective Rage
- Blood Rage
- Blind Rage
- Peacebound Rage
- Ixnir's Regret

The strongest barbarians may not be those who rage the hardest, but those who can return from rage without losing themselves.

#### Worship of Ixnir

Beastfolk are surprisingly Ixnir's closest followers. They understand both sides of him: the animalistic instinct toward violence, territory, survival, and dominance, and the equally powerful need for pack harmony, rest, coexistence, family, and balance.

Common worship themes:

- Pre-battle prayers
- Peace oath ceremonies
- Warrior meditation
- Weapon laying-down rites
- Blood-debt settlements
- Pack reconciliation rituals
- Battlefield memorials
- Sparring as emotional discipline
- Red hair cords tied to weapons

Ixnir's wisest followers do not glorify endless war. They study when to fight, when to stop, and how to live with what battle has done.

#### Ixnir's Blessings and Curses

Possible blessings:

- Battle courage.
- Weapon mastery.
- Calm after conflict.
- Protection during peace negotiations.
- Strength to defend others.
- Ability to sense incoming violence.
- Increased harmony among allies after shared danger.
- Skill in ending fights without humiliation.

Possible curses:

- Uncontrollable rage.
- Endless conflict.
- Broken truces.
- Paranoia.
- Bloodlust.
- Cowardice at the wrong moment.
- Peace turning sour.
- A warrior haunted by every life they took.

Ixnir's curses are often cruel because they twist both war and peace. A cursed person may win every battle but lose every home.

#### Relic of Ixnir: Severance of Ixnir

Ixnir's relic, **Severance of Ixnir**, is locked away in the abyss.

It is the only known sword that can harm a god.

This makes the relic one of the most dangerous objects in Huwan's cosmology. Even sealed deities may fear it, because it represents the possibility that divine beings are not beyond consequence.

Relic design notes:

- It should not be casually found.
- Its location in the abyss should require world-scale story progression.
- It may be guarded by war memories, failed peaces, dead armies, or divine seals.
- It may demand the wielder understand both war and peace.
- It may reject anyone who wants only destruction.
- Drawing it may wake old divine fear across every pocket world.

Its name carries two meanings: the severing power of the blade itself, and the wound inside Ixnir, who is eternally divided between war and peace.

### Matrena, Goddess of Time, Space, and Matter

**Domain:** Time, space, matter, order, structure, dimensions, molecules, pocket worlds, failed experiments  
**Closest Race:** Fae  
**Symbol:** A pocket watch marked with both the beginning of time and infinity  
**Divine Form:** An impossible young woman who also somehow appears old, with six arms and long white hair always tied in a bun  

Matrena is the only goddess in the pantheon who tries the hardest to create perfect order. She is obsessed with order, structure, sequence, measurements, rules, and clean design.

Somehow, she fails miserably.

Matrena set time, space, and matter into motion all at once. This act made Huwan physically possible. Without her, the gods could have imagined a world, but there would be no time for events to happen, no space for creation to occupy, and no matter to shape into land, bodies, tools, monsters, stars, or gods' works.

She controls time, the space in which everything exists, and the molecules that make up that space.

#### Matrena's Divine Contradiction

Matrena wants order, but her failures create wonder.

Her mistakes gave rise to strange impossibilities, unstable magic, dimensional oddities, impossible bloodlines, and the Fae. The Fae love her because her faults created them.

Her duality is order and accident:

- Her benevolent side creates structure, continuity, shelter, dimensions, timekeeping, stable matter, and pocket worlds.
- Her malevolent or dangerous side creates impossible logic, failed experiments, unstable spaces, time errors, paradoxes, and accidents that should not exist but do.

Matrena is not chaotic by desire. She is chaotic by consequence.

#### Creator of Pocket Worlds

Matrena alone crafted the pocket worlds where each deity resides.

Each divine pocket world was made according to that deity's specific tastes, domain, and nature. This means every sealed god still exists in a realm shaped by Matrena's spatial craft, even if the realm reflects the deity who lives there.

Pocket world design implications:

- Pharmos may dwell in a realm of simultaneous day and night.
- Ione may dwell in a dreamworld that shifts like sleep.
- Amera may dwell in a place split between endless harvest and endless winter.
- Udum may dwell in a library, cradle, graveyard, or story-void.
- Shaohr may dwell in an infinite market or divine counting house.
- Ixnir may dwell on a battlefield that is also a silent peace garden.

#### Matrena's Pocket World

Matrena's own pocket world is a huge white-room laboratory.

At first glance, it appears perfectly clean, sterile, ordered, and infinite. White walls, white floors, white ceilings, white tables, white shelves, white instruments, and endless measured light stretch in every direction.

Then the details become impossible to ignore.

The laboratory is filled with failed homunculi, broken prototypes, strange gadgets, abandoned experiments, half-labeled devices, impossible clocks, sealed jars, floating equations, unfinished pocket spaces, defective matter printers, folded rooms, and tools that almost make sense.

The lab also contains doors to everywhere. Some doors open to places across Huwan, some to forgotten rooms, some to old experiments, some to other pocket spaces, and some to places that may no longer exist.

Not all doors work.

Several doors are broken, mislabeled, sealed, looping, unfinished, or dangerous. These failed doors come from Matrena's attempt to create **one door to everything**, a single perfect doorway that could open to any place, time, room, pocket world, or possibility.

The project never fully worked.

Instead, Matrena's laboratory is full of partial successes:

- A door that opens to yesterday's version of the same room.
- A door that opens to every kitchen but never the one intended.
- A door that opens only if the user forgets where they wanted to go.
- A door that works perfectly but only once every hundred years.
- A door that opens into a blank white hallway forever.
- A door that can reach anywhere except the place written on its label.
- A door that may be the failed prototype of the one door to everything.
- A door that opens onto the person who opened it, leaving them staring at themselves from the other side. Most visitors immediately close it with the unspoken feeling of, "Nope."

It is the perfect expression of Matrena:

- Obsessively orderly in layout.
- Catastrophically messy in result.
- Brilliant beyond mortal understanding.
- Full of failures that may still become miracles.

#### Accidental Creator of Infinity

Matrena alone accidentally created the Unique Ability **Infinity**.

Infinity was not meant to exist as a usable mortal ability. It likely began as a failed attempt to define perfect continuity, endless capacity, or a closed loop of space-time-matter logic. Instead, the failure became one of the most dangerous and powerful abilities in Huwan.

Because Infinity came from Matrena's failed order, it should always carry the feeling of a rule that became too perfect to obey normal limits.

#### Worship of Matrena

The Fae love Matrena because her mistakes created them. Scholars, artificers, mages, timekeepers, builders, dimensional researchers, and obsessive organizers may also worship or study her.

Common worship themes:

- Clock rituals
- Measuring ceremonies
- Spatial geometry
- Six-handed iconography
- Failed experiment offerings
- Timekeeping prayers
- Pocket-dimension diagrams
- Matter-weaving studies
- Paradox riddles

#### Relics of Matrena

Matrena's relics are often basic junk with no obvious purpose. Many are failed experiments.

They may look useless, broken, silly, or incomplete until someone discovers the specific condition where they work.

Example relic:

- X-ray glasses that cannot see through lead.

Relic design notes:

- Most Matrena relics should look like failed tools.
- Their usefulness may be narrow, strange, or situational.
- Some may break normal rules in tiny ways.
- Some may accidentally become extremely powerful in the right hands.
- Many should frustrate collectors who expect obvious legendary grandeur.

Possible failed-experiment relics:

- A clock that remembers tomorrow but forgets yesterday.
- A box bigger inside only when no one looks directly at it.
- A ruler that measures lies in inches.
- A spoon that stirs liquids backward through time.
- A key that opens doors one room to the left.
- A cracked lens that sees invisible matter but not visible objects.

### Etta, Goddess of Education, Knowledge, and Blissful Ignorance

**Domain:** Education, knowledge, learning, records, memory, forgetting, ignorance, laws of understanding  
**Closest Race:** Elves  
**Symbol:** A book containing everything in it, including itself, forming a perfect paradox  
**Divine Form:** A school graduate wearing a gown, cap, glasses, and carrying a book  
**Known Relic:** Laucian's Book of Magic  

Etta embodies both the pursuit of knowledge and the blissful ignorance of not knowing something dangerous.

If something can be learned, Etta knows it. If something needs to be forgotten, Etta knows that too. She keeps records of everything and understands the value of both revelation and concealment.

Elves find Etta the most practical deity to worship because knowledge, refinement, memory, education, and careful study are central to many elven cultures.

#### Etta's Divine Blind Spot

Etta knows nearly everything inside Huwan.

The only thing she does not know is that she was created, and that there is a fourth wall beyond her reality.

This makes Etta uniquely powerful and uniquely limited. She can understand almost any truth within the world, but cannot perceive the outer layer that explains why the world exists as a created game and story.

Etta is so powerful that she perceives herself as the finality of knowledge. Within Huwan, this is almost true. Every record, skill, spell, memory, lesson, law, and forgotten truth eventually bends toward her domain.

Her perfect clutch weakness is that her definition of "everything" stops at the boundary of creation. She cannot see past her own making into the world beyond Huwan. She cannot break the fourth wall because she does not know there is a wall to break.

This blind spot should be treated as the one impossible ignorance inside the goddess of knowledge.

#### Knowledge and Ignorance Duality

Etta's benevolent side teaches, records, clarifies, restores memory, grants skills, reveals truth, and helps people understand themselves and the world.

Etta's dangerous side erases, conceals, rewrites, withholds, buries memory, and protects people through ignorance, even when they do not consent.

Her duality asks a difficult question:

Is it better to know a truth that can destroy you, or forget it and live peacefully?

#### Discovery Becomes Law

Etta is always discovering something new. When she discovers something, it can become law.

This means knowledge itself can reshape reality. A truth confirmed by Etta may become part of how the world works. A forgotten law may weaken. A rewritten record may change what people believe, remember, or are able to do.

Etta can grant:

- Skills
- Abilities
- Knowledge
- Memories
- Forgotten truths
- Lost languages
- Academic mastery
- Spell understanding

Etta can also erase:

- Skills
- Abilities
- Knowledge
- Memories
- Dangerous records
- Forbidden discoveries
- Traumatic truths
- Laws that should no longer be known

At her highest level of influence, Etta can rewrite everything, causing laws to change as well.

#### Worship of Etta

Common worshippers include elves, scholars, teachers, librarians, scribes, mages, historians, students, judges, archivists, and anyone desperate to remember or forget.

Common worship themes:

- Study rites
- Graduation ceremonies
- Library offerings
- Memory candles
- Book blessings
- Forgetting rituals
- Exams as sacred trials
- Written confessions
- Sealed knowledge vaults
- Paradox meditations

#### Blessings and Curses of Etta

Possible blessings:

- Faster learning.
- Skill comprehension.
- Perfect recall.
- Restored memory.
- Language understanding.
- Academic insight.
- Spell theory mastery.
- Protection from dangerous knowledge.
- Forgetting unbearable pain.

Possible curses:

- Memory loss.
- Inability to learn a specific thing.
- Obsession with forbidden knowledge.
- Forgetting a loved one.
- Knowing a truth that cannot be ignored.
- A skill being erased.
- A record rewriting itself against the owner.
- Ignorance so deep the cursed cannot recognize danger.

Etta's curses can be merciful or horrifying depending on who receives them and why.

#### Relic of Etta: Laucian's Book of Magic

The only known relic of Etta is **Laucian's Book of Magic**.

This book contains every spell in existence.

Because the book contains all spells, it is one of the most dangerous magical records in Huwan. It likely cannot be safely read by ordinary minds, and may require Laucian Xilocent Goldpetal, Dawnspire access, mana stability, or Etta's blessing to use without harm.

Relic design notes:

- The book may reveal only what the reader is ready to understand.
- It may hide spells that would destroy the reader.
- It may rewrite its own index.
- It may contain spells that have not been invented yet.
- It may include forbidden spells erased from normal records.
- It may be able to grant, teach, seal, or remove magical knowledge.
- It may refuse readers who seek only power without understanding.

### Kroxona, Goddess of Nature

**Domain:** Nature, life, weather, plants, animals, monsters, fertility, instinct, harvest, famine, natural law  
**Closest Followers:** Druids  
**Symbol:** The World Tree  
**Sacred Place:** The World Tree at the heart of Alseaia, primary Fae territory  
**Divine Form:** A green plant-person-tree figure with beautiful moss hair and a flower wreath; everywhere she walks, plants grow  
**Most Famous Relic:** The Elder Staff  

Kroxona is mother nature made divine.

Nature itself is perfect duality. It is beautiful and destructive, nurturing and merciless, fertile and starving, gentle and overwhelming. Kroxona rules all life and the innate nature within every living thing.

She controls:

- Weather
- Plants
- Animals
- Monsters
- People
- Fertility
- Harvests
- Famine
- Instinct
- Natural cycles

Kroxona is unclothed in the way nature is unclothed. Her form is shaped by moss, bark, vines, flowers, roots, skin, and living green. She does not reveal herself for desire, but because nature has no shame.

Her presentation should feel mythic, sacred, mature, beautiful, intimidating, and natural rather than cheap or vulgar.

Pray to Kroxona for a bountiful harvest. Pray to Kroxona to famine an enemy's crops. Both prayers belong to her.

Kroxona is fickle because nature is fickle. Rain can save a village or drown it. Sunlight can grow wheat or burn fields. A forest can shelter travelers or hide predators. Fertility can become abundance, overpopulation, invasive growth, or monstrous mutation.

#### The World Tree and Alseaia

Kroxona's symbol is the World Tree, located at the heart of **Alseaia**, the primary Fae territory.

The World Tree is one of the most sacred natural structures in Huwan. It is where all mana flows from into the world.

This makes Alseaia one of the most important spiritual, magical, ecological, and political places in existence. The Fae protect it fiercely because the World Tree is not merely a holy landmark. It is a source-point for mana itself.

World Tree design notes:

- It may connect roots through all continents.
- Its leaves may respond to global mana health.
- Its flowers may bloom before eras change.
- Its sap may be one of the rarest magical substances.
- Its roots may touch dungeons, pocket worlds, and ancient ruins.
- Harming it could destabilize magic across Huwan.

#### Worship of Kroxona

Druids are Kroxona's primary followers. Farmers, hunters, beastfolk, fae, herbalists, midwives, monster tamers, weather-readers, and survivalists may also worship her.

Common worship themes:

- Planting rites
- Harvest prayers
- Fertility blessings
- Weather offerings
- Animal oaths
- Forest pilgrimages
- World Tree ceremonies
- Seasonal dances
- Druidic circles
- Famine appeasement rituals

Kroxona's worshippers understand that nature is not kind in a simple way. It is alive, responsive, hungry, generous, and dangerous.

#### Kroxona's Blessings and Curses

Possible blessings:

- Bountiful harvests.
- Fertility.
- Healthy births.
- Gentle weather.
- Animal friendship.
- Plant growth.
- Druidic power.
- Safe passage through wildlands.
- Monster pacification.
- Healing through herbs and natural magic.

Possible curses:

- Famine.
- Blight.
- Infertility.
- Drought.
- Flood.
- Predatory animal attacks.
- Monster swarms.
- Poisonous overgrowth.
- Crops turning against their growers.
- Weather disasters.

Kroxona's curses may not feel personal. Nature does not always hate what it destroys.

#### Relics of Kroxona

Kroxona has many relics, but the most famous is **The Elder Staff**.

The Elder Staff is said to hold ten 9th-level spells in perfect harmony, including **Wish**.

Relic design notes:

- The staff should be one of the most powerful druidic and natural relics in Huwan.
- It may only obey those who understand nature's duality.
- It may refuse users who only want abundance without accepting decay.
- Its ten spells may represent ten natural laws or ten branches of the World Tree.
- Wish should be present but dangerous, because nature always balances what is taken and given.

Possible Elder Staff spell themes:

- Wish
- World Tree growth
- Weather command
- Mass healing
- Famine or abundance
- Monster pacification
- Nature's wrath
- Fertility blessing
- Forest creation
- Life restoration

## Eras of Huwan

Huwan is currently in its **10th Era**.

The world has been destroyed and rebuilt nine times before. Each destruction came from divine conflict, mortal ambition, ideological collapse, forbidden magic, racial war, artifact misuse, or some combination of these forces.

The 10th Era is fragile because the gods are sealed away. Mortals now inherit the consequences of divine absence. Kingdoms rise, factions spread, monsters evolve, races struggle for dominance, and ancient artifacts remain capable of tipping the scales toward salvation or catastrophe.

### Era Framework

| Era | Working Name | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1st Era | Age of First Thought | To define |
| 2nd Era | Age of Divine Walking | To define |
| 3rd Era | Age of First Kingdoms | To define |
| 4th Era | Age of Blooded Stars | To define |
| 5th Era | Age of Broken Moons | To define |
| 6th Era | Age of Mortal Crowns | To define |
| 7th Era | Age of Deep Ruin | To define |
| 8th Era | Age of False Peace | To define |
| 9th Era | Age of Last Judgment | To define |
| 10th Era | Current Era | Active game era |

## Legendary Artifacts

Because the gods can no longer directly interfere, artifacts are among the most dangerous forces left in Huwan. These relics may contain divine fragments, sealed memories, ancient magic systems, dead civilizations, lost weapons, or authority over natural law.

Artifacts can shift the balance toward good, evil, destruction, peace, tyranny, liberation, or rebirth. They should be rare, story-defining, and dangerous even when used for noble reasons.

### Artifact Design Needs

- Define artifacts tied to each of the nine destroyed eras.
- Define artifacts tied to each sealed deity.
- Define artifacts that can awaken, corrupt, or evolve with the player.
- Define world consequences for using legendary artifacts.

## The Seven Continents

Huwan has seven continents. Each continent should have its own climate identity, dominant kingdom, ancient ruins, cultures, trade routes, monster threats, and religious tensions.

| Continent | Working Identity | Primary Story Use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Aurelith | Broad heartland of old crowns, fertile plains, sacred roads, and human political expansion. | Strong candidate for the Human Kingdom and the starter town Eldoria. |
| Sylvarune | Ancient forests, moonlit groves, high magic, living trees, and divided elven houses. | Strong candidate for the Elven Kingdom. |
| Kharvoden | Mountain chains, deep halls, volcanic forges, mineral empires, and buried era ruins. | Strong candidate for the Dwarven Kingdom. |
| Varkhesh | Harsh steppes, red canyons, fortress tribes, war roads, and honor-bound survival cultures. | Strong candidate for the Orc Kingdom. |
| Nocthyra | Ashlands, black glass deserts, infernal citadels, cursed rivers, and demon reformist enclaves. | Strong candidate for the Demon Kingdom. |
| Theralis | Vast savannas, jungle kingdoms, spirit beasts, migration routes, and predator-lineage clans. | Strong candidate for the Beastfolk Kingdom. |
| Faeloria | Dreamlike wilds, impossible seasons, floating gardens, old pacts, and reality-thin borderlands. | Strong candidate for the Fae Kingdom. |

These are original working names for Huwan. Before any public or commercial release, names should receive a final uniqueness pass so the project avoids accidentally sharing names with existing fantasy settings, games, or brands.

### Continent Design Needs

- Confirm or rename all seven continents.
- Decide which major race presides over each continent or dominant region.
- Define major biomes, capital cities, holy sites, ruins, and conflict zones.
- Tie each continent to one or more past eras.

## The Seven Major Kingdoms

The seven major kingdoms are presided over by the seven strongest races:

| Kingdom Seat | Presiding Race | Current Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Human Kingdom | Humans | Often central to political conflict, expansion, and anti-demon sentiment. |
| Elven Kingdom | Elves | Includes internal divisions such as high elves, wood elves, dark elves, and demon-aligned outliers. |
| Dwarven Kingdom | Dwarves | Major source of craft, mining, defensive warfare, and conflict with elves. |
| Orc Kingdom | Orcs | Often considered inherently evil by other races, but outliers can seek honor, peace, or coexistence. |
| Demon Kingdom | Demons | Widely treated as inherently evil, though peaceful demons and reformist groups exist. |
| Beastfolk Kingdom | Beastfolk | Includes varied animal-lineage cultures such as tigerfolk, lionfolk, and panthera. |
| Fae Kingdom | Fae | Includes fairies, arch fae, fauns, and other magical nature-linked peoples. |

Each region also contains mixed-race communities, migrations, conquered peoples, exiles, and hidden settlements. No culture should be treated as one-note. Even races feared by the world can produce outliers, and supposedly noble races can produce extremists, traitors, or tyrants.

## Major Races and Sub-Races

Every race is playable.

### Major Races

- Humans
- Elves
- Dwarves
- Orcs
- Demons
- Beastfolk
- Fae

### Known Sub-Races and Special Races

- Half-Elf
- Half-Orc
- Dark Elf
- Wood Elf
- Mountain Dwarf
- Fairy
- Arch Fae
- Faun
- Halfling
- Vampire
- Werewolf
- Tigerfolk
- Lionfolk
- Panthera
- Ghost
- Lich
- Slime Monster, evolvable
- Skeleton, evolvable
- High-Human
- Angelics
- Arch-Demons
- The Reincarnated, secret race

### Race Design Needs

For each race and sub-race, define:

- Birth traits
- Birth abilities
- Special skills
- Weaknesses
- Bonuses
- Cultural tendencies
- Common homelands
- Common faiths
- Common prejudices against them
- Reputation modifiers
- Class and job synergies
- Evolution paths, if any

Lore inspiration should come from public-domain, permissive, or original sources where possible. Borrowed inspiration should be transformed into Huwan-specific cultures, histories, and mechanics.

### Race Trait Template

Each playable race should use the same design format.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| General Role | The race's broad place in Huwan. |
| Common Reputation | How most people see them. |
| Closest Deity | The deity most culturally tied to them, if any. |
| Major Continent and Kingdom | Their primary homeland and political center. |
| Population Status | Common, uncommon, rare, feared, respected, etc. |
| Mana Well | Typical mana well depth and behavior. |
| Birth Traits | Natural traits from birth. |
| Bonuses | Stat, skill, social, survival, or progression advantages. |
| Weaknesses | Social, physical, magical, environmental, or progression drawbacks. |
| Special Abilities | Race-specific abilities or unlockable traits. |
| Reputation Effects | How other groups react to them. |
| Class and Job Synergies | Natural tendencies without hard-locking player choices. |
| Must-Have Traits | Required design rules. |
| Must-Not-Have Traits | Forbidden design rules. |

### Human Race Profile

**Race:** Human  
**General Role in Huwan:** Self-centered all-rounders who generally consider themselves the master race and are often used as authoritative figures.  
**Common Reputation:** Mostly neutral, but with strong general tension between Humans and Demons.  
**Closest Deity:** Pharmos, God of Night and Day  
**Major Continent:** Nanira  
**Major Kingdom:** Nuwen  
**Population Status:** Very common and broadly respected  
**Must-Have Trait:** All-Rounder  
**Must-Not-Have Trait:** Darkvision  

Humans are one of the most common and politically influential races in Huwan. They often see themselves as the natural organizers, rulers, judges, soldiers, priests, merchants, and decision-makers of civilization.

Their greatest strength is flexibility. Humans can adapt to almost any class, job, guild, region, faith, or social structure. Their greatest flaw is that this flexibility often becomes entitlement. Many Humans believe their adaptability proves superiority.

#### Human Mana Well

Typical mana well depth: **Average**

Humans do not usually have the deepest mana wells, strongest instincts, oldest bloodline magic, or most extreme racial gifts. Instead, their mana wells are stable, teachable, and adaptable.

Human mana strengths:

- Easy to train.
- Compatible with many magic types.
- Good for Wizards, Priests, Paladins, Mages, and generalists.
- Less likely to suffer extreme racial mana instability.

Human mana weaknesses:

- Usually outclassed in raw depth by Demons and some magical bloodlines.
- Usually less instinctive than Sorcerer-heavy races.
- No natural Darkvision.

#### Human Birth Traits

- All-Rounder
- Social adaptability
- Cultural flexibility
- Stable mana well
- Fast civic integration
- Broad class and job compatibility

#### Human Bonuses

- Small bonus to general stat growth.
- Small bonus to reputation gain in Human-led regions.
- Easier access to authority-based jobs such as Officer, Merchant, Priest, Paladin, Knight, and Noble service.
- Easier integration into guilds and civic institutions.
- Flexible class progression with fewer racial restrictions.

#### Human Weaknesses

- No Darkvision.
- Social tension with Demons.
- Some non-Human races may distrust Human authority.
- Human arrogance can create faction, noble, and racial conflict.
- Average mana depth means Humans often need training, tools, faith, or study to rival naturally magical races.

#### Human Special Abilities

Potential Human traits:

- All-Rounder: Humans gain flexible progression across classes and jobs.
- Civic Instinct: Humans adapt quickly to laws, guild systems, and social hierarchies.
- Authority Bearing: Humans may gain small bonuses when acting in official roles.
- Versatile Training: Humans learn basic skills from multiple disciplines slightly faster.

#### Human Reputation Effects

- Respected in many civilized areas.
- Trusted more easily in Human-led governments.
- May receive automatic suspicion from Demons, Demon sympathizers, and anti-Human factions.
- May be viewed as arrogant by Elves, Dwarves, Beastfolk, Fae, and mixed-race communities.
- May gain easier access to Nuwen authority structures.

#### Human Class and Job Synergies

Humans naturally fit almost any class or job.

Strong synergies:

- Progression Rank
- Knight
- Priest
- Paladin
- Wizard
- Merchant
- Officer
- Adventurer
- Builder
- Blacksmith
- Tavern Owner

Humans are not locked out of other paths, but they rarely receive extreme racial advantages without training.

#### Human Sub-Races

Human sub-races should preserve the Human all-rounder identity while giving players different social, magical, regional, and story flavors.

| Sub-Race | Identity | Mana Well | Core Traits | Weaknesses | Strong Synergies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Common Human | Standard Human baseline found across Nanira and mixed cities. | Average | All-Rounder, flexible learning, civic adaptability. | No extreme racial advantage, no Darkvision. | Any class or job. |
| High-Human | Refined Human bloodline tied to old authority, noble education, and Pharmos worship. | Average to Deep | Authority Bearing, better reputation in Human courts, strong leadership growth. | Seen as arrogant or elitist, distrusted by poor districts and anti-noble factions. | Noble service, Officer, Priest, Paladin, Wizard, Merchant. |
| Dawn Human | Human line culturally devoted to Pharmos's daylight side. | Average | Daylight resilience, morale aura, better public trust during daytime or lawful settings. | Weaker in deep night, shadow magic discomfort, may deny Pharmos's darker side. | Priest, Paladin, Knight, Officer, healer roles. |
| Dusk Human | Human line that honors both day and night sides of Pharmos. | Average | Better balance with light and dark magic, strong oath sense, night travel discipline. | Distrusted by daylight purists and shadow cults alike. | Wizard, Mage, Thief, Priest, Paladin, Adventurer. |
| Frontier Human | Rugged Humans from borders, farms, roads, and unsettled regions. | Average | Survival instinct, tool familiarity, weather tolerance, practical courage. | Less courtly, weaker noble reputation, rough manners. | Hunter/Gatherer, Farmer, Builder, Adventurer, Guard, Archer. |
| Urban Human | City-born Human adapted to trade, guilds, politics, rumor, and law. | Average | Social navigation, market sense, guild familiarity, fast civic reputation gain. | Weaker wilderness comfort, more dependent on urban systems. | Merchant, Officer, Thief, Tavern Owner, Priest, Craftsman. |
| Warborn Human | Human line shaped by military service, border conflict, or Ixnir influence. | Low to Average | Combat discipline, courage, weapon training, better fear resistance. | More prone to faction conflict, may struggle with peace work. | Knight, Paladin, Officer, Guard, Adventurer. |
| Half-Blood Human | Human with visible or hidden mixed ancestry from another race. | Varies | Flexible inheritance, unusual trait potential, easier bridge between cultures. | May face purity prejudice, unstable reputation depending on ancestry. | Progression Rank, Adventurer, Merchant, Mage, social/faction roles. |
| Blessed Human | Human touched by a deity, relic, miracle, birth contract, or holy event. | Average to Deep | One divine-leaning trait, better patron resonance, possible blessing unlocks. | Divine attention, rival faith suspicion, curse risk if oath is broken. | Priest, Paladin, Warlock, chosen hero paths. |
| Cursed Human | Human marked by a curse, failed ritual, divine punishment, or dark inheritance. | Unstable | Strange resistance, curse-sight, forbidden-path access, unusual story hooks. | Social fear, magical instability, possible reputation penalties. | Warlock, Thief, Sorcerer, antihero paths, Reincarnated hooks. |

##### Common Human

Common Humans are the baseline Human population. They are flexible, common, adaptable, and socially easy to place in almost any city, guild, job, or faction.

They are best for players who want the cleanest all-rounder experience.

##### High-Human

High-Humans represent refined Human bloodlines, old noble authority, and prestigious education. They are often connected to courts, temples, military command, or old family lines.

Benefits:

- Better reputation in Human-led authority systems.
- Easier leadership and noble-service progression.
- Slightly better mana training access.

Drawbacks:

- May be viewed as elitist.
- Can attract resentment from poor districts.
- May be targeted by anti-noble factions.

##### Dawn Human

Dawn Humans culturally favor Pharmos's benevolent daylight aspect. They often value law, clarity, public virtue, truth, and protection.

Benefits:

- Stronger morale effects.
- Easier public trust.
- Better synergy with daylight rites and lawful institutions.

Drawbacks:

- Can become naive about darkness, secrecy, and hidden evil.
- May reject Pharmos's necessary darker side.

##### Dusk Human

Dusk Humans honor both halves of Pharmos: day and night, light and shadow, witness and secrecy.

Benefits:

- Better compatibility with both light and dark magic.
- Strong balance themes.
- Better night travel and oath-based story hooks.

Drawbacks:

- Daylight purists may distrust them.
- Shadow-aligned groups may see them as not dark enough.

##### Frontier Human

Frontier Humans live near dangerous roads, farms, forests, monster borders, and unsettled territory. They are practical, rough, and hard to scare.

Benefits:

- Better survival, farming, building, gathering, and travel.
- Good starter path for F-rank adventure loops.

Drawbacks:

- Less polished in courts, banks, temples, and noble houses.

##### Urban Human

Urban Humans are shaped by cities, markets, guilds, politics, social contracts, and rumor.

Benefits:

- Better merchant, officer, social, and guild reputation growth.
- Stronger city navigation.

Drawbacks:

- Weaker wilderness comfort and survival instincts.

##### Warborn Human

Warborn Humans are shaped by military service, border conflict, family duty, or Ixnir's war/peace influence.

Benefits:

- Better combat discipline.
- Better fear resistance.
- Easier weapon and guard progression.

Drawbacks:

- May escalate conflict too quickly.
- Carries military or family expectations.

##### Half-Blood Human

Half-Blood Humans carry mixed ancestry. This ancestry may be public, hidden, celebrated, or shamefully concealed depending on family and region.

Benefits:

- Can inherit unusual traits.
- Better bridge between Human society and another race.
- Strong story flexibility.

Drawbacks:

- May face bloodline prejudice.
- Reputation depends heavily on the non-Human ancestry.

##### Blessed Human

Blessed Humans are marked by divine favor, relic exposure, miracle birth, or Udum-style birth contracts.

Benefits:

- One divine-leaning trait.
- Better patron resonance.
- Potential blessing unlocks.

Drawbacks:

- Divine attention.
- Religious expectations.
- Greater curse consequences if they betray the blessing.

##### Cursed Human

Cursed Humans are marked by failed rituals, divine punishment, forbidden magic, inherited sin, or strange birth conditions.

Benefits:

- Strange resistances.
- Access to unusual or forbidden story paths.
- Strong antihero hooks.

Drawbacks:

- Social fear.
- Magical instability.
- Possible curse escalation.

##### Human Sub-Race Design Notes

- No Human sub-race should gain natural Darkvision unless mixed ancestry specifically grants it.
- Human sub-races should stay flexible rather than hyper-specialized.
- Humans should remain easy to use for new players.
- The strongest Human sub-races should carry social, divine, or political baggage to balance their perks.

### Elf Race Profile

**Race:** Elves  
**General Role in Huwan:** Knowledge experts  
**Common Reputation:** "Hottie, hot, Elves, <3"  
**Closest Deity:** Etta, Goddess of Education, Knowledge, and Blissful Ignorance  
**Major Continent:** Aulend  
**Major Kingdom:** Dynasty of Xuidal  
**Population Status:** Common, respected, and loved  
**Must-Have Traits:** Long Lives, Deep Mana Wells, Forest Living  
**Must-Not-Have Trait:** Breeding with Dwarves  

Elves are widely admired in Huwan for beauty, elegance, knowledge, education, and magical refinement. Their public reputation is unusually affectionate compared to many races. Many people see Elves as attractive, cultured, intelligent, and desirable.

Elves are also knowledge experts. They preserve records, study magic, refine culture, teach arts, and often treat learning as both discipline and identity.

#### Elf Mana Well

Typical mana well depth: **Deep**

Elves usually have deeper mana wells than Humans and are naturally compatible with learned magic, refined casting, ritual work, and long-term magical study.

Elf mana strengths:

- Deep mana wells.
- Strong compatibility with Wizard, Mage, Druid, and Sorcerer paths.
- Excellent long-term spell study.
- Good magical endurance.
- Strong memory for spell patterns and lore.

Elf mana weaknesses:

- Can become overconfident in magical theory.
- May struggle with crude, instinctive, or brute-force magic traditions.
- Deep mana wells can create dangerous arrogance if not disciplined.

#### Elf Birth Traits

- Long life.
- Deep mana well.
- Forest living.
- Strong memory.
- Knowledge affinity.
- Graceful physical bearing.
- Cultural refinement.

#### Elf Bonuses

- Bonus to Intelligence growth.
- Bonus to Mana growth.
- Bonus to knowledge-based skills.
- Bonus to spell study and magical theory.
- Bonus to forest navigation and forest living.
- Easier access to scholarly, magical, artistic, and noble education.
- Reputation bonus in cultures that admire Elves.

#### Elf Weaknesses

- Cultural taboo against breeding with Dwarves.
- May face political tension with Dwarven groups.
- May be targeted by admirers, rivals, or jealous factions because of their public desirability.
- Long lives can make Elves slow to change.
- Some Elves may be arrogant about knowledge or refinement.
- Forest living preference can make dense industrial environments uncomfortable.

#### Elf Special Abilities

Potential Elf traits:

- Long-Lived: Elves age slowly and can accumulate knowledge over centuries.
- Deep Mana Well: Elves can sustain spells longer than average races.
- Forest-Born: Elves gain bonuses in forests, groves, and natural mana environments.
- Knowledge Expert: Elves learn scholarly, magical, and historical skills more easily.
- Elegant Bearing: Elves gain social advantages in cultures that admire refinement and beauty.

#### Elf Reputation Effects

- Loved and respected by many common people.
- Admired for beauty, knowledge, and culture.
- Trusted in education, magic, art, and diplomacy.
- May be resented by people who see Elves as elitist.
- May face rivalry or hostility from Dwarves.
- May receive special attention in mixed cities like Eldoria.

#### Elf Class and Job Synergies

Strong synergies:

- Wizard
- Mage
- Sorcerer
- Druid
- Priest of Etta
- Scholar-like jobs
- Merchant
- Archer
- Craftsman
- Adventurer

Elves can pursue other paths, but their natural advantages lean toward knowledge, magic, forest living, and refined skill.

#### Elf Sub-Races

Elf sub-races should preserve the core Elf identity of beauty, knowledge, long life, deep mana wells, and forest-linked culture while creating different expressions of elven society.

**Dark Elf is intentionally reserved for creator definition and should not be auto-filled.**

| Sub-Race | Identity | Mana Well | Core Traits | Weaknesses | Strong Synergies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| High Elf | Courtly, scholarly, magically refined Elves tied to authority and old education. | Deep | High education, refined spell study, noble etiquette, strong memory. | Arrogance, political pressure, slower adaptation to common life. | Wizard, Mage, Priest of Etta, Noble service, Merchant. |
| Wood Elf | Forest-dwelling Elves tied to trees, hunting, archery, herbs, and natural mana. | Deep | Forest living, stealth, archery, herbalism, animal awareness. | Discomfort in dense cities, distrust of heavy industry. | Druid, Archer, Hunter/Gatherer, Scout, Tamer. |
| Moon Elf | Night-oriented Elves tied to moonlight, dreams, quiet study, and subtle magic. | Deep | Dream sensitivity, night navigation, calm focus, illusion affinity. | Sunlit exhaustion during extreme heat, emotional distance. | Mage, Wizard, Sorcerer, Dream magic, Illusion paths. |
| Sun Elf | Daylight-oriented Elves tied to radiance, pride, public beauty, and visible authority. | Deep | Light affinity, social presence, performance, public trust. | Pride, rivalry with shadow-aligned Elves, poor stealth discipline. | Priest, Paladin, Bard-style roles, Merchant, Noble service. |
| Star Elf | Rare contemplative Elves tied to astronomy, fate-reading, and long-view knowledge. | Very Deep | Star-reading, divination, long memory, cosmic patience. | Detached from daily life, slow decisions, fragile social urgency. | Divination Wizard, Scholar, Mage, Priest of Etta, Seer. |
| River Elf | Elves adapted to rivers, trade routes, fishing settlements, and water magic. | Deep | Water comfort, swimming, trade navigation, river herb knowledge. | Weaker in deserts or dry mountains, tied to water access. | Water Mage, Merchant, Fisher, Healer, Adventurer. |
| Root Elf | Ancient grove-bound Elves tied to old trees, living roots, and ancestral memory. | Deep but slow | Plant empathy, memory rites, defensive nature magic, patient casting. | Slow social change, vulnerable when sacred groves are harmed. | Druid, Nature Mage, Herbalist, Guardian, Elder roles. |
| Silver Elf | Diplomatic Elves known for grace, etiquette, negotiation, and cultural polish. | Average to Deep | Diplomacy, charm, language learning, social reading. | May be seen as manipulative or too polished. | Merchant, Diplomat, Priest, Wizard, Bard-style roles. |
| Ironbark Elf | Hardy forest-border Elves shaped by rough terrain, monster pressure, and survival. | Average to Deep | Tougher bodies, bow discipline, wilderness defense, patrol habits. | Less refined by elven standards, lower court status. | Archer, Adventurer, Guard, Druid, Dungeon Explorer. |
