| Half-Elf | Mixed Human-Elf lineage that bridges cultures and inherits partial elven traits. | Average to Deep | Social flexibility, mixed heritage, partial long life, adaptive learning. | Bloodline prejudice, identity tension, weaker pure-lineage privileges. | Progression Rank, Merchant, Adventurer, Wizard, Social roles. |
| Dark Elf | Mysterious introverted outliers tied to stealth, knowledge, and quiet power. | Deep | Dark skin, often purple skin, white hair, elf ears, stealth mastery, introversion. | Often suspected of evil, poor fit for outgoing social paths. | Thief, Assassin, Wizard, Mage, Scholar, Spy. |

##### High Elf

High Elves are the polished scholars, court magicians, noble tutors, and refined public face of elven civilization. They are often connected to the Dynasty of Xuidal's high institutions, archives, academies, and noble courts.

Benefits:

- Strong spell study.
- Better noble and academic reputation.
- Better access to formal education.
- Strong synergy with Etta's worship.

Drawbacks:

- May be arrogant or socially distant.
- May underestimate practical workers and common adventurers.
- May be targeted by political rivals.

##### Wood Elf

Wood Elves are forest-living Elves who treat trees, hunting grounds, herbs, and natural paths as part of home. They are some of the most classically forest-linked Elves.

Benefits:

- Strong forest navigation.
- Better archery and stealth in woods.
- Herbal knowledge.
- Easier connection to animals and natural mana.

Drawbacks:

- Dense cities can feel suffocating.
- Heavy industry and mining may create cultural disgust.
- May distrust Dwarven expansion.

##### Moon Elf

Moon Elves are quiet, reflective, and dream-sensitive. They are associated with night study, moonlit groves, subtle magic, and the edge between waking and dreaming.

Benefits:

- Strong dream and illusion affinity.
- Better night travel.
- Calm concentration.
- Easier contact with Ione-flavored dream signs.

Drawbacks:

- Can seem distant, melancholic, or eerie.
- May struggle with direct public leadership.

##### Sun Elf

Sun Elves are radiant, proud, and socially visible. They are often admired by the public and may lean into beauty, performance, public authority, or bright magic.

Benefits:

- Strong light affinity.
- Strong public presence.
- Easier reputation gain in beauty- and status-focused cultures.

Drawbacks:

- Pride can become a problem.
- Poor fit for stealthy or subtle paths unless trained.

##### Star Elf

Star Elves are rare Elves who study the sky, fate, cosmic signs, and long historical cycles. They often think in centuries rather than days.

Benefits:

- Divination affinity.
- Strong long-term memory.
- Excellent patience for deep study.

Drawbacks:

- Can be detached from immediate problems.
- Slow to act unless the pattern feels important.

##### River Elf

River Elves live along rivers, lakes, and trade waterways. They are practical, graceful, and often more commercially connected than deep-forest Elves.

Benefits:

- Swimming and water travel.
- River trade knowledge.
- Water and healing magic compatibility.

Drawbacks:

- Dry or sealed environments weaken comfort and instincts.

##### Root Elf

Root Elves are ancient grove-linked Elves who preserve ancestral memories through trees, roots, and living places. They are patient, defensive, and spiritually heavy.

Benefits:

- Plant empathy.
- Nature defense.
- Strong memory rites.
- Grove-based magic.

Drawbacks:

- Slow to change.
- Sacred groves becoming damaged can deeply harm morale or magic.

##### Silver Elf

Silver Elves are diplomats, negotiators, teachers, and social polishers. They often smooth conflicts through grace and language.

Benefits:

- Diplomacy.
- Language learning.
- Social reading.
- Etiquette.

Drawbacks:

- Others may suspect hidden manipulation.

##### Ironbark Elf

Ironbark Elves are hardy borderland Elves who defend forests against monsters, bandits, Dwarven expansion, and dungeon spillover.

Benefits:

- Tougher than many Elves.
- Strong archery and patrol skills.
- Good monster-border survival.

Drawbacks:

- Less socially prestigious in refined elven courts.

##### Half-Elf

Half-Elves are mixed Human-Elf people who inherit Human flexibility and partial Elven grace, mana, beauty, or longevity.

Benefits:

- Strong cultural bridge.
- Flexible learning.
- Partial deep mana potential.
- Easier mixed-city integration.

Drawbacks:

- May face prejudice from bloodline purists.
- May be treated as not fully Human or not fully Elf.

##### Dark Elf

Dark Elves are mysterious Elf outliers. They are not automatically evil, but many people look at them as possibly evil because of their quiet nature, dark appearance, stealth culture, and secretive reputation.

**General Role in Huwan:** Outliers, looked at as possibly evil, but also seen as mysterious and introverted  
**Common Reputation:** Masters of stealth and mystery  
**Closest Deity:** Etta, Goddess of Education, Knowledge, and Blissful Ignorance  
**Major Continent and Kingdom:** Same as Elves; Aulend and the Dynasty of Xuidal  
**Population Status:** Mysterious rather than directly feared  
**Must-Have Traits:** Dark skin, same Elf ears, introverted, usually purple skin, white hair  
**Must-Not-Have Traits:** Alabaster skin, outgoing  

Dark Elves are knowledge-tied like other Elves, but they often pursue hidden knowledge, forbidden records, quiet observation, stealth techniques, secret histories, and truths other Elves avoid discussing.

Benefits:

- Strong stealth affinity.
- Strong mystery and secrecy reputation.
- Deep mana wells.
- Good night movement.
- Strong compatibility with hidden knowledge, archives, and magical study.

Drawbacks:

- Often suspected of evil.
- Socially misunderstood.
- Poor fit for loud public leadership unless deliberately trained.
- Outgoing personalities are culturally uncommon.
- Alabaster skin does not fit their core visual identity.

Potential traits:

- Shadowed Scholar: Dark Elves learn hidden knowledge and stealth-related lore more easily.
- Quiet Presence: Dark Elves are harder to notice when they avoid attention.
- Mystery Bearing: Dark Elves gain social weight when secrecy, fear, or intrigue matters.
- Deep Mana Well: Dark Elves retain strong Elven magical endurance.

Strong synergies:

- Thief
- Assassin
- Spy
- Wizard
- Mage
- Scholar
- Warlock
- Sorcerer
- Dungeon Explorer

##### Elf Sub-Race Design Notes

- Elves should remain long-lived and mana-rich across most sub-races.
- Elven sub-races should lean toward knowledge, magic, beauty, forests, diplomacy, or refined survival.
- The Dwarf breeding taboo remains culturally important unless the creator defines a specific exception.
- Dark Elves are defined by mystery, stealth, introversion, dark or purple skin, white hair, and Etta-linked knowledge.

### Dwarf Race Profile

**Race:** Dwarves  
**General Role in Huwan:** Masters at hard labor, craftwork, and building  
**Common Reputation:** "If you want it built right, ask a Dwarf." "If you want the best gear, ask a Dwarf."  
**Closest Deity:** Ione, Goddess of Dreams and Nightmares  
**Major Continent:** Egand  
**Major Kingdom:** Wortor Empire  
**Population Status:** Common and definitely respected  
**Must-Have Traits:** All Dwarves have beards, including newborns; all Dwarves have jobs  
**Must-Not-Have Traits:** Beardless, lazy  

Dwarves are the builders, crafters, laborers, smiths, masons, miners, engineers, and gear masters of Huwan. If something must be built correctly, reinforced properly, repaired honestly, or forged to last, people seek Dwarven hands.

Their culture treats work as identity. A Dwarf without a job is considered incomplete, suspicious, cursed, or in crisis. Even newborn Dwarves are born with beards, making beardlessness almost unthinkable in Dwarven society.

#### Dwarf Mana Well

Typical mana well depth: **Average to Deep**

Dwarves are not always flashy casters, but their mana is steady, durable, and excellent for crafting, rune work, enchantment, forge magic, earth magic, and long-term construction projects.

Dwarf mana strengths:

- Stable mana flow.
- Strong compatibility with rune magic.
- Strong compatibility with earth, fire, and crafting magic.
- Excellent enchantment foundation work.
- Strong resistance to magical fatigue during labor or crafting.

Dwarf mana weaknesses:

- Less naturally flexible than Elven magic.
- Less explosive than Sorcerer or Demon mana.
- May struggle with delicate illusion, dream, or social enchantment magic unless trained.

#### Dwarf Birth Traits

- Born with a beard.
- Born into job-oriented culture.
- Strong endurance.
- Labor instinct.
- Craft sense.
- Building sense.
- Stone and metal familiarity.
- Work pride.

#### Dwarf Bonuses

- Bonus to Strength growth.
- Bonus to Defense growth.
- Bonus to crafting skills.
- Bonus to mining, smithing, masonry, building, and repair.
- Bonus when using anvils, furnaces, quarries, stonework, or forge tools.
- Easier access to Blacksmith, Builder, Craftsman, Miner, Armorer, and Engineer-style jobs.
- Higher reputation with people seeking quality gear.

#### Dwarf Weaknesses

- Laziness is culturally despised.
- Beardlessness is socially unacceptable or treated as deeply strange.
- May face strong cultural tension with Elves.
- Can be stubborn about craft standards.
- May overvalue work and undervalue rest.
- May struggle socially in cultures that do not respect labor.

#### Dwarf Special Abilities

Potential Dwarf traits:

- Born Bearded: All Dwarves visibly carry Dwarven identity from birth.
- Work-Bound: Dwarves gain social and progression benefits from having a job.
- Craft Instinct: Dwarves more easily identify item quality, flaws, and repair needs.
- Stone Sense: Dwarves can better read stonework, tunnels, mines, and structural weakness.
- Forge Memory: Dwarves remember crafting steps and material behavior especially well.

#### Dwarf Reputation Effects

- Highly respected for building and gear.
- Trusted as reliable workers and crafters.
- Valued by merchants, adventurers, knights, and nobles who need quality equipment.
- May be stereotyped as stubborn, blunt, or work-obsessed.
- Tension exists with Elves, especially around cultural pride and old disputes.
- A lazy Dwarf may face serious social penalties from other Dwarves.

#### Dwarf Class and Job Synergies

Strong synergies:

- Blacksmith
- Builder
- Craftsman
- Armorer
- Merchant
- Knight
- Fighter-style classes
- Rune-focused Mage
- Earth Magic user
- Dungeon Explorer
- Adventurer

Dwarves can pursue any path, but their strongest natural advantages support labor, craft, gear, building, stone, metal, runes, and endurance.

#### Dwarf Sub-Races

Dwarf sub-races should preserve the core Dwarf identity of work, beards, craft, endurance, jobs, and pride in labor while giving different regional and professional expressions.

| Sub-Race | Identity | Mana Well | Core Traits | Weaknesses | Strong Synergies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Mountain Dwarf | Classic high-mountain miners, smiths, and fortress builders. | Average to Deep | Stone sense, mining, endurance, forge discipline. | Stubborn, poor water comfort, dislikes soft living. | Blacksmith, Builder, Armorer, Miner, Knight. |
| Forge Dwarf | Fire, metal, furnace, and smithing-focused Dwarves. | Deep for crafting | Heat tolerance, metal memory, smithing instinct. | Overwork, fire obsession, lower forest comfort. | Blacksmith, Armorer, Artificer, Rune Mage. |
| Quarry Dwarf | Stonecutters, masons, road builders, and civic construction masters. | Average | Stone shaping, structural reading, building speed. | Less flexible in non-building careers. | Builder, Mason, Craftsman, Merchant. |
| Deep Dwarf | Underground Dwarves adapted to caverns, mines, and dark tunnels. | Average to Deep | Darkvision, cave navigation, pressure endurance. | Sun discomfort, social isolation. | Dungeon Explorer, Miner, Guard, Rune Mage. |
| Goldbeard Dwarf | Wealth and trade-oriented Dwarves tied to banking, high-value gear, and contracts. | Average | Appraisal, negotiation, gear pricing, trade instinct. | Greed reputation, Shaohr temptation. | Merchant, Jeweler, Blacksmith, Banker-style roles. |
| Iron Oath Dwarf | Duty-bound Dwarves who treat work contracts as sacred oaths. | Average | Contract loyalty, defense, disciplined labor. | Rigid, hard to forgive broken promises. | Knight, Officer, Builder, Paladin-like duty paths. |
| Dreamforge Dwarf | Ione-touched crafters who dream designs before building them. | Deep but unusual | Dream crafting, unusual blueprints, inspired gear. | Nightmares can corrupt designs. | Craftsman, Enchanter, Artificer, Wizard. |
| Hearth Dwarf | Domestic, tavern, food, comfort, and community-focused Dwarves. | Average | Cooking, brewing, homecraft, morale building. | Less combat prestige in warrior circles. | Tavern Owner, Farmer, Merchant, Healer-adjacent jobs. |

##### Dwarf Sub-Race Notes

- All Dwarf sub-races have beards, including newborns.
- All Dwarf sub-races are expected to have jobs.
- Lazy Dwarves are socially rejected across most Dwarf cultures.
- Dwarves should lean toward practical work, craft, building, mining, gear, contracts, and endurance.

### Orc Race Profile

**Race:** Orc  
**General Role in Huwan:** Scary, tough, hard to defeat; commonly used as police, bounty hunters, guards, and escorts  
**Common Reputation:** "Did you see that Orc walk by? Shudders. I wouldn't wanna get on his bad side."  
**Closest Deity:** Ixnir, God of War and Peace  
**Major Continent:** Ifai  
**Major Kingdom:** Kingdom of Tozraca  
**Population Status:** Common, feared, and respected  
**Must-Have Traits:** Terrifying Presence, green skin, tusks, muscular build  
**Must-Not-Have Traits:** Weak, high intelligence  

Orcs are one of Huwan's most physically intimidating common races. Their presence alone can stop arguments, scatter petty criminals, and make violent people reconsider their plans.

Because Orcs are scary, tough, and hard to defeat, many societies use them in visible enforcement roles. They are common as police, bounty hunters, guards, escorts, caravan protectors, and frontline deterrents.

Orcs are not automatically evil, but their reputation is heavy. People often fear them before knowing them.

#### Orc Mana Well

Typical mana well depth: **Low to Average**

Orcs are not usually known for refined magic or high magical intelligence. Their strength is physical presence, endurance, combat instinct, and intimidation.

Orc mana strengths:

- Works well with rage, war magic, body enhancement, and simple direct effects.
- Can support Paladin, Warlock, Barbarian, or battle-priest paths when trained.
- Strong emotional resonance with Ixnir's war/peace duality.

Orc mana weaknesses:

- Usually not suited to intricate spell frameworks.
- Rarely high Intelligence by racial tendency.
- May struggle with Wizard-style mathematics and fine spell geometry.
- More likely to use magic physically or instinctively than academically.

#### Orc Birth Traits

- Green skin.
- Tusks.
- Muscular build.
- Terrifying Presence.
- High physical resilience.
- Strong combat instinct.
- Strong intimidation.
- Hard to knock down.

#### Orc Bonuses

- Bonus to Strength growth.
- Bonus to Health growth.
- Bonus to Defense growth.
- Bonus to intimidation.
- Bonus to guarding, escorting, bounty hunting, and frontline combat.
- Strong resistance to fear.
- Easier access to police, guard, bounty hunter, escort, knight, and martial jobs.

#### Orc Weaknesses

- Lower tendency toward high Intelligence.
- Social fear from other races.
- Can be stereotyped as violent even when peaceful.
- May be suspected first during violent incidents.
- May struggle in delicate diplomacy unless trained.
- Some magic paths require extra effort due to lower academic affinity.

#### Orc Special Abilities

Potential Orc traits:

- Terrifying Presence: Orcs can intimidate weaker enemies or civilians through sheer presence.
- Hard to Defeat: Orcs resist being staggered, knocked down, or frightened.
- Guard-Born: Orcs gain advantages in protection, escort, and enforcement roles.
- War Instinct: Orcs read violent intent and battlefield danger more easily.
- Tusks and Muscle: Orcs gain natural close-combat intimidation and physical identity.

#### Orc Reputation Effects

- Feared by many civilians.
- Respected in enforcement and combat roles.
- Trusted as guards when visible force is desired.
- May face prejudice from people who assume Orcs are naturally violent or evil.
- May gain respect from Beastfolk, warriors, guards, bounty hunters, and followers of Ixnir.
- May be watched carefully in wealthy districts.

#### Orc Class and Job Synergies

Strong synergies:

- Knight
- Paladin
- Barbarian-style classes
- Adventurer
- Officer
- Bounty Hunter-style roles
- Guard
- Escort
- Dungeon Explorer
- Blacksmith
- Warlock of Ixnir or martial patrons

Orcs can pursue peaceful lives, but the world often pushes them toward roles where strength and fear are useful.

#### Orc Sub-Races

Orc sub-races should preserve the core Orc identity of strength, fear, toughness, visible power, and enforcement roles while offering different martial and social expressions.

| Sub-Race | Identity | Mana Well | Core Traits | Weaknesses | Strong Synergies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Common Orc | Baseline Orc found across Tozraca and mixed cities. | Low to Average | Strength, tusks, green skin, intimidation. | Low academic affinity, feared socially. | Guard, Escort, Adventurer, Knight. |
| Half-Orc | Mixed Orc lineage, often bridging Orc strength and another culture's flexibility. | Average | Strength, social bridge, mixed identity, resilience. | Prejudice from both sides. | Adventurer, Progression Rank, Guard, Merchant. |
| War Orc | Ixnir-aligned Orcs raised around battle, law enforcement, and visible force. | Average | War instinct, courage, patrol discipline. | Quick to escalate conflict. | Officer, Knight, Paladin, Bounty Hunter. |
| Stonehide Orc | Extremely durable Orcs known for thick skin and high pain tolerance. | Low | Defense, endurance, body toughness. | Slow, less magically flexible. | Tank, Guard, Escort, Dungeon Explorer. |
| Redtusk Orc | Aggressive tusk-proud Orc line associated with intimidation and dueling. | Low to Average | Terrifying Presence, melee pressure, fear tactics. | Scary reputation worsens social trust. | Barbarian-style paths, Bounty Hunter, Adventurer. |
| Gray Orc | Older, calmer Orc line known for peacekeeping, restraint, and hard-won wisdom. | Average | Discipline, negotiation through strength, controlled presence. | Seen as less fearsome by violent Orc circles. | Officer, Guard Captain, Peacekeeper, Monk-like paths. |
| Ironbound Orc | Orcs shaped by armor, discipline, and duty contracts. | Average | Heavy armor comfort, escort discipline, oathkeeping. | Rigid duty, poor stealth. | Knight, Paladin, Guard, Caravan Escort. |
| Wild Orc | Borderland Orcs tied to plains, canyons, and survival combat. | Low to Average | Tracking, endurance, harsh terrain movement. | Poor court manners, less city trust. | Hunter/Gatherer, Adventurer, Scout, Raider-style roles. |

##### Orc Sub-Race Notes

- Orcs should not lean toward high Intelligence as a default racial fantasy.
- Orc sub-races should not be weak.
- Orcs can be peaceful or honorable, but the world often reads them through fear first.
- Half-Orcs are important because they can challenge anti-Orc prejudice while still carrying visible Orc identity.

### Demon Race Profile

**Race:** Demons  
**General Role in Huwan:** Misunderstood and misrepresented as evil; used in bedtime stories to scare children into behaving  
**Common Reputation:** Hated or discriminated against; often seen as automatically criminal without proof  
**Closest Deities:** Udum, God of Birth and Death, or Ixnir, God of War and Peace  
**Major Continent:** Eabus  
**Major Kingdom:** Shoura Dynasty  
**Population Status:** Uncommon, feared, and disrespected  
**Must-Have Traits:** Intricate horns, colored skin, sharp teeth, claws  
**Must-Not-Have Traits:** Innocent-looking or cute, except Succubus and Incubus variants  

Demons are one of the most feared and misunderstood races in Huwan. Many cultures treat them as living warnings, monsters wearing civilization, or proof of what happens when people misbehave.

Parents tell bedtime stories about Demons to keep children in line. Guards suspect them first. Nobles use them as political threats. Priests may preach against them. Poor Demon citizens often live under suspicion even when they have done nothing wrong.

Demons are not automatically evil, but the world often behaves as if they are.

#### Demon Mana Well

Typical mana well depth: **Very Deep**

Demons possess the deepest known racial mana wells. This makes them naturally powerful and terrifying to societies that already distrust them.

Demon mana strengths:

- Very deep mana wells.
- Excellent spell sustain.
- Strong compatibility with Sorcerer, Warlock, Mage, and forbidden magic paths.
- High potential for multiple simultaneous spells.
- Strong resistance to mana depletion.
- Natural affinity for dark, fire, death, pact, and infernal magic.

Demon mana weaknesses:

- Deep mana makes them feared and heavily watched.
- Powerful emotion can destabilize magic if poorly trained.
- Priests and Paladins may treat Demon magic as suspicious by default.
- Mana signatures may be easier to identify or profile.

#### Demon Birth Traits

- Intricate horns.
- Colored skin, usually purple, blue, pink, red, or black.
- Sharp teeth.
- Claws.
- Very deep mana well.
- Intimidating presence.
- Infernal resonance.
- Strong survival instinct.

#### Demon Bonuses

- Major bonus to Mana growth.
- Bonus to spell sustain.
- Bonus to dark, fire, death, pact, and forbidden-adjacent magic.
- Bonus to intimidation.
- Bonus to Warlock and Sorcerer paths.
- Potential resistance to fear and certain infernal effects.
- Strong compatibility with deep magical training if given access.

#### Demon Weaknesses

- Severe discrimination.
- Often assumed guilty without proof.
- Feared by Humans and many religious communities.
- May be denied housing, guild trust, or fair legal treatment.
- Priests and Paladins may react with suspicion or hostility.
- Innocent-looking traits are culturally uncommon except Succubus and Incubus variants.
- Deep mana wells make uncontrolled casting especially dangerous.

#### Demon Special Abilities

Potential Demon traits:

- Deepest Mana Well: Demons sustain magic longer than most races.
- Infernal Presence: Demons can intimidate or unsettle others through aura and appearance.
- Horned Identity: Horn shape can indicate lineage, status, or magical tendency.
- Claws and Teeth: Demons have natural close-range danger even unarmed.
- Pact Affinity: Demons are naturally compatible with Warlock contracts and pact magic.
- Survival Through Hatred: Demons may gain resilience under social pressure or persecution.

#### Demon Reputation Effects

- Feared in most Human-led regions.
- Disrespected and discriminated against in many cities.
- Often suspected of crime without evidence.
- Used as cautionary examples in children's stories.
- May receive sympathy from Peace Between Us, reformist factions, and mixed-race communities.
- May be targeted by The Light Among Us for forced purification.
- May be recruited by dark factions assuming they are already aligned with evil.

#### Demon Class and Job Synergies

Strong synergies:

- Sorcerer
- Warlock
- Mage
- Wizard, if given education
- Thief
- Adventurer
- Dungeon Explorer
- Fencer
- Blacksmith with infernal/firecraft
- Antihero or villain paths
- Priest or Paladin paths only when the story deliberately challenges public prejudice

Demons can pursue peaceful lives, but Huwan often makes peace difficult for them by treating their existence as a threat before they act.

#### Demon Sub-Races

Demon sub-races should preserve the core Demon identity of visible otherness, deep mana, prejudice, horns, claws, sharp teeth, and social suspicion while creating different infernal, reformist, seductive, martial, and magical expressions.

| Sub-Race | Identity | Mana Well | Core Traits | Weaknesses | Strong Synergies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Common Demon | Baseline Demon citizens of Eabus and mixed cities. | Very Deep | Horns, claws, colored skin, sharp teeth, deep mana. | Severe discrimination, automatic suspicion. | Sorcerer, Warlock, Mage, Adventurer. |
| Arch-Demon | High-ranking Demon bloodline with overwhelming mana and terrifying presence. | Extremely Deep | Command aura, infernal authority, high spell sustain. | Hated, hunted, feared by religious groups. | Warlock patron, Sorcerer, Forbidden Magic, Ruler paths. |
| Lesser Demon | Smaller or lower-status Demon line often pushed into labor or criminal stereotypes. | Deep | Survival, stealthy fear, resilient body. | Disrespected even by some Demons. | Thief, Adventurer, Labor, Scout. |
| War Demon | Demon line shaped by conflict, intimidation, and Ixnir-like violence. | Very Deep | Combat magic, claws, fear aura, battlefield pressure. | Easily profiled as violent. | Paladin-antagonist paths, Warlock, Fighter, Bounty Hunter. |
| Flame Demon | Fire-aspected Demon line with heat, ember, and forge affinity. | Very Deep | Fire resistance, flame magic, furnace comfort. | Water/cold discomfort, arson suspicion. | Fire Mage, Blacksmith, Warlock, Sorcerer. |
| Shadow Demon | Dark-aspected Demon line tied to stealth, night, and hidden movement. | Very Deep | Shadow movement, dark magic affinity, quiet threat. | Distrusted as spies or assassins. | Thief, Assassin, Warlock, Spy, Dark Mage. |
| Succubus | Charisma-focused Demon variant that may appear cute, beautiful, or seductive. | Very Deep | Charm, social manipulation, beauty, emotional reading. | Objectified, mistrusted, accused of manipulation. | Enchantment, Warlock, Merchant, Social roles. |
| Incubus | Charisma-focused Demon variant similar to Succubus but masculine or alternate-presenting. | Very Deep | Charm, social manipulation, beauty, emotional reading. | Objectified, mistrusted, accused of manipulation. | Enchantment, Warlock, Merchant, Social roles. |
| Peacebound Demon | Reformist Demon line seeking coexistence and public trust. | Deep to Very Deep | Restraint, diplomacy, controlled mana, anti-prejudice story hooks. | Distrusted by Demons and non-Demons alike. | Priest, Paladin, Merchant, Peace Between Us, Adventurer. |
| Horn-Crowned Demon | Noble Demon line where horn shape signals status and lineage. | Very Deep | Intricate horns, noble bearing, mana authority. | Targeted politically, assumed arrogant or evil. | Noble service, Warlock, Sorcerer, Ruler, Mage. |

##### Demon Sub-Race Notes

- Most Demon sub-races should not look innocent or cute.
- Succubus and Incubus variants are the major exception to the "not cute" rule.
- Demons should usually have colored skin such as purple, blue, pink, red, or black.
- Horns should be important to Demon visual identity and lineage.
- Demons have strong magic potential, but the social cost of being Demon should remain heavy.
- Peacebound Demons are important for challenging the world's prejudice.

### Beastfolk Race Profile

**Race:** Beastfolk  
**General Role in Huwan:** Often used as servants, slaves, or sideshow attractions, especially Werewolf variants  
**Common Reputation:** Seen as a race to own; "Mommy, can you buy me a Rabbitoid?"  
**Closest Deity:** Kroxona, Goddess of Nature  
**Major Continent:** Emea  
**Major Kingdom:** Khalia Kingdom, rebuilt ruins  
**Population Status:** Uncommon  
**Must-Have Traits:** Animalistic features, upright walking, animal senses, Darkvision  
**Must-Not-Have Traits:** Human-looking skin, eyes, or faces  

Beastfolk are one of Huwan's most visibly non-Human races. They walk upright and live as people, but their bodies, senses, instincts, and appearances are strongly animalistic.

Many societies fail to treat Beastfolk as full people. They are often objectified, bought, displayed, owned, exoticized, or forced into service. Some are used as servants or slaves. Some, especially Werewolf variants, are treated as sideshow attractions or dangerous spectacles.

This makes Beastfolk central to Huwan's relationship-driven moral conflicts. How a player treats Beastfolk says a great deal about who they are.

#### Beastfolk Mana Well

Typical mana well depth: **Average**

Beastfolk mana is often tied to instinct, senses, animal lineage, survival, and nature. They are not usually the deepest mana-well race, but many have strong practical compatibility with nature magic, druidic traditions, beast taming, and physical enhancement.

Beastfolk mana strengths:

- Strong compatibility with nature magic.
- Strong compatibility with Druid paths.
- Good instinctive casting for survival magic.
- Strong connection to animal companions and monster ecology.
- Good compatibility with physical enhancement and sensory magic.

Beastfolk mana weaknesses:

- May struggle with highly academic Wizard frameworks unless trained.
- May be underestimated or denied formal magic education.
- Emotional stress from exploitation can destabilize magic or instincts.

#### Beastfolk Birth Traits

- Animalistic features.
- Upright walking.
- Animal senses.
- Darkvision.
- Lineage-specific instincts.
- Enhanced hearing, smell, sight, balance, or movement depending on animal type.
- Non-Human skin, eyes, and facial structure.

#### Beastfolk Bonuses

- Bonus to Dexterity or Strength depending on lineage.
- Bonus to Survival.
- Bonus to tracking.
- Bonus to perception through animal senses.
- Bonus to night navigation through Darkvision.
- Bonus to Druid, Hunter/Gatherer, Archer, Scout, Tamer, and Beastmaster-style paths.
- Bonus to wilderness movement.

#### Beastfolk Weaknesses

- Often treated as ownable.
- May face enslavement, trafficking, forced service, or display.
- May be exoticized rather than respected.
- Human-looking features are not appropriate for true Beastfolk identity.
- Some towns may restrict Beastfolk rights.
- Certain lineages may be feared, especially Werewolves and predator types.

#### Beastfolk Special Abilities

Potential Beastfolk traits:

- Darkvision: Beastfolk can see in darkness better than Humans.
- Animal Senses: Beastfolk gain enhanced perception based on lineage.
- Lineage Instinct: Beastfolk receive behavior and survival bonuses tied to animal ancestry.
- Natural Movement: Beastfolk may gain climbing, sprinting, swimming, stealth, or balance bonuses depending on type.
- Wild Empathy: Beastfolk can better read animals and certain monsters.

#### Beastfolk Reputation Effects

- Often objectified by wealthy citizens, nobles, collectors, and cruel families.
- May be treated as servants before being treated as equals.
- May receive sympathy from Druids, Peace Between Us, abolitionist groups, poor districts, and some Fae.
- May be targeted by slavers, criminal markets, sideshow owners, and corrupt nobles.
- Predator-lineage Beastfolk may be feared more than prey-lineage Beastfolk.
- Beastfolk who gain status can become powerful symbols of freedom.

#### Beastfolk Class and Job Synergies

Strong synergies:

- Druid
- Archer
- Hunter/Gatherer
- Tamer
- Adventurer
- Scout-style roles
- Dungeon Explorer
- Guard or Escort
- Beastmaster-style paths
- Survival-focused jobs

Beastfolk can pursue any path, but their strongest natural advantages come from senses, survival, movement, nature, and animal lineage.

#### Beastfolk Sub-Races

Beastfolk sub-races are defined by animal lineage. Every Beastfolk should feel visibly non-Human, with animalistic skin, eyes, face, senses, movement, instincts, and body language.

| Sub-Race | Identity | Mana Well | Core Traits | Weaknesses | Strong Synergies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Rabbitoid | Rabbit-lineage Beastfolk often objectified as cute, quick, and ownable. | Average | Speed, hearing, jumping, prey instinct. | Exploited, fragile, fear response. | Scout, Gatherer, Merchant, Social roles. |
| Wolfkin | Wolf-lineage Beastfolk tied to packs, tracking, and loyalty. | Average | Smell, pack tactics, endurance, night hunting. | Pack grief, predator fear, silver folklore risk if werewolf-adjacent. | Guard, Hunter, Druid, Adventurer. |
| Werewolf | Cursed or lineage-shift Beastfolk often treated as sideshow danger. | Deep but unstable | Transformation, predator strength, fear aura. | Social fear, loss of control, exploitation. | Barbarian-style paths, Druid, Hunter, Antihero. |
| Tigerfolk | Tiger-lineage Beastfolk known for power, stealth, and solitary confidence. | Average to Deep | Stealth, burst strength, claws, predator senses. | Territorial, feared by prey-lineages. | Assassin, Hunter, Adventurer, Guard. |
| Lionfolk | Lion-lineage Beastfolk tied to pride, leadership, and visible strength. | Average | Leadership, roar, group tactics, courage. | Pride, social dominance conflicts. | Officer, Knight, Guard, Paladin. |
| Panthera | Panther-lineage Beastfolk tied to stealth, night movement, and elegant danger. | Average to Deep | Darkvision, stealth, silent movement, claws. | Distrusted as assassins or thieves. | Assassin, Thief, Spy, Dungeon Explorer. |
| Foxfolk | Fox-lineage Beastfolk tied to cunning, speech, tricks, and social survival. | Average | Cleverness, charm, stealth, bargain sense. | Distrust, trickster reputation. | Thief, Merchant, Bard-style roles, Warlock. |
| Bearfolk | Bear-lineage Beastfolk tied to size, protection, and endurance. | Low to Average | Strength, toughness, intimidation, cold tolerance. | Slow, large body issues in cities. | Tank, Guard, Escort, Hunter. |
| Birdfolk | Bird-lineage Beastfolk tied to sight, height, and travel. | Average | Keen sight, balance, gliding or wing traits depending lineage. | Fragile bones, enclosed-space discomfort. | Scout, Archer, Messenger, Adventurer. |
| Serpentfolk | Snake-lineage Beastfolk tied to heat sense, patience, and venom traditions. | Average | Heat sense, flexibility, venom lore, stillness. | Cold weakness, distrust. | Assassin, Alchemist, Druid, Spy. |
| Sharkfolk | Shark-lineage Beastfolk tied to water, blood sense, and aquatic danger. | Average | Swimming, blood scent, bite strength. | Dry-land discomfort, fear reputation. | Fisher, Guard, Adventurer, Water Druid. |
| Horsefolk | Horse-lineage Beastfolk tied to endurance, hauling, speed, and travel. | Low to Average | Running, stamina, caravan work, road sense. | Exploited for labor, awkward interiors. | Escort, Merchant, Farmer, Courier. |

##### Beastfolk Sub-Race Notes

- Beastfolk should not have Human-looking skin, eyes, or faces.
- Their social conflict often comes from being treated as pets, property, servants, attractions, or labor.
- Predator Beastfolk are often feared; prey Beastfolk are often exploited.
- Beastfolk sub-races should have strong sensory and movement identities.
- Werewolves should carry special social tension because they are both Beastfolk and transformation danger.

### Fae Race Profile

**Race:** Fae  
**General Role in Huwan:** Weirdos and strange creatures with strange ways of doing things  
**Common Reputation:** "Did you see that fairy talking to the tree? Do you think the tree talked back? Chuckles."  
**Closest Deities:** Ione, Goddess of Dreams and Nightmares, or Kroxona, Goddess of Nature  
**Major Continent:** Drizis  
**Major Kingdom:** Empire of Vuntis  
**Population Status:** Rare, seen as funny-looking or strange  
**Must-Have Traits:** Deformed or whimsical; unusual, dreamlike, strange  
**Must-Not-Have Trait:** Normality  

Fae are Huwan's reminder that not all life obeys ordinary logic. They are strange, whimsical, dreamlike, deformed, beautiful, unsettling, funny-looking, and impossible to fully categorize.

Fae may speak to trees, argue with stones, dance with moonlight, mislabel doors on purpose, follow rules nobody else knows, or treat serious events with baffling emotional timing. Sometimes the tree talks back.

They are rare, and their rarity makes them even harder for other races to understand.

Confirmed Fae sub-races include Fairies, Arch Fae, Fauns, Halflings, Sprites, Briar Fae, Moth Fae, Mirror Fae, Rootling Fae, and Dream Fae.

#### Fae Mana Well

Typical mana well depth: **Deep but unstable**

Fae magic is often tied to dreams, nature, weird logic, bargains, illusions, growth, glamour, and impossible rules.

Fae mana strengths:

- Strong compatibility with dream magic.
- Strong compatibility with nature magic.
- Strong compatibility with illusion, enchantment, and strange utility magic.
- Good affinity for pocket spaces, weird doors, and non-linear magical effects.
- Natural connection to Ione and Kroxona.

Fae mana weaknesses:

- Unstable or unpredictable.
- May behave oddly under strict rune or wizard frameworks.
- Can create strange side effects.
- May follow fae logic rather than ordinary spell logic.

#### Fae Birth Traits

- Dreamlike presence.
- Whimsical or deformed features.
- Strange proportions, colors, textures, or movement.
- Unusual senses.
- Fae logic.
- Nature or dream resonance.
- Resistance to being fully understood.

#### Fae Bonuses

- Bonus to illusion and enchantment.
- Bonus to nature or dream magic.
- Bonus to unusual problem-solving.
- Bonus to social confusion, misdirection, and strange bargains.
- Bonus in forests, fae territory, dreamlike areas, and reality-thin places.
- Easier interaction with sprites, dryads, treants, and other fae-linked beings.

#### Fae Weaknesses

- Seen as strange, funny-looking, or hard to trust.
- Rare and poorly understood.
- May struggle with rigid social institutions.
- May confuse ordinary citizens without meaning to.
- Normality is not appropriate for Fae identity.
- Some Fae rules may be dangerous if outsiders misunderstand them.

#### Fae Special Abilities

Potential Fae traits:

- Fae Glamour: Fae can influence appearance, emotion, or perception in small ways.
- Dream-Touched: Fae interact more easily with dreams, illusions, and Ione's influence.
- Nature-Whisper: Fae may communicate with plants, trees, or natural spirits.
- Strange Bargain: Fae can form unusual agreements with magical consequences.
- Reality Oddity: Fae sometimes resist ordinary spatial, social, or magical expectations.

#### Fae Reputation Effects

- Seen as weird, whimsical, suspicious, or amusing.
- Often underestimated because people treat them like jokes.
- Respected by Druids, dreamers, artists, and certain mages.
- Misunderstood by Humans, Dwarves, and rigid institutions.
- Sometimes feared when their strange behavior proves powerful.
- May be welcomed in places that value wonder and feared in places that value order.

#### Fae Class and Job Synergies

Strong synergies:

- Druid
- Sorcerer
- Mage
- Warlock of Arch-Fey patrons
- Illusion-focused Wizard
- Enchanter-style paths
- Artist, performer, herbalist, or dream-related jobs
- Tamer
- Adventurer

Fae can pursue many paths, but they should never feel normal. Their strongest identity is dreamlike, strange, natural, whimsical, and slightly impossible.

#### Fae Sub-Races

Fae sub-races should preserve strangeness, whimsy, deformity, dream logic, nature resonance, and anti-normality. Fae do not need to be pretty or ugly; they need to feel unusual.

| Sub-Race | Identity | Mana Well | Core Traits | Weaknesses | Strong Synergies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Fairy | Tiny or small winged Fae tied to flight, mischief, flowers, and glamours. | Deep but unstable | Flight, glamour, tiny size, nature talk. | Fragile, easily underestimated or captured. | Druid, Enchanter, Illusion, Scout. |
| Arch Fae | High Fae beings with strange authority and old pact power. | Very Deep and unstable | Fae command, bargains, glamour, reality oddity. | Bound by strange laws, dangerous attention. | Warlock patron, Sorcerer, Enchanter, Ruler. |
| Faun | Goat/deer-like Fae tied to music, revelry, forests, and instinct. | Deep | Hooves, horns, music, fertility, wild movement. | Distracted by revelry, social chaos. | Bard-style roles, Druid, Tamer, Performer. |
| Halfling | Small Fae-adjacent folk tied to quick feet, appetite, wit, and survival. | Average | Small size, luck, stealth, comfort craft. | Physically small, underestimated. | Thief, Merchant, Cook, Archer, Adventurer. |
| Sprite | Tiny light-nature Fae tied to small magic, curiosity, and quick movement. | Deep but small capacity | Glow, speed, plant talk, mischief. | Fragile, distractible. | Scout, Illusion, Druid, Messenger. |
| Briar Fae | Thorn, vine, and hedge Fae tied to barriers and prickly bargains. | Deep | Thorn growth, defensive nature magic, boundary sense. | Rigid about promises and trespass. | Druid, Guard, Rune-like wards, Enchanter. |
| Moth Fae | Night-winged Fae tied to dreams, moonlight, and quiet attraction to light. | Deep | Dream sense, silent flight, night movement. | Light obsession, fragile wings. | Dream magic, Illusion, Scout, Priest of Ione. |
| Mirror Fae | Reflection-linked Fae tied to doubles, strange doors, and self-facing truths. | Deep but unstable | Reflection travel, mimicry, self-visions. | Identity confusion, mirror dependence. | Illusion, Spy, Enchanter, Matrena oddities. |
| Rootling Fae | Small root-and-bark Fae tied to old trees and slow memory. | Deep but slow | Plant body, root sense, old memory. | Fire weakness, slow movement. | Druid, Herbalist, Guardian, Elder roles. |
| Dream Fae | Ione-touched Fae who drift between dreams and waking. | Very Deep and unstable | Dream speech, nightmare resistance, imagination magic. | Reality confusion, unstable emotions. | Sorcerer, Dream magic, Illusion, Seer. |

##### Fae Sub-Race Notes

- Fae should never feel normal.
- Fae may be whimsical, unsettling, funny-looking, beautiful, deformed, tiny, tall, plantlike, animal-like, reflective, or dreamlike.
- Fae social rules can be strange but should have internal consistency.
- Fae are excellent for unusual player builds, strange magic, bargains, dreams, nature, and reality-bending story hooks.

### Special Playable Lineages and Evolvable Races

Some playable races and sub-races do not fit cleanly under one major race. These lineages are special, transformed, undead, monster-born, divine-touched, or progression-based.

| Lineage | Identity | Mana Well | Core Traits | Weaknesses | Strong Synergies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vampire | Undead blood-drinker tied to night, charm, old wealth, and predation. | Deep but cursed | Night power, charm, regeneration, blood hunger. | Sun weakness, Church suspicion, hunger management. | Noble, Thief, Warlock, Enchanter, Assassin. |
| Werewolf | Transformation lineage also listed under Beastfolk. | Deep but unstable | Beast form, strength, tracking, fear aura. | Control risk, social fear, exploitation. | Druid, Hunter, Barbarian-style paths. |
| Ghost | Dead spirit lineage that remains after death or special birth/death conditions. | Strange, spirit-based | Intangibility, spirit sight, haunting, memory residue. | Weak physical interaction, Church attention, unfinished business. | Spirit Magic, Spy, Seer, Reincarnated hooks. |
| Lich | Undead spellcaster lineage tied to death magic, phylactery-like anchors, and forbidden study. | Deep but death-bound | Death magic, long existence, undead resilience. | Feared, hunted, anchor vulnerability. | Wizard, Necromancer, Warlock, Forbidden Magic. |
| Skeleton | Evolvable undead body lineage. | Low to Average, death-aspected | No hunger, bone body, undead persistence, evolution path. | Fragile early body, Church hostility, social fear. | Adventurer, Warrior, Necromancy, Dungeon Explorer. |
| Slime Monster | Evolvable monster lineage tied to slime evolution. | Varies by slime type | Body adaptation, absorb traits, evolution branches. | Vulnerable to containment, identity instability. | Tamer, Alchemist, Monster paths, Slime Gear. |
| Angelic | Divine-touched lineage tied to holy light, duty, and radiant presence. | Deep, holy-aspected | Radiance, flight or wing potential, holy affinity. | Burden of expectation, dark magic vulnerability. | Priest, Paladin, Healer, Noble service. |
| Reincarnated | Secret lineage/class-state unlocked after true death without revival. | Varies, soul-enhanced | Expanded character creation, 15 skills, 15 abilities, unique/master options. | Old story closed, divine attention, possible fragmented memory. | Any path, Progression Rank, hidden paths. |
| Homunculus | Artificial life, often tied to Matrena's failed experiments. | Artificial or unstable | Custom body logic, lab-born traits, odd resistances. | Identity issues, creator ownership risk, unstable design. | Mage, Artificer, Wizard, Experiment paths. |
| Dragon-Blooded | Mortal lineage touched by dragon ancestry, pact, dungeon mana, or hoard exposure. | Deep and draconic | Breath trace, draconic instinct, rune affinity. | Dragon attention, pride, treasure fixation. | Rune Mage, Sorcerer, Warlock, Dungeon Explorer. |

#### Evolvable Race Notes

Evolvable races should grow through form changes, absorbed traits, experience, environment, magic exposure, and milestone choices.

Strong evolvable candidates:

- Slime Monster
- Skeleton
- Vampire
- Werewolf
- Ghost
- Lich
- Dragon-Blooded
- Reincarnated

Evolution should not only mean stronger stats. It should change body, needs, reputation, abilities, social treatment, and story options.

#### Undead Lineage Notes

Undead playable lineages should interact heavily with:

- The Church
- Priests
- Paladins
- Death Magic
- Spirit Magic
- Udum
- House Gravewyn
- Reincarnation
- Resurrection law
- Social fear

Undead players should be playable, but society should react.

#### Divine and Artificial Lineage Notes

Angelic and Homunculus lineages should sit on opposite sides of creation identity.

- Angelics feel divinely touched, expected to be holy, useful, or good.
- Homunculi feel artificial, experimental, possibly ownerless, possibly abandoned.

Both should create strong story hooks around identity, purpose, and whether a being is more than its origin.

## Playable Identity

Every player begins as a **Noob**.

The Noob state is not just a joke title. It is the player's initial life stage before they prove themselves through skills, abilities, choices, quests, relationships, and survival.

Players do not immediately receive a full class or job. They unlock classes and jobs by developing the required skills and abilities.

### Player Class Rules

- Every player starts as Progression Zero.
- A player can multiclass into up to 3 classes.
- Classes require skill and ability unlocks.
- Class progression should reflect player actions, not only menu choices.

### Player Job Rules

- A player can hold up to 5 jobs.
- Jobs represent social, economic, crafting, religious, criminal, or civic roles.
- Jobs should interact with towns, guilds, reputation, economy, and NPC relationships.

### Progression Zero

Every player begins the game as **Progression Zero**. This is the true starter class and signals that the player has not yet committed to a major path. It also lets players know that there is a progressive level beyond the beginning.

After the tutorial, players can choose a starting class path.

Starting class choices:

- Archer
- Priest
- Knight
- Adventurer Rank F
- Thief
- Progression Rank 1

Progression Rank 1 is the allrounder class path. It is useful for players who do not want to join a guild immediately.

Progression Rank 1 is basically Noob class level 2.

When a player does not immediately choose a specialized class, they can continue developing as a Noob through Progression Rank 1. This grants roughly one-third of every starter class as a broad bonus, letting the player keep progressing without committing too early.

Progression Rank 1 benefits:

- Generalist growth.
- Small bonuses from multiple class directions.
- More time to explore guilds, jobs, and hidden paths.
- Better chance to discover the secret Mages Guild later.
- Flexible stat growth before specialization.

Progression Rank 1 is weaker than a focused class in its specialty, but stronger than staying completely undeveloped.

The Progression Rank path can continue up to **Level 10**.

Reaching Progression Rank Level 10 is a very long road and an extremely hard milestone. It is meant for players who refuse to specialize early and instead push the Noob/generalist path as far as it can go.

Progression Rank Level 10 reward:

- Mastery of every class.
- Access to all class abilities.
- Access to all class spells.
- Access to all class traits.
- Access to all class rewards.
- Broad stat mastery.
- Access to mastery points from every class path.

This is the ultimate "I mastered everything I could" route. It should feel almost ridiculous to achieve, but astounding when earned.

Reaching Progression Rank Level 10 also unlocks the hidden path to become a **Demi-God**.

Demi-God is no joke. It could easily take over 20 years of player experience, grinding, stat training, world favor, and World Popularity to achieve.

Exact Demi-God unlock requirements:

- Build exactly 100 trillion experience points.
- Max all stats at 999.
- Fill the World Favor bar.
- Max out World Popularity by being top server champion 10 years in a row.
- Receive approval from most of the world's pantheon.

Meeting these requirements transforms the player into a Demi-God.

The Demi-God path allows the player to basically create anything they choose within the world or server, depending on permissions, balance, and story context.

#### Demi-God Ascension Announcement

When a player becomes a Demi-God, the event should be impossible to miss.

The screen lights up and announces the ascension to the whole server. The announcement may also echo across official player forums, records, or server history pages.

Core announcement concept:

**A player has become a Demi-God and unlocked the ability to change Huwan.**

This should feel like a myth becoming real in front of everyone. After the first Demi-God ascension, the hidden goal becomes visible in player culture. People begin asking how it happened, what the requirements are, who can do it next, and how far the game actually lets them go.

The first Demi-God ascension should permanently alter how players think about Huwan. It is not just a reward. It is proof that the world can be changed by someone who earns enough power, favor, history, and divine approval.

#### Real-World Creator Invitation

For the first players who legitimately achieve Demi-God status, the reward may extend beyond the game.

The creator may personally contact the player, invite them to the studio, discuss what changes they want to bring into Huwan, and pay them for their contribution or consultation.

This turns Demi-God ascension into a real creator-recognized achievement. The player does not merely unlock world-changing power in the server. They earn a chance to help shape Huwan's future as part of the living development history of the game.

This should be treated as an extraordinary out-of-game reward, not a routine automated system.

Possible Demi-God capabilities:

- Create items.
- Create structures.
- Create quests.
- Create magical effects.
- Create localized events.
- Shape parts of the world.
- Influence server economy.
- Bless or curse areas.
- Build custom systems under moderation.
- Participate in divine-scale storylines.

This path should remain extremely rare. Demi-God status is not a normal class reward; it is a world-changing achievement that marks a player as someone who has become part of Huwan's myth.

Choosing not to join a guild right away can preserve access to later discovery of the secret Mages Guild. This makes restraint and curiosity valid progression choices instead of punishing players who delay commitment.

### Class Limits

- Each player can have up to 3 classes.
- Each class maxes out at level 20.
- Classes require stat growth, skills, abilities, quests, training, guild access, or special discoveries to unlock.

### Starting Jobs

First available job choices:

- Builder
- Hunter/Gatherer
- Adventurer
- Officer
- Merchant
- Blacksmith
- Tavern Owner
- Farmer
- Fencer
- Dungeon Explorer
- Craftsman
- Baker
- Contractor

### Job Limits

- Each player can have up to 5 jobs.
- Each job maxes out at Master Rank.
- Some jobs have hidden ranks beyond Master Rank.
- Example: Blacksmith can have a hidden God Rank.

Jobs require stat growth, tools, training, resources, reputation, guild permission, or practice to unlock and advance.

### Job Rank Ladder

Jobs use a rank ladder instead of normal class levels.

Standard job ranks:

| Rank | Meaning | Typical Unlocks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Novice | First real exposure to the job. | Basic tasks, basic tools, low pay, simple reputation gain. |
| Apprentice | Learns under another worker or guild. | Better tasks, mentor access, basic recipes, low-risk contracts. |
| Skilled | Can work independently. | Better pay, job-specific quests, minor business access. |
| Professional | Recognized as reliable. | Licensed work, larger contracts, hired help, better market trust. |
| Expert | Known for quality. | Specialized recipes, rare contracts, apprentices, regional reputation. |
| Advanced | High-level worker with serious authority. | Advanced tools, better facilities, guild influence, magical storage eligibility. |
| Master | Peak public rank for the job. | Masterwork creation, top contracts, strong social respect, teaching rights. |
| Hidden God Rank | Secret rank beyond Master for rare jobs. | Mythic creation, world recognition, legendary recipes, divine or server-level notice. |

Job rank progression should depend on:

- Practice
- Completed work orders
- Quality of output
- Reputation
- Tools
- Facilities
- Materials
- Guild approval
- Customer satisfaction
- Innovation
- Risk taken
- Teaching others
- Major quests

Job rank can also fall through fraud, repeated failure, criminal abuse, broken guild law, abandoned contracts, or catastrophic public mistakes.

### Starter Job Profiles

These are the first job profiles for early player and NPC progression. Exact recipes, work orders, wages, and stat requirements can be expanded later.

| Job | Role | Main Stats | Core Tools | Early Unlocks | Economy and Quest Hooks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Builder | Constructs and repairs homes, shops, roads, walls, bridges, and town structures. | Strength, Dexterity, Defense | Hammer, rope, shovel, basic wood, stone, clay | Basic repairs, small structures, work crew access | NPCs request repairs, nobles fund buildings, poor district needs housing. |
| Hunter/Gatherer | Collects food, herbs, hides, bones, fish, wood, and field resources. | Dexterity, Wisdom, Luck | Gathering knife, bow, fishing rod, skinning knife, pouches | F-rank gathering routes, animal harvesting, basic tracking | Feeds markets, meal halls, alchemists, taverns, and poor residents. |
| Adventurer | Takes quests, fights monsters, explores danger zones, and gathers proof of completion. | Strength, Dexterity, Health, Luck | Basic weapon, armor, backpack, guild tag | F-rank quests, bounty proof, party finding | Responds to monster population, NPC needs, guild requests, and dungeon reports. |
| Officer | Enforces law, patrols districts, investigates crimes, and manages civic order. | Charisma, Defense, Wisdom, Strength | Badge, weapon, notebook, restraints | Patrol rights, questioning authority, minor warrants | Handles theft, violence, magic misuse, district unrest, and noble pressure. |
| Merchant | Buys, sells, transports, prices, stores, and negotiates goods. | Charisma, Intelligence, Luck, Wisdom | Money pouch, ledger, stall, wagon | Basic stall, small trade routes, appraisal | Drives market supply, banks, auctions, resource shortages, and Shaohr contracts. |
| Blacksmith | Smelts ore, forges weapons, armor, tools, and repairs gear. | Strength, Dexterity, Intelligence | Furnace, anvil, hammer, tongs, sharpening stone | Iron bars, basic weapons, basic repairs | Needs ore, fuel, hides, guild orders, Adventurers Guild demand. |
| Tavern Owner | Runs food, drink, rooms, rumors, social spaces, and local hospitality. | Charisma, Wisdom, Luck, Intelligence | Cooking utensils, pots, pans, storage, tables | Basic meals, room rentals, rumor gathering | Links travelers, workers, poor residents, guilds, gossip, and job rumors. |
| Farmer | Grows food, herbs, flax, livestock feed, and seasonal crops. | Wisdom, Strength, Health, Luck | Hoe, rake, watering can, seeds, shovel | Small crop plots, herb beds, livestock care | Drives food prices, famine risk, Amera worship, meal halls, and harvest quests. |
| Fencer | Skilled blade user and duelist, often tied to light weapons, technique, and reputation. | Dexterity, Strength, Charisma, Luck | Sword, falchion, katana, gloves, sheath | Basic duels, weapon technique, precision strikes | Can train nobles, settle disputes, guard clients, or join Adventurer work. |
| Dungeon Explorer | Enters dungeons, maps routes, identifies traps, gathers relics, and returns alive. | Wisdom, Dexterity, Luck, Health | Rope, torch/light source, map, lockpicks, backpack | Floor mapping, trap spotting, chest recovery | Feeds dungeon economy, Thieves Guild maps, Adventurers Guild proof, relic discovery. |
| Craftsman | General maker of useful goods, tools, furniture, pouches, containers, and repairs. | Dexterity, Intelligence, Wisdom | Sewing kit, wood carving kit, hammer, repair kit | Basic tools, pouches, simple gear, furniture | Supports every other job through tools, repairs, and low-tier production. |
| Baker | Produces bread, pastries, trail food, meal hall staples, and morale foods. | Wisdom, Dexterity, Charisma, Luck | Oven, flour, water, honey, herbs, cooking utensils, pans | Basic bread, travel biscuits, honey cakes, simple food buffs | Ties farmers, taverns, poor districts, meal halls, Adventurers Guild rations, and famine response together. |
| Contractor | Organizes workers, materials, construction jobs, repairs, permits, and paid labor teams. | Charisma, Intelligence, Wisdom, Strength | Ledger, contracts, permits, measuring tools, wagon access | Hire crews, organize builds, estimate costs, manage work orders | Connects builders, nobles, merchants, poor district housing, guild halls, road repair, and city expansion. |

### Job Rank Effects

Higher job ranks affect more than personal skill.

Job rank can improve:

- Pay
- Salary
- Resource discounts
- Customer trust
- Guild standing
- Shop access
- Tool access
- Recipe access
- Social respect
- NPC hiring
- Apprenticeship options
- Quest quality
- Market influence
- Work speed
- Item quality
- Failure recovery

Job rank can also affect relationships. A Master Blacksmith is treated differently than a Novice Blacksmith. A Professional Tavern Owner hears rumors a Novice never would. An Advanced Officer can enter places a normal guard cannot.

### Job Salary and Resource Discounts

Jobs provide salary, but they also provide practical access to discounted resources, tools, services, facilities, and supplies.

As a player or NPC rises in job rank, they gain better buying power within that profession's economy.

Discounts can apply to:

- Raw materials
- Tools
- Crafting stations
- Repairs
- Storage
- Transport
- Food
- Lodging
- Training
- Guild services
- Professional permits
- Work-related gear

At high enough rank, some job-related resources can become free.

Example logic:

- A Novice Blacksmith pays normal price for iron ore.
- An Apprentice Blacksmith gets a small ore discount.
- A Professional Blacksmith gets regular supplier pricing.
- A Master Blacksmith may receive common ore for free because suppliers want their business, reputation, and finished goods.

Discounts should match the job.

Examples:

- Blacksmiths get discounts on ore, coal, metal bars, anvils, hammers, repairs, and forge access.
- Farmers get discounts on seeds, tools, livestock feed, water access, and land services.
- Merchants get discounts on stalls, wagons, storage, banking, and trade permits.
- Tavern Owners get discounts on food, drink, furniture, cooking tools, and room supplies.
- Dungeon Explorers get discounts on rope, maps, torches, lockpicks, backpacks, and dungeon gear.
- Officers get discounts or issued access to uniforms, weapons, restraints, reports, and civic services.
- Hunter/Gatherers get discounts on bows, arrows, pouches, skinning knives, fishing rods, and field gear.
- Bakers get discounts on flour, honey, fruits, herbs, ovens, kitchen tools, fuel, and food storage.
- Contractors get discounts on bulk wood, stone, clay, permits, wagon transport, hired labor, and construction tools.

This makes jobs economically meaningful even outside direct quest rewards.

### Hidden God Rank Jobs

Some jobs can secretly progress beyond Master Rank into **Hidden God Rank**.

Hidden God Rank should be extremely rare and tied to legendary achievement, impossible craftsmanship, divine notice, world-changing contribution, or server-wide recognition.

Known possible Hidden God Rank jobs:

- Blacksmith
- Craftsman
- Builder
- Merchant
- Farmer
- Dungeon Explorer
- Tavern Owner, if the tavern becomes historically important
- Baker, if their food becomes culturally or magically legendary
- Contractor, if their projects reshape cities or continents

Hidden God Rank examples:

- A God Rank Blacksmith can forge gear that becomes myth.
- A God Rank Builder can create structures that change city history.
- A God Rank Merchant can reshape world trade.
- A God Rank Farmer can end famine or create legendary crops.
- A God Rank Dungeon Explorer can map impossible spaces and survive what should not be survivable.
- A God Rank Baker can create food that heals morale, feeds armies, preserves life, or becomes sacred culture.
- A God Rank Contractor can coordinate impossible builds, rebuild ruined kingdoms, or create cities that change history.

Hidden God Rank should never be easy, public, or guaranteed. It should feel discovered, not handed out.

### Advanced Leadership Jobs

Leadership jobs are not normal starter jobs. They require reputation, trust, appointment, followers, property, guild authority, noble recognition, election, conquest, or major achievement.

Leadership jobs affect other people directly. They can change laws, assign labor, manage resources, command guards, influence markets, start conflicts, settle disputes, and generate quests for others.

| Leadership Job | Role | Unlock Requirements | Authority | Risks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Mayor | Runs a town or city district through civic administration. | Local reputation, citizen support, council approval, property or appointment. | Taxes, local projects, services, disputes, public works. | Scandals, unrest, faction pressure, noble interference. |
| Guildmaster | Leads a guild branch or entire guild. | High guild rank, member trust, major guild service, leadership vote or appointment. | Quest boards, guild funds, member discipline, training access. | Rival guilds, corruption, member failure, economic pressure. |
| Noble | Holds title, land, family authority, and political obligations. | Birthright, marriage, appointment, conquest, adoption, purchase, or royal grant. | Land, retainers, taxes, private guards, marriage alliances. | House rivalry, assassination, scandal, rebellion. |
| Commander | Leads military, guards, knights, mercenaries, or patrol units. | Combat record, leadership trust, rank, appointment, officer path. | Troop movement, defense plans, patrol orders, battle strategy. | Casualties, mutiny, war crimes, political blame. |
| Councilor | Sits on a council and shapes policy, law, or economic decisions. | Reputation, election, appointment, guild backing, noble backing. | Votes, policy influence, hearings, civic debates. | Bribery, public anger, faction pressure. |
| Overseer | Manages workers, production sites, mines, farms, quarries, or large projects. | Contractor, Builder, Merchant, Farmer, or noble appointment. | Work schedules, material allocation, wages, quotas. | Exploitation, strikes, accidents, poor district unrest. |
| Village Chief | Leads a smaller settlement or tribal community. | Community trust, elder approval, strength, tradition, or inheritance. | Local defense, food stores, disputes, traditions. | Monster threats, famine, family feuds, outside pressure. |
| Ruler | King, Queen, Emperor, Dynasty head, or equivalent high ruler. | Royal bloodline, conquest, pantheon approval, massive World Popularity, succession. | Kingdom law, armies, taxes, diplomacy, war, succession. | Rebellion, war, assassination, divine judgment. |
| Faction Leader | Leads an ideological, religious, criminal, or political faction. | Followers, charisma, doctrine, reputation, victory, fear, or vision. | Faction strategy, recruitment, doctrine, operations. | Splits, betrayal, public backlash, extremist escalation. |
| Dungeon Lord | Controls a dragon-born dungeon through taming, pact, conquest, or divine-scale agreement. | Tame or befriend/control dungeon dragon, dungeon authority, guild recognition or force. | Dungeon access, resource flow, monster pressure, floor permissions, dungeon economy. | Guild conflict, noble claims, Mages Guild monitoring, raids. |

#### Leadership Job Rules

Leadership jobs should require more than personal skill. A player must be recognized by other people, institutions, followers, or the world itself.

Leadership progression depends on:

- Reputation
- World Popularity
- Charisma
- Wealth
- Allies
- Followers
- Guild rank
- Noble recognition
- Public trust
- Successful decisions
- Crisis management
- Economic stability
- Military or civic achievement

Leadership jobs can create quests for others. For example:

- A Mayor can post city repair quests.
- A Guildmaster can create guild contracts.
- A Commander can assign patrol or escort missions.
- A Noble can issue land, marriage, scandal, or inheritance quests.
- A Dungeon Lord can open floors, set resource permissions, or request dungeon defense.

Leadership jobs should also create accountability. If a leader abuses authority, NPCs remember, factions react, markets shift, and rebellion becomes possible.

#### Leadership Job Benefits

Leadership jobs can grant:

- Followers
- Authority
- Tax income
- Salary
- Resource access
- Discounts up to free materials for official projects
- Unique quests
- Policy control
- Troop or worker assignment
- Reputation influence
- World Popularity growth
- Access to restricted meetings and records

#### Leadership Job Risks

Leadership jobs can cause:

- Public anger
- Assassination attempts
- Noble rivalry
- Guild strikes
- Faction war
- Economic collapse
- Corruption scandals
- Divine scrutiny
- Loss of authority
- Rebellion

Leadership is power, but in Huwan power creates relationships, and relationships determine the story.

### Long-Term Player-Run Server Vision

Far in the future, mature Huwan servers should be populated, run, managed, and balanced primarily by players.

The long-term goal is for player civilization to become self-sustaining.

Players may eventually control:

- Guild leadership
- Town leadership
- Kingdom politics
- Faction direction
- Dungeon economies
- Trade routes
- Markets
- Resource access
- Laws
- Public works
- Military defense
- Worker organization
- Quest creation
- Conflict resolution
- Cultural events
- Server history

Developer systems, AI simulation, Laucian moderation, and world rules provide the foundation, but long-lived servers should gradually shift meaningful authority to players who earn it.

This creates a world where players are not only adventurers inside Huwan. They become citizens, leaders, merchants, rulers, rivals, reformers, villains, legends, and eventually possible Demi-Gods.

Player-run balance should emerge through relationships, law, economy, reputation, world favor, guild authority, faction conflict, and consequences. If a player-led system becomes abusive, the world should respond through NPC memory, market pressure, rebellion, rival factions, divine attention, or moderator intervention when necessary.

The goal is not perfect safety. The goal is a living server where freedom, accountability, and player history shape the world.

### Character Progression, Classes, Jobs, Skills, And Abilities System Completion V1.0

Character progression is the player's personal climb through Huwan. It begins with **Progression Zero**, grows through skills, abilities, stats, classes, jobs, guilds, relationships, contracts, reputation, factions, and world events, and can eventually reach impossible hidden paths such as **Reincarnated**, **Hidden God Rank jobs**, **Progression Rank 10**, and **Demi-God ascension**.

Core rule:

- **Progression should come from what the player actually does.**
- A player who hunts, crafts, governs, trades, studies, heals, steals, raises a family, leads a faction, tames monsters, clears dungeons, honors contracts, or becomes infamous should grow differently.
- Menus show progress, but actions create progress.

#### Player Progression Spine

| Stage | Meaning | Main Unlocks |
|---|---|---|
| Character Creation | Player defines origin, race, sub-race, body, alignment, 5 skills, and 5 abilities. | Permanent identity choices, birth traits, starting ability profile. |
| Tutorial | Laucian's white lab teaches movement, HUD, inventory, combat, map, quests, and survival basics. | Basic controls, Progression Zero confirmation, starter kit. |
| Progression Zero | True starter state before commitment. | Early survival, basic quests, stat growth, path discovery. |
| First Path Choice | Player chooses starting class path or stays on Progression Rank. | Archer, Priest, Knight, Adventurer Rank F, Thief, Progression Rank 1. |
| First Jobs | Player begins economic or social roles. | Up to 5 jobs over time; early tools, salary, discounts. |
| Class Growth | Player levels classes through action and training. | Up to 3 classes, each max level 20. |
| Job Growth | Player ranks jobs through work, quality, reputation, and contracts. | Rank ladder up to Master, rare Hidden God Rank. |
| Advanced Identity | Player gains titles, guild rank, faction role, property, relationships, and reputation. | Better quests, leadership, rare trainers, social power. |
| Hidden Paths | Player discovers secret classes, races, guilds, abilities, or fate routes. | Mages Guild, Reincarnated, monster paths, relic compatibility. |
| Mythic Progression | Player enters server-scale growth. | Legendary gear, dragon treaties, Dungeon Lord, kingdom rule, demi-god path. |

#### Character Creation Rules

At character creation, players choose:

- Race
- Sub-race or lineage
- Body customization
- Alignment
- 5 starting skills
- 5 starting abilities
- Starting personality flavor, if supported
- Starting social identity, if supported

Starting skills and abilities are permanent. They cannot be unlearned because they represent foundational identity, birth talent, early life, soul pattern, training, or innate nature.

Reincarnated character creation is stronger and more detailed:

- Up to 15 skills
- Up to 15 abilities
- Access to unique, rare, or master-level options
- Possible memory echoes
- Possible old reputation traces
- Possible soul affinity
- Possible hidden path recognition

#### Class System

Classes represent combat, magic, faith, stealth, adventure, and power identity.

Class rules:

| Rule | Value |
|---|---|
| Starting state | Progression Zero |
| Normal class limit | Up to 3 active classes |
| Class max level | 20 |
| Starting class choices | Archer, Priest, Knight, Adventurer Rank F, Thief, Progression Rank 1 |
| Unlock style | Stats, skills, abilities, guilds, quests, training, reputation, discoveries |
| Class identity | Defines combat role, magic access, class abilities, traits, and progression rewards |

Class unlock examples:

| Class | Likely Unlock Requirements |
|---|---|
| Archer | Dexterity, bow use, tracking or scouting practice, Archers Guild work. |
| Priest | Faith commitment, deity/patron selection, healing or holy training, Church access. |
| Knight | Defense, Strength, law reputation, oath training, Knights Guild or noble backing. |
| Adventurer Rank F | Adventurers Guild registration, basic survival, combat readiness, proof handling. |
| Thief | Dexterity, stealth, lock use, underworld contact, Thieves Guild attention. |
| Mage | Mana well awakening, rune study, Dawnspire clues, Laucian contact. |
| Wizard | Intelligence, spell framework study, spellbook discipline, academy or mentor access. |
| Sorcerer | Innate magic, visualization control, meditation, mana stability. |
| Warlock | Contract with patron, pact terms, ongoing obligations. |
| Paladin | Priest foundation plus weapon training, Strength, oath, holy combat. |
| Druid | Nature bond, animal handling, healing/nature magic, wildshape discovery. |

#### Progression Rank Path

Progression Rank is the generalist "Noob-plus" path for players who refuse to specialize too soon.

Progression Rank rules:

- Progression Rank 1 becomes available after the tutorial if the player avoids immediate guild/class commitment.
- It grants roughly one-third of several starter class directions.
- It keeps the player flexible and improves chances to discover hidden paths such as the Mages Guild.
- Progression Rank can eventually reach Level 10, but the road should be brutally long.

Progression Rank 10 unlocks:

- Mastery of every class path.
- Access to all class abilities, spells, traits, rewards, stats, and mastery points.
- Hidden path toward Demi-God ascension.

Progression Rank should be weaker than specialization early, but absurdly rewarding for players who commit to the impossible long road.

#### Job System

Jobs represent economic, civic, crafting, criminal, religious, social, and leadership identity.

Job rules:

| Rule | Value |
|---|---|
| Job limit | Up to 5 jobs |
| Public max rank | Master |
| Hidden max rank | Hidden God Rank for rare jobs |
| Progress style | Work, contracts, quality, reputation, tools, facilities, customers, risk |
| Rewards | Salary, discounts, free resources at high rank, recipes, authority, contacts |

Job progression should create daily gameplay. A job is not just a passive buff; it ties the player into markets, NPC needs, contracts, faction requests, and settlement life.

Job advancement factors:

- Completed work orders
- Quality of output
- Customer satisfaction
- Repeated practice
- Tools and workstations
- Guild approval
- Innovation
- Reputation
- Teaching apprentices
- Risk taken
- Economy impact
- Contract completion
- Public demand

#### Stat System

Player stats:

| Stat | Primary Use |
|---|---|
| Strength | Melee power, forging, carrying, heavy labor, intimidation. |
| Charisma | Leadership, romance, persuasion, trade presence, law lobbying. |
| Intelligence | Finework, spell frameworks, crafting precision, strategy, learning. |
| Wisdom | Alchemy, judgment, taming, perception, faith, survival sense. |
| Dexterity | Crafting, archery, stealth, lockpicking, agility, precision. |
| Luck | Loot, rare encounters, critical moments, market fortune, strange fate. |
| Health | Survival, stamina, wounds, disease resistance, physical endurance. |
| Mana | Spell capacity, mana well use, magic control, sustained casting. |
| Defense | Damage resistance, armor use, endurance under pressure. |
| World Popularity | Hidden global recognition, fame/infamy, legendary attention, demi-god path. |

Stats grow from actions, training, skills, abilities, class progression, job work, relationships, blessings, curses, gear, and world events.

Stat max:

- Normal long-term maximum target: 999 for demi-god path requirements.
- Reaching 999 should be extreme and require years of play, rare training, world favor, gear, and story achievement.

#### Skill System

Skills represent learned practice.

Skill sources:

- Character creation
- Race/sub-race traits
- Class training
- Job work
- Guild training
- Mentors
- Books
- Repeated use
- Quest rewards
- Legendary NPCs
- Faction work
- Reincarnation
- Relics
- Dawnspire

Skill rules:

- Skills improve through use and relevant training.
- Skills should unlock recipes, actions, dialogue, jobs, classes, routes, and safer outcomes.
- Skills can be hidden until the player discovers the relevant activity.
- Some skills can decay in practical sharpness if ignored for extremely long periods, but should not simply vanish without design reason.

Skill categories:

- Combat
- Magic
- Crafting
- Gathering
- Survival
- Social
- Trade
- Crime
- Leadership
- Exploration
- Taming
- Faith
- Knowledge
- Governance
- Relationship

#### Ability System

Abilities are more identity-defining than skills. They can be passive, active, permanent, temporary, unique, divine, cursed, inherited, awakened, or granted.

Ability sources:

- Character creation
- Race and sub-race
- Bloodline magic
- Class
- Job
- Deity blessing
- Curse
- Relic
- Legendary gear
- Reincarnation
- Monster evolution
- Dragon pact
- Warlock pact
- Soul mate bond
- Laucian or Dawnspire
- Rare world event

Ability rules:

- Character creation abilities are permanent.
- Powerful abilities should have costs, requirements, drawbacks, counters, or story consequences.
- Unique abilities should be rare and tracked carefully.
- Abilities that alter mana wells, time, space, death, reincarnation, world law, or server state require special safeguards.

Examples:

| Ability | Use |
|---|---|
| Born Leader | Improves Charisma growth and ally trust. |
| Indomitable | Improves Defense growth and resistance under pressure. |
| Infinity | Can affect mana well capacity if properly cast, but does not automatically make the user all-powerful. |
| Darkvision | Race/bloodline perception trait. |
| Blindsight | Bloodline or special sensory ability. |
| Rage | Barbarian-style battle state tied to Ixnir's war/peace duality. |
| Wildshape | Druid masterwork ability. |

#### Needs And Progression

Hunger, thirst, and social levels are progression pressure systems.

| Need | Progression Role |
|---|---|
| Hunger | Ties players to food, hunting, farming, taverns, cooking, poverty, travel, and survival. |
| Thirst | Ties players to water, rivers, travel planning, settlement services, and survival risk. |
| Social | Ties players to friendship, romance, family, faction life, reputation, and mate discovery. |

Needs should not be annoying chores when managed well. They should create choices, urgency, economy, and social connection.

#### Unlock Logic

Unlocks should usually require multiple gates.

Gate types:

- Stat gate
- Skill gate
- Ability gate
- Class level gate
- Job rank gate
- Guild gate
- Reputation gate
- Relationship gate
- Contract gate
- Quest gate
- Item gate
- Location gate
- Deity/patron gate
- Race/sub-race gate
- Reincarnation gate
- World Popularity gate
- Admin/moderator approval gate for world-altering effects

Example:

- A player cannot become a Paladin just by clicking "Paladin." They need priestly foundation, weapon competence, physical strength, oath commitment, holy authority, and public or patron recognition.

#### Reincarnated Progression

Reincarnated is a secret race-state or class-state unlocked after true death when revival is impossible or refused.

Reincarnated benefits:

- More detailed character creation.
- Up to 15 skills and 15 abilities.
- Access to unique or master-level options.
- Easier progression on the next life.
- Possible old memory echoes.
- Possible reputation or soul traces.
- Possible relic, deity, or relationship recognition.

Reincarnated limits:

- The old story closes or transforms.
