- Old property, family, contracts, and reputation may not transfer cleanly.
- Some people may recognize the soul; others may reject the claim.
- Reincarnation can create divine, legal, inheritance, romance, and faction complications.

#### Demi-God Progression

Demi-God is the ultimate hidden goal.

Known requirements:

- Exactly 100 trillion experience points.
- All stats maxed at 999.
- World Favor bar filled.
- World Popularity maxed by being top server champion 10 years in a row.
- Approval from most of the Pantheon.
- Progression Rank Level 10 hidden path unlocked.

Demi-God rules:

- Demi-God is not a normal class.
- Demi-God status should be server-notable and world-changing.
- Demi-God powers should allow creation and change, but still require server rules, story context, and Admin safeguards.
- First legitimate Demi-God ascension may become a real creator-recognized achievement.

#### Progression And Other Systems

Progression should connect to:

- Reputation
- World Popularity
- Relationships
- Family and legacy
- Contracts
- Oaths
- Crime
- Law
- Factions
- Settlements
- Dungeons
- Dragon treaties
- Legendary encounters
- Crafting
- Economy
- Magic
- AI simulation
- Admin approval for world-changing effects

Examples:

- A famous healer gains priest opportunities faster.
- A hated thief may unlock underworld skills but lose city trust.
- A beloved ruler gains leadership progression but loses free time.
- A dragon-befriending tamer gains monster and dragon progression.
- A blacksmith who crafts legendary gear advances job rank and World Popularity.
- A player who breaks contracts may gain infamy but lose pact access.

#### Progression UI

Progression should be readable without revealing every secret at once.

UI areas:

- Character sheet
- Class page
- Job page
- Skills page
- Abilities page
- Stats page
- Needs display
- Reputation page
- Contract Board
- Quest journal
- Hidden progression hints
- Demi-god path fragments, once discovered

Hidden paths should appear first as strange achievements, rumors, locked icons, incomplete bars, dream messages, old lore, divine notices, or Laucian comments.

#### Progression Data Handoff

Recommended progression data fields:

| Field | Purpose |
|---|---|
| CharacterId | Player character identity. |
| RaceLineage | Race, sub-race, secret state, reincarnation state. |
| ProgressionState | Progression Zero, Progression Rank, class path, hidden path. |
| Classes | Up to 3 active classes and levels. |
| Jobs | Up to 5 active jobs and ranks. |
| Stats | Strength, Charisma, Intelligence, Wisdom, Dexterity, Luck, Health, Mana, Defense, World Popularity. |
| Skills | Learned, permanent, hidden, leveled, locked, and creation skills. |
| Abilities | Active, passive, permanent, temporary, unique, divine, cursed, bloodline. |
| Needs | Hunger, thirst, social level. |
| UnlockFlags | Guilds, classes, jobs, hidden paths, relic compatibility, Dawnspire. |
| ReputationLinks | Reputation and World Popularity effects. |
| RelationshipLinks | Romance, family, tamed, bonded, mentor, rival, patron. |
| ContractLinks | Oaths, pacts, jobs, debts, guild terms, dragon treaties. |
| AchievementFlags | Server firsts, titles, legendary events, demi-god fragments. |
| ReincarnationData | Soul echoes, old memories, inherited traces. |
| AdminFlags | Protected or world-changing progression requiring approval. |

#### Progression Implementation Checklist

Progression is implementation-ready when the game supports:

- Character creation with 5 permanent skills and 5 permanent abilities.
- Reincarnated creation with expanded choices.
- Progression Zero start.
- Starting class selection.
- Progression Rank path.
- Up to 3 classes.
- Class levels up to 20.
- Up to 5 jobs.
- Job rank ladder to Master.
- Hidden God Rank jobs.
- Stat growth from actions.
- Hunger, thirst, and social needs.
- Skill experience and unlocks.
- Ability sources and permanence rules.
- Class/job unlock gates.
- Reputation and World Popularity integration.
- Relationship and family integration.
- Contract, crime, law, faction, settlement, dungeon, and dragon integration.
- Hidden demi-god path tracking.
- Admin approval gates for world-changing progression.

#### Final Progression Rules

- Every player starts small.
- Every path should feel earned.
- Specialization should be strong.
- Generalist mastery should be absurdly hard but worth it.
- Jobs should matter as much as classes for shaping the world.
- Skills are what the player practices.
- Abilities are what define the player's nature.
- Reputation decides who opens doors.
- Relationships decide who keeps them open.
- World Popularity decides when the world starts watching.
- Demi-God is not the end of progression. It is the moment the player becomes part of Huwan's myth.

### Player Stats

Players can build the following stats:

- Strength
- Charisma
- Intelligence
- Wisdom
- Dexterity
- Luck
- Health
- Mana
- Defense
- World Popularity, secret stat

Stats influence class unlocks, job unlocks, crafting, combat, social success, magic, survival, reputation, and world events.

World Popularity is a secret stat that measures how widely the player is known across the world. It may affect fame, rumors, noble attention, guild treatment, faction invitations, hero status, villain reputation, and story escalation.

### Survival and Social Needs

Players must monitor:

- Thirst
- Hunger
- Social level

Thirst and hunger support survival gameplay and connect players to food, drink, farming, hunting, taverns, cooking, merchants, and poor district aid.

Social level represents the player's ability to form meaningful bonds. Higher social rank brings the player closer to finding a mate, building a family, strengthening alliances, unlocking social quests, and deepening NPC relationships.

### Skill and Ability Stat Growth

Certain skills and abilities help specific stats progress.

Examples:

- Born Leader helps build Charisma with allies.
- Indomitable helps build Defense.

Skills and abilities should support natural progression. A player who protects others should grow differently from a player who trades, studies magic, hunts monsters, steals, farms, leads, or crafts.

### Character Creation Skills and Abilities

At character creation, players choose:

- 5 skills
- 5 abilities

These starting choices are permanent and cannot be unlearned.

This gives every character a lasting identity from the beginning. Even though players start as Progression Zero and can later grow into classes, jobs, guilds, and secret paths, their first chosen skills and abilities remain part of who they are.

### Tutorial and First Hour Flow

After character creation, each player is magically teleported to Laucian's white lab space for the tutorial.

This tutorial should feel diegetic. The player is not just in a menu. They are standing in one of Laucian Xilocent Goldpetal's controlled tutorial spaces, where the SSS-ranked Mages Guild leader can safely explain how Huwan works without immediately dropping the player into danger.

#### Tutorial Location: Laucian's White Lab Space

The tutorial space is a clean white laboratory or testing room connected to Dawnspire-style logic.

Visual identity:

- White walls
- White floor
- Soft lighting
- Floating instructions
- Practice dummies
- Inventory pedestals
- Training chests
- Map table
- Quest board
- Safe combat arena
- Rune-marked exit door
- Laucian observing, explaining, teasing, and occasionally breaking the fourth wall

Laucian may use this space to explain that the player is still new, still Progression Zero, and very much not ready to touch everything that glows.

#### Core Tutorial Topics

The Laucian tutorial should teach:

- Movement
- Camera control
- Interact button
- Basic UI navigation
- Inventory management
- Equipment basics
- Item pickup
- Item use
- Basic battle system logistics
- Basic attack
- Dodge/block, if available
- Targeting, if available
- HUD reading
- Health display
- Mana display
- Hunger and thirst display
- Quest tracker
- Save game aspects
- Map reading
- Minimap reading
- Quest auto-follow
- Quest completion
- Basic NPC interaction
- Basic container opening

The tutorial should avoid overwhelming the player. It teaches enough to survive the first hour and lets the rest of the world teach advanced systems later.

#### Tutorial Sequence

Suggested sequence:

1. Character creation ends.
2. Player appears in white lab space.
3. Laucian introduces himself or pretends not to care while clearly managing everything.
4. Movement and camera tutorial.
5. Interaction tutorial using a simple object.
6. Inventory tutorial using a training chest.
7. Equipment tutorial using starter gear.
8. HUD tutorial showing health, mana, hunger, thirst, and social indicators.
9. Combat tutorial against a harmless dummy or illusion.
10. Map tutorial at a floating table.
11. Quest auto-follow tutorial using a simple lab quest.
12. Quest completion tutorial.
13. Save game explanation.
14. Laucian opens a rune-marked exit.
15. Player is sent to Eldoria as Progression Zero.

#### Deferred Learning Prompts

Advanced systems should be taught later when the player naturally encounters them.

Deferred tutorials:

- Crafting
- Gathering
- Farming
- Jobs
- Guilds
- Magic classes
- Rune placement
- Spellbooks
- Sorcerer control
- Warlock contracts
- Priest patrons
- Paladin smites
- Druid Wildshape
- Taming
- Slime evolution
- Theft
- Law
- Relationships
- Romance/mates
- Leadership jobs
- Dungeon systems
- Dragon resolution paths
- Marketplace/auction systems
- Player-run governance

Each deferred tutorial should appear only when relevant. For example, crafting prompts appear at a crafting station, theft prompts appear near owned containers, and relationship prompts appear after meaningful NPC interaction.

#### First Hour After Tutorial

After leaving Laucian's lab, the player should arrive in or near Eldoria.

Recommended first-hour flow:

1. Arrive as Progression Zero.
2. Meet a simple NPC guide or town greeter.
3. Learn hunger and thirst through a food or water task.
4. Visit a safe part of Guild Row.
5. See the Adventurers Guild, Merchants Guild, and Archers Guild from the outside.
6. Complete a simple F-rank gathering task in Mossgate Outskirts.
7. Encounter slimes in tall grass.
8. Return to town and complete the quest.
9. Receive first money, item, or relationship reward.
10. Choose whether to pursue a class, job, guild, or remain on Progression Rank.

The first hour should show the player that Huwan is not only about combat. It is about needs, people, work, relationships, resources, and choices.

### HUD, Minimap, and Navigation

The HUD should give players enough information to survive, travel, work, and follow quests without making Huwan feel like a menu-driven world.

The minimap is one of the most important HUD elements. It should act like the player's local awareness of the surrounding area.

#### HUD Display Philosophy

The normal HUD should be quiet by default and loud when something matters.

Players should not get so used to seeing status icons that they ignore dangerous effects. Minor information should stay available in menus, tooltips, or expanded HUD views, while harmful effects should appear clearly only when they need attention.

Core rule:

- Calm world state means a calm HUD.
- Dangerous world state means a louder HUD.
- Severe harmful effects should interrupt the player's attention without fully blocking gameplay.

#### Normal Exploration HUD

During normal exploration, the HUD should show only the information players need moment to moment.

Default visible elements:

- Health
- Mana
- Stamina or fatigue, if currently relevant
- Hunger and thirst only when shifting toward a meaningful threshold
- Social need only when it begins affecting mood, relationships, or progression
- Minimap
- Current tracked quest, if one is active
- Interaction prompt, only when near something usable
- Hotbar for equipped weapon, tool, item, spell, or ability shortcuts

The HUD should not constantly show every possible status, stat, currency, relationship score, job value, guild standing, or inventory warning. Those details belong in the full menus unless they become relevant.

#### Alert-Driven Status Effects

Harmful effects should appear when they begin affecting the player or are about to become dangerous.

Examples:

- Bleeding
- Poison
- Disease
- Burning
- Freezing
- Curse
- Mana depletion
- Mana overload risk
- Exhaustion
- Starvation
- Dehydration
- Suffocation
- Broken bone
- Crippled limb
- Heavy injury
- Infection
- Overweight inventory
- Critical equipment durability
- Wanted status
- Active bounty
- Stealth exposure risk
- Controlled, charmed, or magically influenced state

These effects should be visually clear and easy to understand quickly.

Alert behavior:

- Minor warnings appear briefly.
- Ongoing harmful effects remain visible until solved.
- Severe effects pulse, glow, shake, darken, flash, or use stronger sound cues.
- Effects that can kill the player should take priority over convenience information.
- Multiple harmful effects should stack cleanly without covering the minimap, hotbar, or combat view.

#### Status Severity Levels

Status alerts should use severity levels.

| Severity | Meaning | HUD Behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Minor | Useful to know, not urgent | Small icon, soft color, optional tooltip. |
| Warning | Can become dangerous soon | Persistent icon, stronger color, short sound cue. |
| Dangerous | Actively harming the player | Clear icon, pulse or glow, visible timer or severity meter. |
| Critical | Could kill, incapacitate, or cause major failure | Strong visual/audio cue, priority placement, possible controller vibration. |

Examples:

- Hunger at 60% does not need a warning.
- Hunger near starvation should show a warning.
- Starvation should become a dangerous or critical alert.
- A small bruise can stay in the status menu.
- Bleeding should appear immediately.
- A weak poison can show as warning.
- A lethal poison should become critical.

#### Expanded Status Screen

The full status screen should still exist for players who want details.

The status screen can show:

- All active buffs
- All active debuffs
- Hidden or unidentified effects, if the player has the skill to detect them
- Hunger value
- Thirst value
- Social value
- Sleep or fatigue level
- Disease progression
- Poison progression
- Injury details
- Reputation effects
- Relationship modifiers
- Guild penalties
- Job penalties
- Blessings
- Curses
- Mana well condition
- Equipment condition
- Carry weight
- Crime and bounty details

This gives advanced players full information without forcing every player to stare at constant status noise during normal play.

#### Currency and Inventory Visibility

Gold, pouch contents, and inventory weight should not always dominate the HUD.

Gold should appear when:

- Inventory is open.
- Money pouch is open.
- A shop, bank, auction, trade, bribe, contract, or payment screen is open.
- The player gains or loses gold.
- A quest reward or fee is being shown.

Inventory warnings should appear only when:

- The player is overweight.
- A pouch is full.
- A key container is missing.
- A tool breaks.
- A needed quest item is missing.
- A dangerous item is leaking, cursed, unstable, stolen, or illegal.

#### Crime, Stealth, and Danger Alerts

Crime and stealth information should appear only when the player is at risk of being noticed, caught, hunted, or punished.

Examples:

- Trespassing warning
- Theft witness risk
- Guard suspicion
- Wanted level
- Active bounty
- Illegal item discovered
- Contraband scan
- Stealth visibility
- Noise level
- Disguise risk
- Magical tracking

This keeps crime gameplay tense without making honest play feel crowded.

#### Minimap Core Display

The minimap should show:

- Player position
- Player facing direction
- Nearby roads
- Nearby paths
- Bridges
- City gates
- Building outlines
- Important buildings
- Important areas
- Merchant stalls
- Active merchants
- Quest marker location
- Active quest route, when auto-follow is enabled
- North, South, East, and West markers

The cardinal markers should always make it clear where North, South, East, and West are. This helps players navigate Eldoria, wilderness roads, ruins, dungeons, and player-built settlements without constantly opening the full map.

#### Battle Fog and Discovery

Huwan should use battle fog for minimaps and full maps.

If a place has not been discovered, mapped, purchased, shared, or revealed, it should not appear clearly on the player's map. The world should feel unknown until the player earns knowledge of it.

Battle fog rules:

- Undiscovered roads are hidden or shown only as vague terrain.
- Undiscovered buildings do not show their exact function.
- Undiscovered stalls and merchants do not appear as service icons.
- Undiscovered dungeon entrances do not appear unless revealed by rumor, quest, map, guild data, or direct discovery.
- Undiscovered danger zones do not show threat level.
- Hidden routes remain hidden even after the surrounding area is mapped.
- Secret locations require special discovery, purchased information, guild access, theft, or relationship trust.
- Entering an area clears the local battle fog around the player.
- Climbing towers, using scouting skills, flying, scrying, or hiring scouts can clear larger areas.

Maps should become valuable trade goods.

NPCs and players can sell maps that clear battle fog. A purchased map does not need to reveal everything perfectly. Its accuracy should depend on who made it, how old it is, and whether the seller is honest.

Map item types:

- Crude sketch maps
- Local street maps
- Merchant route maps
- Guild-certified maps
- Noble estate maps
- Dungeon floor maps
- Thieves Guild tunnel maps
- Resource survey maps
- Wilderness scout maps
- Dragon-born dungeon maps
- Fake maps
- Outdated maps

Map quality should matter.

Possible map qualities:

- Poor: reveals rough shape, may contain mistakes.
- Common: reveals roads, major buildings, and public landmarks.
- Good: reveals stalls, merchants, service buildings, and safe routes.
- Excellent: reveals danger zones, resource areas, patrol routes, and hidden approaches.
- Guild-certified: highly accurate within its stated area.
- Stolen or secret: may reveal hidden routes, noble passages, vaults, or illegal markets.
- Fake: clears false information and can lead the player into danger.

This creates jobs and gameplay for cartographers, scouts, merchants, thieves, dungeon explorers, guild officers, and players who specialize in selling knowledge.

#### Important Area Icons

Important areas should appear on the minimap once discovered, revealed by a quest, purchased from a map seller, or granted by a guild.

Common important area types:

- Guild halls
- Banks
- Stalls
- Merchants
- Inns
- Taverns
- Churches
- Guard stations
- Armories
- Crafting stations
- Farms
- Resource fields
- Dungeon entrances
- Quest zones
- Market boards
- Auction houses
- Stables
- Gates
- Safe houses
- Known danger zones

Hidden locations should not automatically appear. Thieves Guild routes, secret guild entrances, illegal markets, hidden tunnels, disguised cult sites, and secret noble passages should only show when the player earns, buys, discovers, steals, or is given that information.

#### Quest Marker Logic

The active quest marker should show the player's current objective location.

Quest marker behavior:

- A tracked quest places a marker on the minimap.
- If the target is nearby, the minimap shows the exact marker.
- If the target is far away, the minimap edge points toward the objective.
- If auto-follow is enabled, roads and safe routes may highlight.
- If the quest giver does not know the exact location, the marker can show a search area instead of a precise point.
- If the quest is secret, criminal, or uncertain, the marker can be vague or disabled until the player gathers more information.

This lets quests guide players without spoiling secrets.

#### Minimap Automation

Most minimap details should be automated by the map and city generation systems.

The generator should create:

- Road data
- Building data
- Stall data
- Merchant location data
- Important area tags
- Quest marker anchors
- District boundaries
- Discovery states
- Battle fog reveal states
- Map item reveal data
- Hidden route flags
- Danger zone tags
- Map icon categories
- Player-owned location markers
- NPC service markers
- Resource marker availability

When Eldoria changes, the minimap should update with it. If a stall closes, a merchant moves, a road is blocked, a building burns down, a guild expands, or a player-built structure appears, the minimap data should eventually reflect that change.

#### Full Map Relationship

The minimap should show immediate local information. The full map should show broader district, wilderness, region, and continent information.

The full map can include:

- District names
- Long-distance roads
- Major landmarks
- Discovered biomes
- Known dungeon locations
- Trade routes
- Guild territories
- Noble house influence
- Player-owned property
- Party member locations
- Known danger levels
- Quest chains

The minimap and full map should share the same world data, but the minimap should stay clean enough to read during combat, travel, and city movement.

### Player Menus and Management Screens

Huwan's menus should be mostly automated from the player's live data, but the most important screens need enough detail that players can understand their character, trade, crafting, and long-term progression without needing outside guides.

Menu design rule:

- Simple menus can be generated automatically from world data.
- Important menus need detailed breakdowns, tooltips, filters, comparisons, and progression hints.
- Menus should explain what the player already knows, not reveal secrets they have not discovered.
- Hidden information can become visible through skills, abilities, guild rank, relationships, purchased knowledge, maps, divination, research, or story progress.

#### Core Menu List

The game should eventually include these menu screens:

- Inventory
- Character sheet
- Equipment
- Status
- Skills
- Abilities
- Classes
- Jobs
- Crafting
- Spellbook
- Rune book
- Quest journal
- Map
- Relationships
- Guilds
- Factions
- Market and auction
- Bank and storage
- Companions and tamed creatures
- Property and housing
- Player settlements
- Codex
- Bestiary
- Lore archive
- Settings
- Save and load
- Moderator tools for Laucian/admin characters

Automated menus should pull from the same data used by NPC simulation, inventory, economy, quest generation, guild systems, and world memory. If an item, NPC, quest, map marker, skill, or market price changes in the world, the related menu should update naturally.

#### Inventory Menu

The inventory menu should be one of the most detailed menus in the game. Players need to understand what they own, where it is stored, what it is worth, whether it is legal, whether it is useful, and what it can become.

Inventory layout:

- Character equipment panel
- Backpack panel
- Pouch panel
- Magical storage panel, when unlocked
- Nearby container panel, when looting or storing
- Item detail panel
- Item comparison panel
- Quick action buttons
- Search bar
- Sort controls
- Filter tabs

Inventory filter tabs:

- All
- Equipped
- Weapons
- Armor
- Clothing
- Tools
- Materials
- Food and drink
- Potions
- Alchemy
- Magic items
- Runes
- Relics
- Maps
- Books and documents
- Quest items
- Stolen items
- Illegal items
- Favorites
- Junk

Each item should be able to display:

- Name
- Icon
- Item type
- Equipment slot, if any
- Rarity
- Rank
- Item level
- Quality
- Durability
- Weight
- Stack count
- Maximum stack size
- Value
- Estimated local market value
- Owner, if known
- Stolen status
- Illegal status
- Contraband risk
- Freshness or spoilage, if food or organic material
- Enchantments
- Rune effects
- Curses
- Blessings
- Class requirements
- Job requirements
- Race or sub-race requirements
- Skill requirements
- Ability interactions
- Crafting uses
- Salvage output
- Repair materials
- Known source
- Lore note, if discovered

Inventory actions:

- Use
- Equip
- Unequip
- Compare
- Inspect
- Split stack
- Move to pouch
- Move to backpack
- Move to magical storage
- Transfer to container
- Favorite
- Mark as junk
- Lock item
- Drop
- Sell
- Trade
- Gift
- Repair
- Dismantle
- Salvage
- Refine
- Cook
- Consume
- Read
- Study
- Learn recipe
- Track material
- Pin to crafting list

Pouches should matter. A basic backpack may hold only 5 pouches, while better backpacks can hold more. The inventory menu should clearly show which pouch contains which items, because alchemy pouches, material pouches, money pouches, component pouches, jewel pouches, and gathering pouches all affect player flow.

Inventory warnings should be clear but not noisy.

Important warnings:

- Overweight
- Pouch full
- Backpack full
- Quest item missing
- Tool missing
- Tool broken
- Food spoiled
- Potion cracked
- Cursed item equipped
- Stolen item visible
- Illegal item visible
- Unstable magic item
- Mana-leaking item
- Item incompatible with current class, job, race, or skill

The inventory menu should also support player-created gear, because blacksmiths, tailors, alchemists, enchanters, cooks, builders, and crafters need to understand the value of their work.

Player-created items should show:

- Creator name
- Crafting station used
- Materials used
- Quality roll
- Skill contribution
- Ability contribution
- Enchantment source
- Rune source
- Durability bonus or penalty
- Market history, if sold before

#### Character Sheet

The character sheet should explain who the player is, what they are becoming, and why their choices matter.

Character sheet sections:

- Identity
- Appearance summary
- Race and sub-race
- Birth traits
- Bloodline traits
- Alignment
- Reputation
- Titles
- Current classes
- Current jobs
- Stats
- Derived stats
- Survival needs
- Social needs
- Mana well
- Relationship summary
- Guild standing
- Faction standing
- Crime and bounty status
- Blessings and curses
- World Favor
- World Popularity
- Demi-God path progress, if discovered

Main stats:

- Strength
- Charisma
- Intelligence
- Wisdom
- Dexterity
- Luck
- Health
- Mana
- Defense
- World Popularity, hidden until discovered

Derived stats can include:

- Carry capacity
- Physical damage
- Magical damage
- Healing strength
- Crafting precision
- Forging power
- Alchemy stability
- Stealth rating
- Social influence
- Trade influence
- Movement speed
- Dodge chance
- Block strength
- Critical chance
- Mana recovery
- Hunger rate
- Thirst rate
- Disease resistance
- Poison resistance
- Fear resistance
- Charm resistance

The character sheet should show progression without spoiling everything.

Progression hints:

- Next stat growth opportunity
- Skills close to leveling
- Abilities close to unlocking
- Class unlock hints
- Job unlock hints
- Guild requirement hints
- Relationship consequences
- Reputation changes
- Hidden path hints, only after clues are discovered

For example, the Demi-God path should not be fully visible at character creation. It should begin as strange achievements, rumors, old lore, divine approval fragments, and impossible-looking progress bars until the player understands what they are chasing.

#### Skills Menu

The skills menu should show what the player has learned through action, training, race, class, job, guilds, and character creation.

Skills should be divided into categories.

Skill categories:

- Combat
- Weapon
- Armor
- Magic
- Rune
- Crafting
- Gathering
- Survival
- Social
- Leadership
- Trade
- Stealth
- Crime
- Faith
- Nature
- Taming
- Dungeon
- Exploration
- Scholarly
- Monster processing
- Relationship

Each skill should show:

- Skill name
- Skill category
- Current rank or level
- Experience toward next rank
- How it improves
- Main stat used
- Secondary stats used
- Related class
- Related job
- Related guild
- Trainer sources, if known
- Practice methods
- Unlock requirements
- Known bonuses
- Hidden bonuses, if discovered
- Ability synergy
- Item synergy
- Crafting recipe unlocks, if any

Character creation skills should be marked as permanent. They cannot be unlearned and should remain part of the character identity forever.

The skills menu should help players understand cause and effect. If using a bow raises bow handling, dexterity, hunting, stealth, and archery class progress, the menu should make that relationship visible after the player learns it.

#### Abilities Menu

Abilities are more identity-defining than normal skills. They can come from birth, race, sub-race, class, job, training, divine blessing, curse, monster evolution, reincarnation, relics, relationships, or rare events.

Ability categories:

- Birth ability
- Race ability
- Bloodline ability
- Class ability
- Job ability
- Guild ability
- Divine blessing
- Curse
- Unique ability
- Master ability
- Monster ability
- Tamed bond ability
- Reincarnated ability
- Relic ability
- Relationship ability

Each ability should show:

- Ability name
- Ability category
- Passive or active type
- Current level or mastery
- Mana cost, stamina cost, health cost, or special cost
- Cooldown
- Requirements
- Scaling stats
- Known effects
- Hidden effects, if discovered
- Risks
- Limitations
- Upgrade path
- Source
- Whether it can be unlearned
- Whether it is permanent

Starting abilities chosen at character creation should be clearly marked as permanent.

The menu should also explain dangerous abilities. For example, Infinity should explain that an infinite mana well does not make the caster all-powerful, and that poor control, spell overload, or mana well damage can still be catastrophic.

Abilities should be searchable and filterable by use:

- Combat
- Survival
- Crafting
- Trade
- Magic
- Social
- Stealth
- Taming
- Leadership
- Travel
- World impact

#### Crafting Menu

The crafting menu should be heavily detailed because players can create weapons, armor, clothes, tools, potions, food, buildings, furniture, maps, runes, and magical gear.

Crafting screen layout:

- Recipe list
- Material inventory
- Required tools
- Required station
- Required skill
- Required job or class
- Output preview
- Quality forecast
- Failure risk
- Durability forecast
- Market value estimate
- Crafting action button
- Batch crafting controls
- Experimentation controls, if unlocked

Recipe categories:

- Weapons
- Armor
- Clothing
- Tools
- Food
- Potions
- Alchemy
- Runes
- Enchantments
- Furniture
- Building parts
- Containers
- Bags and pouches
- Maps
- Traps
- Ammunition
- Jewelry
- Relics, if discovered and allowed

Each recipe should show:

- Required materials
- Optional materials
- Required tools
- Required crafting station
- Required skill level
- Required job rank
- Required class, if any
- Required ability, if any
- Main stat
- Secondary stat
- Time required
- Expected quality
- Failure chance
- Resource loss on failure
- Possible upgrades
- Possible variants
- Known uses
- Market demand
- Guild demand
- NPC demand

Crafting should support basic recipes and player experimentation.

Examples:

- Iron ore can be smelted into iron bars.
- One iron bar and one basic wood can become a short sword.
- Two iron bars and one basic wood can become a longsword.
- Three basic wood and one iron bar can become a shield.
- Flax can become bowstring.
- Five basic wood and bowstring can become a basic bow.
- Two stone and one wood can become arrows.
- Fiber and hide can become quivers.
- Tanned hides can become leather armor pieces.
- Glass can become potion containers.

Crafting quality should depend on:

- Material quality
- Tool quality
- Station quality
- Skill level
- Job rank
- Relevant stat
- Ability bonuses
- Recipe familiarity
- Environmental conditions
- Time spent

The crafting menu should also support NPC and market demand. If Eldoria needs arrows, shoes, bread, potions, stone blocks, or swords, the crafting menu can show local demand after the player learns it through the market, Merchants Guild, town board, or NPC requests.

#### Market and Auction Menu

The market and auction menu should teach the player how trade works in Huwan.

Trade is not just buying and selling. It connects NPC needs, resource supply, guild contracts, merchant stalls, noble politics, player crafting, dungeon loot, map sales, shortages, bribes, and Shaohr's divine finance rules.

Market screen sections:

- Local market listings
- Merchant stalls
- Player listings
- Auction listings
- Buy orders
- Sell orders
- Trade history
- Price trends
- Local demand
- Local supply
- Taxes and fees
- Guild discounts
- Job discounts
- Reputation modifiers
- Relationship modifiers
- Legality warnings
- Stolen goods risk
- Shaohr pact option, when available

Each listing should show:

- Item name
- Item type
- Quantity
- Quality
- Condition
- Seller
- Seller reputation
- Asking price
- Estimated fair price
- Recent average price
- Demand level
- Supply level
- Location
- Delivery requirement, if any
- Tax or fee
- Legal status
- Auction time remaining, if auctioned
- Bid history, if available

Market filters:

- Weapons
- Armor
- Clothing
- Tools
- Materials
- Food
- Potions
- Alchemy
- Magic
- Runes
- Relics
- Maps
- Animals
- Tamed monsters
- Property
- Contracts
- Services
- Jobs
- Bulk goods
- Stolen goods, if the player has access
- Black market, if the player has access

Prices should react to:

- Local supply
- Local demand
- NPC inventory
- Player purchases
- Player sales
- Quest demand
- Monster population
- Dungeon loot flow
- Farm output
- Weather
- War
- bandit activity
- Guild contracts
- Noble taxes
- Merchant Guild policy
- Relationship with seller
- Reputation
- Job rank
- Merchant skill
- Shaohr worship or pact use

Market actions:

- Buy
- Sell
- Bid
- List auction
- Cancel listing
- Place buy order
- Place sell order
- Compare prices
- Negotiate
- Bundle trade
- Request delivery
- Hire caravan
- Insure shipment
- Bribe
- Use Shaohr's symbol for honorable trade pact
- Use double-headed coin for risky bribery or trickery, if available

The market should help honest merchants, clever traders, thieves, crafters, nobles, and guild workers all play differently.

Stolen goods should not behave like normal goods. Selling stolen goods through public stalls should be risky. Selling through fences, the Thieves Guild, corrupt merchants, or black markets should be easier but may reduce value, increase debt, create leverage, or build criminal reputation.

Auction houses should support rare items:

- Relics
- Legendary materials
- Dragon parts
- Dungeon maps
- Noble contracts
- Guild-certified gear
- Player-created masterwork gear
- Tamed monsters
- Magical storage items
- Rare books
- Unique runes

The market and auction menu should make trade readable, but not perfectly safe. A player should be able to become wealthy through trade, but bad deals, fake maps, stolen goods, bribes, taxes, and dishonest sellers should keep the economy alive.

#### Other Menus

Other menus can be automated with simpler rules until they need deeper design.

Quest journal:

- Active quests
- Completed quests
- Failed quests
- Expired quests
- Guild quests
- Personal quests
- Hidden quests, once discovered
- Quest rewards
- Quest timers
- Quest relationships
- Quest morality consequences

Relationship menu:

- Known NPCs
- Trust
- Fear
- Respect
- Love
- Loyalty
- Rivalry
- Hatred
- Debt
- Suspicion
- Admiration
- Shame
- Gratitude
- Obligation
- Slave
- Bonded
- Soul mate
- Tamed
- Controlled

Guild menu:

- Guild membership
- Guild rank
- Available quests
- Guild reputation
- Guild benefits
- Guild fees
- Guild enemies
- Guild laws
- Guild discounts
- Promotion requirements

Classes and jobs menu:

- Current classes
- Current jobs
- Class levels
- Job ranks
- Unlock hints
- Requirements
- Salary
- Discounts
- Benefits
- Conflicts
- Hidden ranks, once discovered

Spellbook and rune book:

- Known spells
- Known runes
- Spell categories
- Magic schools
- Mana costs
- Component needs
- Framework notes
- Rune geometry notes
- Legal risk through misuse
- Casting requirements
- Spell mastery

Map menu:

- Full map
- Battle fog
- Purchased maps
- Discovered locations
- Quest markers
- Player markers
- Guild markers
- Danger zones
- Trade routes
- Hidden routes, if known

Codex, bestiary, and lore archive:

- Known creatures
- Known races
- Known sub-races
- Known deities
- Known artifacts
- Known factions
- Known laws
- Known crafting materials
- Known monster drops
- Known edible and inedible parts
- Known dungeon rules

The simple version of every menu can come first. The detailed version should grow as the systems become playable.

### Automated Data Templates

The menus should be powered by reusable data templates. These templates are the foundation for inventory, character sheets, skills, abilities, crafting, markets, auctions, NPC simulation, quest generation, and Unreal Engine implementation.

The goal is to avoid writing every menu by hand. If the data is structured correctly, the UI can build itself from the player's current character, known information, items, skills, recipes, market listings, and world state.

#### Template Design Rules

Every important data object should have:

- A stable ID
- A display name
- A category
- Tags
- Discovery state
- Visibility rules
- Ownership rules, if relevant
- Source history, if relevant
- UI display rules
- Economy rules, if relevant
- Save/load rules
- Multiplayer/server authority rules

Discovery matters. The player should not automatically know every detail about an item, skill, NPC, relic, monster part, dungeon, recipe, or market listing.

Discovery states:

- Unknown
- Rumored
- Seen
- Inspected
- Identified
- Studied
- Mastered
- Secret
- False information

Visibility rules should decide what the player sees. A common sword may show almost everything immediately, while a relic, cursed ring, fake map, hidden skill, or divine contract may hide information until identified.

#### Character Profile Template

The character profile template should be used by players, fully simulated NPCs, important NPCs, companions, tamed creatures, and monsters that require deeper simulation.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Character ID | Stable saved identity. |
| Display name | Name shown to the player, if known. |
| True name | Hidden name for secret identities, divine contracts, or disguise systems. |
| Character type | Player, NPC, companion, tamed creature, monster, boss, admin character, deity projection. |
| Race | Major race or special lineage. |
| Sub-race | Sub-race, bloodline, or evolved form. |
| Age | Current age. |
| Lifespan data | Expected lifespan, aging speed, immortality flags, undeath flags. |
| Sex and body data | Used for character identity and cosmetic appearance. |
| Appearance data | Body, hair, skin, eyes, horns, ears, tail, wings, scars, markings, clothing preview. |
| Birth traits | Traits gained at creation, birth, reincarnation, or bloodline. |
| Alignment | Good, evil, neutral, or more detailed moral profile. |
| Reputation | Public reputation values by city, guild, faction, race, and server. |
| Titles | Earned titles, noble titles, guild titles, criminal names, divine names. |
| Classes | Up to 3 active classes and their levels. |
| Jobs | Up to 5 active jobs and their ranks. |
| Stats | Core stat template values. |
| Derived stats | Calculated values such as carry weight, damage, resistance, trade influence, and movement. |
| Needs | Hunger, thirst, fatigue, social level, and other survival values. |
| Mana well | Depth, recovery, stability, damage risk, overload risk, and special modifiers. |
| Skills | Learned skills, permanent character creation skills, hidden skills, skill experience. |
| Abilities | Active, passive, permanent, temporary, hidden, unique, divine, curse, and bloodline abilities. |
| Inventory | Equipped gear, backpack, pouches, storage, money pouch, magical storage, containers. |
| Wealth | Gold, bank funds, property value, debt, credit, contracts, investments. |
| Relationships | Relationship values toward known characters, groups, pets, patrons, and tamed creatures. |
| Guild standing | Guild membership, rank, fees, benefits, restrictions, and quests. |
| Faction standing | Faction reputation, loyalty, enemies, and ideology pressure. |
| Crime data | Wanted status, bounties, crimes, evidence, witnesses, fines, imprisonment risk. |
| Blessings and curses | Divine, magical, bloodline, relic, relationship, or curse effects. |
| Memory | Important memories, player interactions, promises, betrayals, debts, rumors, and events. |
| AI routine | Daily schedule, goals, wants, needs, job tasks, and simulation depth. |
| Quest hooks | Quests this character can offer, trigger, progress, fail, or resolve. |
| World Favor | Server/world approval and hidden heroic significance. |
| World Popularity | Hidden or revealed server fame and long-term achievement value. |
| Admin permissions | Special controls for Laucian or moderator characters only. |

#### Character Stat Template

Each stat should be its own data entry so the character sheet, progression system, class unlocks, job unlocks, abilities, and crafting formulas can all read the same values.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Stat ID | Stable stat identifier. |
| Display name | Player-facing name. |
| Category | Physical, mental, social, survival, magical, hidden, divine, economy. |
| Description | Plain-language explanation. |
| Is visible | Whether the stat appears by default. |
| Is secret | Whether it must be discovered. |
| Minimum value | Lowest possible value. |
| Maximum value | Highest possible value, usually 999 for major stats. |
| Base value | Starting value before modifiers. |
| Current value | Current effective value. |
| Permanent modifiers | Race, sub-race, birth, reincarnation, class, job, or deity changes. |
| Temporary modifiers | Buffs, debuffs, injuries, equipment, hunger, thirst, curses, blessings. |
| Growth actions | Actions that train the stat. |
| Growth skills | Skills that affect or increase the stat. |
| Growth abilities | Abilities that affect or increase the stat. |
| Unlock links | Classes, jobs, skills, abilities, quests, or ranks unlocked by this stat. |
| Derived outputs | Values calculated from this stat. |
| Decay rules | Whether the stat can decay, weaken, or be damaged. |
| UI priority | Where and when the stat is shown. |

Main stat IDs:

- Strength
- Charisma
- Intelligence
- Wisdom
- Dexterity
- Luck
- Health
- Mana
- Defense
- World Popularity

Example stat automation:

- Strength affects forging, melee damage, carry weight, heavy weapons, shields, and intimidation.
- Dexterity affects crafting, archery, knives, dodging, lockpicking, sewing, and fine manipulation.
- Intelligence affects spellcraft, wizard frameworks, finework, research, engineering, and rune study.
- Wisdom affects alchemy, priest discipline, druid awareness, survival, and safe magical judgment.
- Charisma affects leadership, trade, persuasion, relationships, fame, guild influence, and recruiting.
- Luck affects rare drops, critical fortune, gambling, discovery, odd events, and strange divine timing.

#### Item Template

Items should be split into base item data and item instance data.

Base item data describes what an item is. Item instance data describes this exact copy of the item in the world.

Base item fields:

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Item ID | Stable base item identifier. |
| Display name | Player-facing name. |
| Item type | Weapon, armor, clothing, tool, material, food, potion, map, book, relic, etc. |
| Subtype | Sword, bow, robe, herb, ore, hide, potion bottle, dungeon map, and so on. |
| Tags | Search, filtering, crafting, economy, legality, and UI tags. |
| Description | Normal player-facing description. |
| Hidden description | Secret or identified-only description. |
| Rarity | Common, uncommon, rare, epic, legendary, divine, relic, unique, etc. |
| Rank | F, E, D, C, B, A, S, SS, SSS if allowed. |
| Item level | Mechanical level or scaling tier. |
| Quality range | Poor through masterwork or better. |
| Max durability | Highest durability for a fresh version. |
| Base weight | Weight per item. |
| Stackable | Whether it stacks. |
| Max stack | Stack limit, usually 99 for many items. |
| Equipment slot | Head, chest, hand, ring, pouch, backpack, magical storage, etc. |
| Stat modifiers | Strength, defense, mana, dexterity, etc. |
| Damage data | Damage type, base damage, scaling, attack speed, range. |
| Defense data | Armor value, resistances, coverage, movement penalties. |
| Tool data | Supported gathering or crafting actions. |
| Food data | Hunger, thirst, freshness, spoilage, poison risk. |
| Magic data | Mana cost, rune slots, enchant capacity, curse risk, spell storage. |
| Requirements | Race, class, job, skill, ability, level, rank, guild, or relationship needs. |
| Legal status | Legal, restricted, illegal, contraband, stolen-only, guild-controlled. |
| Crafting uses | Recipes that consume or require it. |
| Salvage output | Materials gained by dismantling or processing. |
| Repair materials | Materials and skills needed to repair it. |
| Economy data | Base value, demand category, supply category, price volatility. |
| Source data | Drop, gather, craft, merchant, dungeon, relic, quest, divine, unique. |
| Lore data | Lore note, known history, deity relation, faction relation. |
| UI icon | Icon reference. |
| Visual asset | Unreal asset reference later. |

Item instance fields:

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Instance ID | Unique saved copy of the item. |
| Base item ID | Links to base item data. |
| Current owner | Player, NPC, guild, store, chest, monster, dungeon, bank, unknown. |
| Legal owner | Who the law thinks owns it. |
| Creator | Player or NPC who crafted it. |
| Creation method | Crafted, looted, bought, stolen, summoned, rewarded, inherited, divine. |
| Creation location | Where the item entered the world. |
| Current durability | Current durability. |
| Current quality | This copy's quality. |
| Current stack count | Stack amount. |
| Enchantments | Active enchantments. |
| Runes | Active or dormant runes. |
| Curses | Known and hidden curses. |
| Blessings | Known and hidden blessings. |
| Freshness | Food, herbs, monster parts, and organic material decay. |
| Stolen flag | Whether it is stolen. |
| Illegal flag | Whether it is illegal in current region. |
| Contraband risk | Chance guards or scans care about it. |
| Storage location | Equipped, backpack, pouch, bank, container, market, auction, world. |
| Price history | Last bought, last sold, average sale, market notes. |
| Discovery state | Unknown, inspected, identified, studied, mastered. |

Starter example:

| Field | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Item ID | weapon_basic_short_sword |
| Display name | Basic Short Sword |
| Item type | Weapon |
| Subtype | Sword |
| Rank | F |
| Materials | 1 iron bar, 1 basic wood |
| Skill requirement | Smithing level 1 |
| Main stat | Strength |
| Secondary stat | Dexterity |
| Base use | Starter melee weapon |

#### Skill Template

Skills should describe learned practice. They improve through action, training, study, guild work, class progression, job progression, and special events.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Skill ID | Stable skill identifier. |
| Display name | Player-facing name. |
| Category | Combat, weapon, crafting, gathering, magic, rune, social, trade, stealth, etc. |
| Description | What the skill means. |
| Discovery state | Whether the player knows the skill exists. |
| Current level | Current skill level or rank. |
| Experience | Progress toward next level. |
| Rank name | Beginner, apprentice, skilled, expert, master, etc. |
| Main stat | Primary stat that supports the skill. |
| Secondary stats | Other helpful stats. |
| Growth actions | Actions that increase the skill. |
| Training sources | NPC trainers, guilds, books, practice tools, schools. |
| Related classes | Classes that use this skill. |
| Related jobs | Jobs that use this skill. |
| Related guilds | Guilds that teach, require, or reward this skill. |
| Unlock requirements | What unlocks the skill. |
| Unlock rewards | Recipes, abilities, actions, dialogue, quests, or equipment use unlocked. |
| Passive bonuses | Ongoing bonuses from having the skill. |
| Active actions | Actions enabled by the skill. |
| Failure rules | What happens when skill is too low. |
| Hidden effects | Secret bonuses revealed through mastery, lore, or trainers. |
| Permanent flag | Whether it is one of the 5 character creation skills. |
| UI priority | Whether it appears in main skill list, hidden list, or context prompt. |

Example:

| Field | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Skill ID | skill_smithing |
| Display name | Smithing |
| Category | Crafting |
| Main stat | Strength |
| Secondary stat | Dexterity |
| Growth actions | Smelting, forging, repairing, sharpening, dismantling metal gear. |
| Unlock rewards | Basic weapons, shields, repair quality, metal refinement. |

#### Ability Template

Abilities are identity-defining powers, traits, special actions, or supernatural rules. They can be passive, active, permanent, temporary, inherited, learned, granted, cursed, or awakened.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Ability ID | Stable ability identifier. |
| Display name | Player-facing name. |
| Category | Birth, race, bloodline, class, job, guild, divine, curse, unique, master, relic, relationship. |
| Ability type | Passive, active, toggle, aura, reaction, hidden, transformation, contract. |
| Description | Known effect. |
| Hidden description | Secret effect or advanced explanation. |
| Discovery state | Whether the player knows it exists or understands it. |
| Source | Race, sub-race, class, job, deity, relic, contract, reincarnation, etc. |
| Permanent flag | Whether it can ever be removed. |
| Can be unlearned | Whether the player can replace or lose it. |
| Current level | Current rank or mastery. |
| Mastery progress | Practice or use progress. |
| Cost type | Mana, stamina, health, gold, item, relationship, soul, cooldown, none. |
| Cost amount | Amount paid to use it. |
| Cooldown | Time before reuse. |
| Requirements | Stat, class, job, skill, race, guild, patron, relationship, item. |
| Scaling stats | Stats that improve the effect. |
| Active effects | What happens when used. |
| Passive effects | Ongoing effects. |
| Risks | Mana overload, social penalty, legal risk, curse, exhaustion, patron anger. |
| Limitations | Range, target rules, environment, cooldown, oath, law, material needs. |
| Upgrade path | How it grows or evolves. |
| Synergy | Skills, spells, items, relationships, or jobs that interact with it. |
| UI warning | Whether the ability needs a warning before use. |

Example:

| Field | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Ability ID | ability_born_leader |
| Display name | Born Leader |
| Category | Birth ability |
| Ability type | Passive |
| Source | Character creation or rare birth trait |
| Main effect | Builds Charisma faster with allies and improves group morale. |
| Permanent flag | Yes, if chosen at character creation. |

Unique ability example:

| Field | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Ability ID | ability_infinity |
| Display name | Infinity |
| Category | Unique ability |
| Ability type | Active/permanent modifier |
| Source | Extremely rare Matrena-linked anomaly |
| Main effect | Can make a mana well infinite. |
| Risk | Does not grant control; overload, spell chaos, and self-destruction risk still exist. |

#### Recipe Template

Recipes should power crafting, NPC production, market demand, guild requests, and quest generation.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Recipe ID | Stable recipe identifier. |
| Display name | Player-facing recipe name. |
| Recipe category | Weapon, armor, tool, food, potion, rune, enchantment, map, building, etc. |
| Output item ID | Item created. |
| Output quantity | How many items are produced. |
| Required materials | Materials consumed. |
| Optional materials | Materials that improve, alter, or flavor the output. |
| Required tools | Tools needed. |
| Required station | Furnace, anvil, sewing bench, alchemy table, campfire, rune table, etc. |
| Required skill | Skill and level required. |
| Required job | Job and rank required, if any. |
| Required class | Class required, if any. |
| Required ability | Ability required, if any. |
| Main stat | Primary stat used. |
| Secondary stats | Supporting stats. |
| Craft time | Time required. |
| Batch support | Whether multiple can be crafted at once. |
| Failure chance | Base chance of failure. |
| Failure output | What is lost or produced on failure. |
| Quality formula | What improves quality. |
| Durability formula | What improves durability. |
| Variant rules | Ways the recipe can create variants. |
| Experimentation rules | How unknown variants can be discovered. |
| XP rewards | Skills, jobs, classes, and stats improved. |
| Market demand category | How economy systems classify demand. |
| Guild demand category | Guilds that may request it. |
| NPC demand category | NPCs likely to request or buy it. |
| Discovery source | How the recipe is learned. |
| UI warnings | Missing tool, missing station, low skill, illegal output, unstable magic, dangerous failure. |

Starter recipe examples:

| Recipe | Materials | Requirement | Output |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Basic Short Sword | 1 iron bar, 1 basic wood | Smithing level 1, furnace, anvil | Basic Short Sword |
| Basic Longsword | 2 iron bars, 1 basic wood | Smithing level 1, furnace, anvil | Basic Longsword |
| Basic Shield | 3 basic wood, 1 iron bar | Smithing level 1 or Woodworking level 1 | Basic Shield |
| Basic Bow | 5 basic wood, flax bowstring | Woodworking level 1 | Basic Bow |
| Basic Arrows | 2 stone, 1 basic wood | Crafting level 1 | Basic Arrows |
| Basic Quiver | 5 grass fiber, 1 small hide | Sewing level 1 | Basic Quiver |
| Basic Pouch | 1 small hide | Sewing level 1 | Basic Pouch |
| Basic Chest | 10 basic wood | Woodworking level 1 | Basic Chest |
| Waterskin | 1 animal bladder, 1 small hide | Tanning level 1 | Waterskin |
| Potion Container | 1 glass | Crafting level 1 | Potion Container |

#### Market Listing Template

Market listings should connect items, NPC needs, player trade, auctions, theft, taxes, guild discounts, and economy simulation.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Listing ID | Stable market listing identifier. |
| Listing type | Sale, auction, buy order, sell order, contract, service, black market, bribe offer. |
| Item instance ID | Exact item being sold, if unique. |
| Base item ID | Base item being requested or sold. |
| Quantity | Amount offered or requested. |
| Quality | Quality requirement or quality of item sold. |
| Condition | Durability or freshness. |
| Seller ID | NPC, player, guild, stall, auction house, fence, black market. |
| Buyer ID | Buyer if reserved, contracted, or buy order. |
| Location | Stall, market, city, guild, auction house, route, black market, dungeon camp. |
| Asking price | Seller price. |
| Minimum price | Lowest acceptable price, if negotiable. |
| Estimated fair price | System estimate based on supply/demand. |
| Recent average price | Local or regional trade history. |
| Demand level | None, low, normal, high, urgent, crisis. |
| Supply level | Scarce, low, normal, high, flooded. |
| Tax rate | Local tax or auction fee. |
| Guild discount | Discount from job, guild rank, or faction standing. |
| Relationship modifier | Trust, fear, debt, love, rivalry, hatred, or obligation effect. |
| Reputation modifier | Local reputation effect. |
| Legality | Legal, restricted, illegal, stolen, contraband, forged, cursed. |
| Stolen risk | Risk of guard reaction or owner discovery. |
| Delivery requirement | Whether transport is needed. |
| Delivery risk | Bandits, weather, monsters, thieves, political interference. |
| Auction timer | Time remaining, if auction. |
| Bid history | Current and past bids. |
| Shaohr pact flag | Whether the deal invokes Shaohr's symbol. |
| Double-headed coin flag | Whether bribery or trickery was used. |
| Expiration | When listing ends or demand changes. |
| Market event links | War, famine, dungeon loot, harvest, festival, tax change, bandit activity. |

Price automation inputs:

- Base value
- Local supply
- Local demand
- Item quality
- Item condition
- Season
- Weather
- War status
- Bandit activity
- Dungeon loot flow
- Farm output
- Monster population
- Noble taxes
- Merchant Guild policy
- NPC wealth
- Player reputation
- Seller relationship
- Job rank discounts
- Guild discounts
- Stolen or illegal risk
- Distance from source
- Delivery cost
- Shaohr pact bonus or penalty

#### Map Data Template

The map template should support minimaps, full maps, battle fog, purchased maps, fake maps, and discovery.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Map Data ID | Stable map region identifier. |
| Region name | City, district, road, biome, dungeon floor, route, property, or continent area. |
| Map type | Local, street, wilderness, dungeon, estate, route, resource, secret, fake. |
| Area bounds | World-space area covered. |
| Roads | Known roads and paths. |
| Buildings | Known buildings and public functions. |
| Important areas | Guild halls, stalls, banks, dungeons, farms, gates, danger zones. |
| Merchant markers | Active or known merchant locations. |
| Quest anchors | Quest objective marker points. |
| Resource markers | Wood, herbs, ore, fish, farms, monster parts, mana crystals if known. |
| Battle fog state | Hidden, vague, explored, mapped, fully known. |
| Hidden route flags | Secret paths, tunnels, noble passages, Thieves Guild routes. |
| Accuracy | Poor, common, good, excellent, guild-certified, secret, fake. |
| Age | How old the map data is. |
| Creator | Cartographer, guild, NPC, player, thief, liar, monster, magical source. |
| Seller honesty | Trustworthiness of the map seller. |
| Reveal rules | What the map clears and what remains hidden. |
| Update rules | Whether the map updates dynamically or becomes outdated. |

#### Status Effect Template

Status effects should drive HUD alerts, expanded status details, medicine, alchemy, healing, curses, law, and combat outcomes.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Status ID | Stable effect identifier. |
| Display name | Player-facing name. |
| Category | Buff, debuff, injury, disease, poison, curse, blessing, crime, social, hunger, thirst, magical. |
| Severity | Minor, warning, dangerous, critical. |
| Discovery state | Known, hidden, misidentified, unidentified. |
| Duration | Time remaining or permanent. |
| Stack behavior | Stacks, refreshes, intensifies, replaces, or conflicts. |
| Effects | Stat changes, damage over time, restrictions, UI alerts, relationship changes. |
| Cure methods | Potion, spell, priest, rest, surgery, food, water, antidote, quest, deity, time. |
| Worsening rules | What makes it worse. |
| Spread rules | Whether it spreads to others. |
| Death or failure rules | What happens if ignored. |
| HUD behavior | When and how loudly it appears. |

#### Menu Automation Flow

The menu system should follow a simple flow.

1. Read the player's character profile.
2. Read discovered and hidden data linked to the player.
3. Read inventory, equipment, pouches, storage, and nearby containers.
4. Read skills, abilities, classes, jobs, and stat growth.
5. Read local market, NPC demand, guild demand, and auction data.
6. Read battle fog, map markers, quest anchors, and discovery states.
7. Apply visibility rules.
8. Apply filters, sort rules, warnings, and tooltips.
9. Show detailed information only when known, discovered, identified, or earned.

This lets Huwan's menus grow automatically as the player learns the world.

#### Unreal Implementation Direction

When Huwan moves deeper into Unreal Engine, these templates can become:

- Data Tables
- Primary Data Assets
- Save game structs
- Replicated server state
- UI view models
- Economy simulation records
- Quest generation inputs
- NPC simulation records
- Market and auction records
- Map and battle fog records

The first prototype should not try to build every template at once. The clean starting point is:

1. Character profile template
2. Character stat template
3. Item base template
4. Item instance template
5. Skill template
6. Ability template
7. Recipe template
8. Market listing template
9. Map data template
10. Status effect template

### Starter Data Set V0.1

This starter data set turns the early-game ideas into structured seed data. It should be treated as the first playable database layer, not the final item list.

Default starter data rules:

- Most starter items are F Rank.
- Most starter items are Common rarity.
- Most starter items are legal in Eldoria.
- Most starter items are visible after tutorial, inspection, guild registration, or first pickup.
- Starter gear should be replaceable, repairable, craftable, and tradeable.
- Items made by players should become item instances with creator, quality, durability, and market history.
- Hidden traits, curses, relic properties, and unusual material effects should remain hidden until discovered.

Starter ID prefixes:

- Weapons: `item_weapon_basic_`
- Armor and clothing: `item_armor_basic_` or `item_clothing_basic_`
- Gear and accessories: `item_gear_basic_`
- Tools: `item_tool_`
- Materials: `mat_f_`
- Skills: `skill_`
- Abilities: `ability_`
- Recipes: `recipe_`

#### First Ability Seed List

At character creation, players choose 5 skills and 5 abilities. The ability pool should be filtered by race, sub-race, birth traits, bloodline, and discovered character options.

Universal starter abilities:

| Ability ID | Name | Type | Main Use | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ability_born_leader | Born Leader | Passive | Builds Charisma faster with allies and improves group morale. | Strong for leaders, officers, nobles, and guild paths. |
| ability_indomitable | Indomitable | Passive | Builds Defense faster under pressure and resists fear. | Strong for knights, guards, tanks, and stubborn survivors. |
| ability_keen_eye | Keen Eye | Passive | Improves spotting resources, traps, tracks, and map details. | Helps scouts, archers, thieves, gatherers, and dungeon explorers. |
| ability_quick_hands | Quick Hands | Passive | Improves crafting speed, lockpicking, tool use, and item handling. | Strong for crafters, thieves, and fletchers. |
| ability_steady_aim | Steady Aim | Passive/active | Improves ranged accuracy when calm or braced. | Strong for archers, hunters, and thrown weapons. |
| ability_iron_grip | Iron Grip | Passive | Improves heavy weapon control, carrying, climbing, and grappling. | Strength-friendly starter. |
| ability_field_medic | Field Medic | Passive/active | Improves stabilizing wounds and using healer's kits. | Strong for priests, druids, parties, and survivalists. |
| ability_streetwise | Streetwise | Passive | Improves rumor reading, poor district navigation, fences, and danger sense. | Useful in Eldoria's Low Lantern Ward and Ashdoor Lanes. |
| ability_silver_tongue | Silver Tongue | Passive | Improves persuasion, bargaining, apologies, and social recovery. | Strong for merchants, nobles, priests, and bards. |
| ability_gatherers_sense | Gatherer's Sense | Passive | Highlights likely herbs, fibers, wild food, and surface resources after discovery. | Strong for F-rank gathering. |
| ability_light_sleeper | Light Sleeper | Passive | Improves ambush detection while resting and camping. | Useful for wilderness and dungeon travel. |
| ability_iron_stomach | Iron Stomach | Passive | Reduces sickness risk from rough food and questionable meals. | Does not make poisonous food safe. |
| ability_minor_mana_sense | Minor Mana Sense | Passive | Lets the player feel obvious magic, runes, mana leaks, and enchanted items. | Entry point to magic discovery. |
| ability_lucky_find | Lucky Find | Passive | Slightly improves odds of small bonus finds while gathering or looting. | Luck-friendly starter. |
| ability_beast_calm | Beast Calm | Passive/active | Helps avoid panicking animals and improves early taming attempts. | Strong for beastfolk, druids, hunters, and farmers. |

| ability_mana_depth_minor | Minor Deep Well | Magic-leaning race or birth trait | Slightly deeper mana well before age-set rules matter. |
| ability_tusked_presence | Tusked Presence | Orc or compatible lineage | Intimidation and fear pressure in close social encounters. |
| ability_horn_sensitivity | Horn Sensitivity | Demon or horned lineage | Detects nearby mana pressure and emotional threat changes. |
| ability_animal_senses | Animal Senses | Beastfolk or compatible lineage | Better hearing, smell, and tracking. |
| ability_whimsical_step | Whimsical Step | Fae or compatible lineage | Slightly odd movement, misdirection, and dreamlike evasion. |
| ability_elven_focus | Elven Focus | Elf or compatible lineage | Better study, memory, spell learning, and knowledge tasks. |
| ability_dwarven_work_ethic | Dwarven Work Ethic | Dwarf or compatible lineage | Better labor stamina, crafting patience, and job progression. |
