| Recipe ID | Output | Inputs | Tool or Station | Skill | Main Stat |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| recipe_reinforced_basic_sword_1 | Reinforced Basic Sword Level 1 | Basic Sword, slime gel, small bones, field stone | Repair kit or hammer | Smithing 1, Repair 1 | Strength |
| recipe_armor_upgrade_1 | Armor Level 1 | Basic armor piece, hide or fiber, slime gel | Sewing kit or repair kit | Repair 1 | Dexterity |
| recipe_oaken_bow | Oaken Bow | Better wood, bowstring, slime gel | Woodworking bench | Bowyer 1 | Dexterity |
| recipe_oaken_arrows | Oaken Arrows | Better wood, small bones, grass fiber | Wood carving kit | Fletcher 1 | Dexterity |
| recipe_oaken_quiver | Oaken Quiver | Better wood strips, small hide, grass fiber, slime gel | Sewing kit | Leatherworking 1 | Dexterity |
| recipe_slime_gear_basic | Basic Slime Gear | Slime core, slime gel, cloth or hide | Alchemy kit, sewing kit | Alchemy 1 or Sewing 1 | Wisdom or Dexterity |
| recipe_alchemic_cloak | Alchemic Cloak | Travel cloak, lavender, mint, rosemary, slime gel, oil | Alchemy kit | Alchemy 1, Sewing 1 | Wisdom |
| recipe_adventuring_backpack | Adventuring Backpack | Small hides, flax, grass fiber, basic wood, slime gel | Sewing kit, workbench | Sewing 1, Woodworking 1 | Dexterity |
| recipe_upgraded_fishing_rod_1 | Upgraded Fishing Rod Level 1 | Fishing rod, basic wood, fiber, small bones, slime gel | Wood carving kit | Woodworking 1 | Dexterity |
| recipe_magical_enchanted_ring_basic | Magical Enchanted Ring | Basic ring, slime core, common herbs, minor rune service | Rune table or enchanter | Rune 1 or Enchanting service | Intelligence |
| recipe_pickaxe_1 | Pickaxe Level 1 | Pickaxe, basic wood, field stone, slime gel | Repair kit or hammer | Repair 1 or Smithing 1 | Strength |

#### Starter Market Seed

The first market seed should make Eldoria's early economy readable without locking final prices too soon.

| Market Category | Common Starter Goods | Supply | Demand | Main Sellers |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Basic weapons | Basic sword, knife, bow, staff, axe | Normal | Normal to high | Blacksmiths, Adventurers Guild, thrift stalls. |
| Basic armor | Leather, cloth, chain, plate | Normal | High | Armorers, Knights Guild surplus, secondhand merchants. |
| Gathering tools | Pickaxe, hatchet, fishing rod, gathering knife | Normal | High | Archers Guild, tool sellers, farmers. |
| Crafting kits | Sewing kit, alchemy kit, repair kit, wood carving kit | Low to normal | High | Crafting stalls, guild vendors, traveling merchants. |
| F-rank materials | Wood, flax, grass fiber, herbs, hides, slime parts | High | Constant | Gatherers, farmers, hunters, Adventurers Guild. |
| Food and drink | Fish, wild fruit, vegetables, meat, honey | Normal | Constant | Farmers, bakers, taverns, meal halls. |
| Maps | Local maps, crude sketch maps, route maps | Low | Medium | Cartographers, scouts, merchants, thieves. |
| Storage | Pouches, bags, chests, quivers | Normal | High | Leatherworkers, tailors, general merchants. |
| Stolen goods | Jewelry, tools, maps, small weapons | Hidden | Depends on criminal access | Fences, Thieves Guild, corrupt merchants. |

Starter market value bands:

- Very cheap: grass fiber, seeds, common herbs, field stone.
- Cheap: basic wood, clay, small fish, wild fruit, wild vegetables.
- Standard: small hides, small bones, slime gel, flax, simple tools.
- Useful: slime cores, tanned hide, iron bars, bows, armor pieces, pouches.
- Expensive early: alchemy kits, repair kits, magical armbands, enchanted rings, good maps.
- High fixed demand: gold, because gold remains valuable regardless of world inventory.

This starter data set gives the first menus enough content to display real items, real recipes, real skills, real abilities, and early trade behavior.

#### Starting Loadout Automation

Starting loadouts should be generated from the player's first choices instead of being one fixed pile of items.

Loadout inputs:

- Race
- Sub-race
- Character creation skills
- Character creation abilities
- Chosen starting class, if any
- Chosen starting job, if any
- Guild joined after tutorial, if any
- Progression Zero or Progression Rank path
- Starting reputation modifiers
- Starting wealth modifiers
- Tutorial performance
- Server rules

Loadout rules:

- All players receive a minimum survival kit.
- Guild kits add role-specific gear.
- Job kits add work-specific tools.
- No starter loadout should give rare, legendary, relic, or high-rank items.
- Most starter items should be F Rank and Common rarity.
- Guild-issued gear may be marked as guild property.
- Loaned gear can be reclaimed if the player is expelled, disgraced, or fails to pay required dues.
- Poor-quality, used, or repaired items may appear in thrift, charity, or poor district starts.
- Noble, guild, or wealthy backgrounds may receive cleaner gear, but not stronger progression.
- Race and body size should adjust item fit without changing the kit's core value.

The loadout generator should avoid giving every player the same identity. A player who chooses Stealth, Lockpicking, and Streetwise should not receive the same extras as a player who chooses Field Medic, Social Etiquette, and Born Leader.

##### Minimum Progression Zero Kit

Every new player receives this minimum kit after Laucian's tutorial, unless a story rule replaces it.

| Loadout ID | Path | Contents | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| loadout_progression_zero_minimum | Progression Zero | Basic Cloth Clothing, Basic Underwear, Basic Footgear, Basic Travel Bag, Basic Money Pouch, 1 simple food item, 1 water item, crude local map, tutorial note | Lets the player enter Eldoria with basic dignity, storage, survival supplies, and direction. |

Progression Zero players should not be forced into a weapon immediately. They can pick up, buy, craft, earn, or be issued gear based on their first choices.

Optional Progression Zero starter picks:

- One basic weapon, if the player completed the combat tutorial or chooses a combat-leaning start.
- One basic tool, if the player completed a gathering or work tutorial.
- One minor healing item, if the player performed poorly in combat.
- One small map upgrade, if the player performed well in map reading.
- One social introduction note, if the player chooses a social or merchant path.

##### Starting Class Loadout Seeds

Starting class loadouts should add a small role identity on top of the minimum kit.

| Loadout ID | Starting Path | Added Gear | Early Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| loadout_class_archer | Archer | Basic Bow, Basic Arrows, Basic Quiver, Basic Knife, Basic Hood | Hunting, ranged combat, scouting, and F-rank wilderness quests. |
| loadout_class_priest | Priest | Basic Robes, Basic Staff, Basic Necklace or holy symbol, Healer's Kit, simple blessing token | Healing, social trust, undead response, and Church entry. |
| loadout_class_knight | Knight | Basic Sword, Basic Shield, Basic Chain Mail or training plate, Basic Sword Sheath | Guard training, defense, escorts, and Knights Guild path. |
| loadout_class_adventurer_f | Adventurer Rank F | Basic Sword or Basic Staff, Basic Leather Armor, Basic Travel Cloak, Gathering Knife | Flexible F-rank questing and beginner monster work. |
| loadout_class_thief | Thief | Basic Knife, Basic Hood, Lockpicks, Basic Travel Cloak, small fake-name note | Stealth, retrieval jobs, locks, and underworld introductions. |
| loadout_class_progression_rank_1 | Progression Rank 1 | Basic Staff or Basic Knife, Basic Cloth Armor, Gathering Knife, crude local map | Broad noob-plus path with room to discover secret options later. |

Class loadouts should be lightly customizable. For example, a Knight can choose sword and shield or heavier training armor, while an Archer can choose extra arrows or a better gathering knife.

##### Starting Job Loadout Seeds

Job loadouts give tools, not power. They help the player begin working immediately.

| Loadout ID | Starting Job | Added Gear | Early Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| loadout_job_builder | Builder | Hammer, Rope, Basic Tool Pouch, small wood order note | Repairs, simple construction, city work, and contractor path. |
| loadout_job_hunter_gatherer | Hunter/Gatherer | Gathering Knife, Gathering Pouch, Hatchet or Fishing Rod, Basic Knife | F-rank gathering, hunting, herbs, wood, and food quests. |
| loadout_job_adventurer | Adventurer | Basic weapon choice, Basic Leather Armor, Basic Travel Cloak | General F-rank questing. |
| loadout_job_officer | Officer | Basic Sword, Basic Shield, official writ, Basic Chain Mail | Law, patrols, escort work, and authority training. |
| loadout_job_merchant | Merchant | Basic Money Pouch, Basic Travel Bag, crude ledger, stall token | Buying, selling, inventory reading, and Merchants Guild entry. |
| loadout_job_blacksmith | Blacksmith | Hammer, Tongs, Sharpening Stone, small iron order note | Repair, forging, metalwork, and smithing progression. |
| loadout_job_tavern_owner | Tavern Owner | Cooking Utensils, Pot, Pan, small supply ledger | Food, drink, service economy, and local relationships. |
| loadout_job_farmer | Farmer | Hoe, Rake, Watering Can, Seeds | Farming, crops, gardens, and local food economy. |
| loadout_job_fencer | Fencer | Basic Knife, Basic Money Pouch, coded buyer note | Stolen goods handling and risky trade. |
| loadout_job_dungeon_explorer | Dungeon Explorer | Rope, Flint and Steel, Basic Knife, crude dungeon sketch | Early dungeon caution, maps, traps, and survival. |
| loadout_job_craftsman | Craftsman | Sewing Kit or Wood Carving Kit, Basic Material Pouch | Early crafting and work orders. |
| loadout_job_baker | Baker | Cooking Utensils, Pot, Seeds or Flour Supply Note, small recipe card | Bread, food economy, meal halls, and merchant demand. |
| loadout_job_contractor | Contractor | Hammer, crude ledger, local work permit, measuring cord | Building contracts, labor teams, and city expansion. |

If a class and job both grant the same item, the generator should avoid waste by upgrading quantity, durability, quality, or supplies instead.

Example:

- Archer plus Hunter/Gatherer should not receive two basic knives by default. The generator can give one better-condition Basic Knife, extra arrows, extra gathering pouch capacity, or a small food bundle.

##### Guild-Issued Loadout Seeds

Guild-issued loadouts should come from registration, sponsorship, dues, quests, or guild permission.

| Loadout ID | Guild | Issued Gear | Guild Property Risk |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| loadout_guild_adventurers_basic | Adventurers Guild | Basic weapon choice, Basic Leather Armor, Basic Travel Bag, 1 F-rank quest token | Low; usually paid for through registration or first quests. |
| loadout_guild_archers_gathering | Archers Guild | Gathering Pouch, Gathering Knife, Hatchet, Fishing Rod, basic outdoor task list | Low; unofficial guild may be flexible but poor. |
| loadout_guild_knights_recruit | Knights Guild | Basic Shield, Basic Sword, Chain Mail or training plate, official tabard | High; gear can be reclaimed or debt-marked. |
| loadout_guild_thieves_tools | Thieves Guild | Lockpicks, Thieves Tools, Basic Hood, coded contact token | High; debts, favors, and leverage may replace normal payment. |
| loadout_guild_merchants_starter | Merchants Guild | Basic Money Pouch, basic stall permit, crude ledger, small wagon access note | Medium; permits, fees, and taxes matter. |
| loadout_guild_church_initiate | Church | Basic Robes, holy relic token, Healer's Kit, funeral or cleansing writ | Medium; doctrine and reputation matter. |
| loadout_guild_mages_hidden | Mages Guild | No public kit at character creation; secret mana-well clues, strange rune mark, dream invitation seed | Secret; only appears after hidden unlock tasks. |

The Mages Guild should not be available as a normal starting guild. Players who avoid committing too early, deepen awareness of their mana well, follow strange clues, or catch Laucian's attention can later be invited to Dawnspire.

##### Secret Mage Path Hints

Secret Mage path hints should be subtle. The player should feel like something is strange before the game confirms anything.

Possible hint items or events:

- A faint rune mark only visible under certain light.
- A map corner that refuses to stay folded.
- A training dummy in Laucian's lab that briefly reacts to the player's mana.
- A harmless dream of a sky-bound white door.
- A minor mana pulse when touching a common spell component.
- A damaged page that contains incomplete rune geometry.
- A brief fourth-wall comment from Laucian that sounds like a joke until later.

These hints should not grant full mage power. They are invitations, not rewards.

##### Loadout Quality Automation

Starter loadouts can vary by item condition.

| Quality Band | Meaning | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Worn | Used, scratched, repaired, lower durability | Poor district, thrift, charity, criminal start. |
| Standard | Normal starter condition | Most guild, job, and tutorial starts. |
| Clean | Better presentation, same power | Noble, wealthy, Church, Merchant starts. |
| Guild-Marked | Officially issued and tracked | Knights, Church, Merchants, Adventurers. |
| Debt-Marked | Useful but owed to someone | Thieves Guild, poor district, desperate starts. |
| Personalized | Cosmetic identity, no power increase | Family item, cultural item, race flavor. |

Quality should affect durability, social impression, resale value, and ownership risk more than raw combat power.

##### Loadout Conflict Rules

Some choices should change the loadout.

- Humans do not receive Darkvision-related tools or hints by default.
- Demons may receive more suspicious reactions when carrying weapons in human districts.
- Beastfolk may receive altered gear fits for ears, tails, claws, hooves, paws, wings, or muzzles.
- Fae gear may be strange, whimsical, tiny, oversized, crooked, dreamlike, or magically inconvenient.
- Dwarven kits should always include work identity, because all dwarves have jobs.
- Orc kits may lean toward authority, guard work, bounty hunting, or intimidation-friendly gear.
- Thief kits should hide obvious criminal tools unless the player has underworld access.
- Priest and Paladin kits should show patron or doctrine identity.
- Progression Rank 1 kits should avoid locking the player out of the secret Mages Guild path.

Starting loadouts should support roleplay, not trap the player. A player can sell, lose, repair, replace, upgrade, steal, gift, or craft their way into a different path.

#### First Eldoria Quest Automation V0.1

The first Eldoria quests should teach Huwan through play. The player should not feel like they are trapped in a long tutorial after Laucian's lab. Instead, Eldoria should give small, practical problems that naturally introduce survival, gathering, trade, combat, relationships, guilds, maps, and hidden magic.

First quest rules:

- Every player should receive at least one safe survival task.
- Every player should be shown at least one guild, but not forced to join.
- Every player should see the market economy early.
- Every player should experience a relationship choice early.
- Every player should be able to encounter slimes safely near Eldoria.
- Players who avoid immediate commitment should receive subtle Dawnspire hints.
- Quest markers should respect battle fog and map knowledge.
- Quest rewards should update NPC inventory, market supply, relationship values, skills, reputation, and guild interest.
- Quests should adapt to player loadout, race, class, job, skills, abilities, and reputation.

The first quest web should be modular. The game can choose a clean path for new players, but it should also support players who wander, steal, trade, fight, talk, craft, or ignore instructions.

##### Starter Quest Template

Every generated quest should use a simple shared template.

| Field | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | Stable quest identifier. |
| Display name | Player-facing title. |
| Quest type | Survival, gathering, combat, trade, social, guild, hidden, tutorial follow-up. |
| Starting NPC | NPC or system that offers the quest. |
| Starting location | Eldoria district or nearby area. |
| Required discovery | What the player must know before the quest appears. |
| Requirements | Item, skill, class, job, race, relationship, guild rank, or map condition. |
| Objective steps | Clear actions the player can take. |
| Alternate solutions | Nonlinear ways to solve the problem. |
| Failure rules | Timeouts, NPC solves it, problem worsens, or relationship drops. |
| Rewards | Gold, items, skill XP, job XP, reputation, relationship, maps, hints. |
| World updates | Inventory, market, NPC needs, district state, quest chain state. |
| Map behavior | Marker, search area, battle fog reveal, or no marker. |
| Relationship effects | Trust, respect, debt, suspicion, gratitude, fear, or rivalry changes. |
| Hidden hooks | Secret class, Dawnspire, faction, noble, deity, or dungeon links. |

##### First Arrival Flow

After leaving Laucian's white lab, the player arrives in or near Eldoria.

The first ten minutes should usually include:

1. Arrival marker appears on the minimap.
2. Battle fog clears around the entry point.
3. Player receives or notices a simple food/water need.
4. A nearby NPC offers a low-pressure task.
5. Player is guided toward Guild Row or Goldscale Market.
6. Player learns how quest markers, minimap icons, and local map knowledge work.
7. Player sees at least one merchant, one guild sign, and one ordinary NPC routine.
8. Player gets a choice between helping, ignoring, bargaining, stealing, or asking more questions.

This gives the player agency immediately after the controlled tutorial.

##### First Quest Chain Seeds

| Quest ID | Name | Type | Starting NPC or Source | Core Lesson |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| quest_intro_thirst_and_bread | Bread, Water, and First Footsteps | Survival/social | Town greeter, meal hall worker, or poor district helper | Hunger, thirst, interaction, simple kindness. |
| quest_intro_local_map | Find Your Feet in Eldoria | Map/navigation | Gate clerk, cartographer apprentice, or tutorial note | Minimap, battle fog, map markers, NSEW. |
| quest_intro_gathering_green_edge | Green Edge Gathering | Gathering | Archers Guild runner or market supplier | Herbs, fibers, wood, basic resource nodes. |
| quest_intro_first_sale | The First Honest Sale | Trade/market | Stall merchant or Merchants Guild clerk | Selling items, fair price, local demand. |
| quest_intro_slime_in_grass | Slimes in the Tall Grass | Combat/gathering | Adventurers Guild board or worried farmer | Safe combat, harvesting, slime cores, slime gel. |
| quest_intro_guild_row_walk | The Guild Row Walk | Guild/social | Town greeter or quest board | See major guilds without forcing choice. |
| quest_intro_small_kindness | A Small Kindness | Relationship/social | Poor resident, child courier, elder, Beastfolk laborer, or Demon refugee | First meaningful relationship choice. |
| quest_intro_odd_rune_echo | The Mark That Wasn't There | Hidden magic | Strange rune mark, odd map corner, dream, or Laucian echo | First Dawnspire hint. |

These quests can appear in different order depending on the player's actions. A hungry player may see the food quest first. A merchant-minded player may see the market sale first. A combat-minded player may be pushed toward the slime quest. A Progression Rank 1 player may receive a stronger hidden magic hint.

##### First Food and Water Task

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | quest_intro_thirst_and_bread |
| Name | Bread, Water, and First Footsteps |
| Type | Survival/social |
| Starting Location | Eldoria entry road, Dawnmere Commons, Low Lantern Ward, or market edge |
| Starting NPC | Town greeter, meal hall worker, poor district helper, or bakery runner |
| Objective | Obtain food and water, then eat and drink once. |
| Basic Solution | Accept a simple meal and water from a helper. |
| Trade Solution | Buy cheap bread and water from a stall. |
| Work Solution | Carry a small crate, sweep a stall front, or deliver a message in exchange for food. |
| Theft Solution | Steal food, with witness and reputation risk. |
| Charity Solution | Visit a Dawnmere meal hall, exposing the player to House Dawnmere's public virtue. |
| Rewards | Hunger/thirst lesson, small relationship gain, minor social XP, possible bread or waterskin. |
| World Update | Vendor or meal hall inventory changes; NPC remembers whether player paid, worked, thanked, stole, or demanded. |

This quest should quietly introduce class tension. A wealthy-looking NPC may treat the player differently than a poor resident. A Demon or Beastfolk player may see prejudice sooner. A kind response should matter.

##### First Map and Battle Fog Task

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | quest_intro_local_map |
| Name | Find Your Feet in Eldoria |
| Type | Map/navigation |
| Starting Location | Eldoria gate, cartographer stand, or first road crossing |
| Starting NPC | Gate clerk, cartographer apprentice, scout, or the tutorial note from Laucian |
| Objective | Open the map, identify North/South/East/West, reveal a nearby road, and mark one destination. |
| Basic Solution | Follow the road to a public landmark. |
| Map Purchase Solution | Buy a cheap local street map to clear a small patch of battle fog. |
| Social Solution | Ask an NPC for directions, revealing a marker without buying a map. |
| Theft Solution | Steal a crude sketch map, with risk. |
| Rewards | Local map marker, map reading XP, small battle fog reveal. |
| World Update | The player learns that not all map data is free or accurate. |

This quest should introduce fake, outdated, and poor maps without punishing the player too hard yet.

##### First Gathering Quest

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | quest_intro_gathering_green_edge |
| Name | Green Edge Gathering |
| Type | Gathering/resource |
| Starting Location | Mossgate Outskirts or Archers Guild notice |
| Starting NPC | Robin Quickfoot, Archers Guild runner, herbalist, baker, or market supplier |
| Objective | Gather a small bundle of F-rank materials. |
| Common Targets | Grass fiber, lavender, mint, rosemary, basic wood, wild fruit, wild vegetables, clay, or field stone. |
| Tool Support | Gathering Knife, Hatchet, Pickaxe, Gathering Pouch, Fishing Rod. |
| No-Tool Solution | Gather lower-quality herbs, wild fruit, or loose fiber by hand. |
| Trade Solution | Buy the materials from another player or NPC. |
| Social Solution | Convince a gatherer to share supplies in exchange for a favor. |
| Rewards | Gathering XP, small gold, material pouch hint, Archers Guild interest, market demand hint. |
| World Update | Local resource node count updates; NPC or market inventory improves. |

This quest should teach that resources respawn later, but overharvesting can create short-term shortages.

##### First Merchant Sale Quest

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | quest_intro_first_sale |
| Name | The First Honest Sale |
| Type | Trade/market |
| Starting Location | Goldscale Market |
| Starting NPC | Stall merchant, Merchants Guild clerk, Count August Silvertongue's clerk, or baker |
| Objective | Sell one useful item or complete one small trade. |
| Basic Solution | Sell gathered materials to a merchant. |
| Crafting Solution | Craft a simple item, then sell it. |
| Merchant Job Solution | Use Merchant job hints to compare prices before selling. |
| Barter Solution | Negotiate a slightly better price. |
| Dishonest Solution | Try to sell stolen goods, triggering risk and possible Thieves Guild attention. |
| Rewards | Gold, barter XP, market menu tutorial, local price knowledge. |
| World Update | Merchant inventory, local supply, demand, and price memory update. |

This quest teaches that trade is alive. Selling a stack of mint should matter more if a healer, baker, or alchemist currently needs it.

##### First Slime Encounter

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | quest_intro_slime_in_grass |
| Name | Slimes in the Tall Grass |
| Type | Combat/gathering |
| Starting Location | Mossgate Outskirts |
| Starting NPC | Adventurers Guild board, farmer, worried child, or F-rank Adventurer |
| Objective | Drive off, defeat, capture, or study a small slime group. |
| Combat Solution | Defeat 1 to 3 basic slimes. |
| Taming Solution | Calm or feed a slime if the player has Beast Calm, taming tools, or suitable food. |
| Gathering Solution | Collect slime gel from a harmless slime trail. |
| Alchemy Solution | Identify slime gel and slime cores for crafting. |
| Avoidance Solution | Mark the slime patch on the map and report it. |
| Rewards | Slime gel, possible slime core, F-rank combat XP, harvesting XP, Adventurers Guild interest. |
| World Update | Local slime population changes; monster population data feeds Adventurers Guild quest generation. |

This quest should be safe but not meaningless. If ignored, slimes may damage crops, block a path, dirty water, or create a later cleanup quest.

##### First Guild Introduction Quest

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | quest_intro_guild_row_walk |
| Name | The Guild Row Walk |
| Type | Guild/social |
| Starting Location | Guild Row |
| Starting NPC | Town greeter, guild runner, quest board, or curious adventurer |
| Objective | Visit at least three guild halls and learn what they offer. |
| Guilds Shown | Adventurers Guild, Merchants Guild, Church, Knights Guild, Archers Guild, and visible rumors of the Thieves Guild. |
| Hidden Guild Rule | Mages Guild is not publicly listed. |
| Basic Solution | Walk to public guild entrances and inspect signs. |
| Social Solution | Speak to guild representatives. |
| Job Solution | Ask about work based on current job. |
| Secret Solution | Notice one strange missing sign, rune scratch, or impossible blank spot on the map. |
| Rewards | Guild menu unlock, guild marker reveal, first guild reputation notes. |
| World Update | Guilds record that the player showed interest. |

This quest should make Eldoria feel like a city of opportunities, not a forced class-selection menu.

##### First Relationship Choice

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | quest_intro_small_kindness |
| Name | A Small Kindness |
| Type | Relationship/social |
| Starting Location | Low Lantern Ward, Dawnmere Commons, Goldscale Market, or road to Mossgate |
| Starting NPC | Poor resident, hungry child, injured gatherer, Demon refugee, Beastfolk laborer, elderly merchant, or lost courier |
| Objective | Decide how to respond to a small personal problem. |
| Kind Solution | Help freely. |
| Practical Solution | Help for pay or trade. |
| Cruel Solution | Mock, exploit, threaten, or rob the NPC. |
| Lawful Solution | Bring the issue to guards, Church, or guild authority. |
| Deceptive Solution | Promise help and then manipulate the outcome. |
| Rewards | Relationship value changes, reputation seed, possible item, rumor, or future contact. |
| World Update | NPC memory records the choice; nearby witnesses may spread reputation. |

This should be the player's first clear proof that relationships determine the story. A tiny act can become a future ally, enemy, debt, rumor, romance seed, guild invite, criminal opening, or divine notice.

##### First Dawnspire Hint

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quest ID | quest_intro_odd_rune_echo |
| Name | The Mark That Wasn't There |
| Type | Hidden magic |
| Starting Location | Anywhere after tutorial, most likely Guild Row, map table, market stall, or quiet alley |
| Starting Source | Laucian echo, strange rune, damaged page, map behavior, mana pulse, or dream fragment |
| Objective | Notice something impossible and choose whether to investigate. |
| Basic Trigger | Player remains Progression Zero or Progression Rank 1 and does not immediately commit to a standard guild. |
| Magic Trigger | Player has Minor Mana Sense, deep mana well trait, rune interest, spell curiosity, or odd tutorial response. |
| Map Trigger | A map corner folds toward an unseen sky door. |
| Social Trigger | An NPC says the Mages Guild does not exist, then forgets saying it. |
| Reward | Hidden Dawnspire flag, minor mana-well awareness, incomplete rune clue, or Laucian remark. |
| World Update | Mages Guild discovery progress begins quietly. |

This quest should not reveal Dawnspire immediately. It should plant the first impossible breadcrumb.

Possible Laucian-style lines:

- "Nope. You did not see that. Unless you did. In which case, neat."
- "The devs made me subtle today. Weird choice."
- "Keep walking. Or don't. I am not your quest marker. Mostly."

##### Automated Quest Selection Rules

The starter quest system should pick quests based on what the player is already doing.

| Player Behavior | Suggested Quest Priority |
| --- | --- |
| Opens inventory repeatedly | Food/water, equipment, or first sale quest. |
| Opens map repeatedly | Local map and battle fog quest. |
| Walks toward Mossgate | Gathering or slime quest. |
| Talks to merchants | First sale or market tutorial quest. |
| Talks to guards | Guild Row, officer work, or law intro quest. |
| Talks to poor NPCs | Small Kindness or Dawnmere Commons quest. |
| Equips weapon quickly | Slime or Adventurers Guild quest. |
| Uses gathering tools | Green Edge Gathering quest. |
| Uses stealth or lockpicks | Thief rumor or stolen goods warning. |
| Avoids guild commitment | Dawnspire hint chance increases. |
| Has Minor Mana Sense | Dawnspire hint chance increases. |
| Has Beast Calm | Slime taming option unlocks earlier. |
| Has Silver Tongue | Barter and relationship options become clearer. |
| Has Born Leader | Group-help and escort variants appear earlier. |

This lets the first hour respond to the player instead of forcing a single quest order.

##### Starter Quest Rewards

Rewards should stay small but meaningful.

Reward categories:

- Small gold
- Food
- Water
- Basic materials
- F-rank equipment
- Simple maps
- Skill XP
- Job XP
- Class XP
- Relationship gains
- Guild interest
- Reputation seed
- Market price knowledge
- Battle fog reveal
- Hidden Dawnspire progress
- Follow-up quest unlock

The best early reward is not always an item. Sometimes the best reward is trust, a useful name, a merchant discount, a map marker, a rumor, or an NPC who remembers the player kindly.

##### Starter Quest Failure and Expiration

Starter quests should be forgiving but not frozen forever.

Failure and expiration rules:

- Survival tasks can be ignored, but hunger and thirst continue.
- Gathering requests can expire when another NPC fills the order.
- Merchant requests can change when prices shift.
- Slime problems can worsen if ignored.
- Relationship chances can pass if the player walks away.
- Guild interest may cool if the player repeatedly wastes representatives' time.
- Dawnspire hints do not fail permanently, but the first hint can be missed.

If a quest expires, the world should continue. The NPC may solve it, suffer from it, sell the problem to another player, ask a guild, become angry, lose money, gain debt, or generate a different quest later.

##### First Hour Quest Web

Recommended first-hour web:

1. Minimum Progression Zero kit is applied.
2. Arrival point clears battle fog.
3. Food/water task appears nearby.
4. Map task teaches NSEW, roads, and battle fog.
5. Player is nudged toward Guild Row or Goldscale Market.
6. Gathering, first sale, or slime quest appears based on behavior.
7. Relationship choice appears as a human-scale interruption.
8. Guild introduction opens public guild options.
9. Hidden Dawnspire hint may trigger if conditions fit.
10. The player chooses a path, stays Progression Rank, joins a guild, takes a job, or wanders.

The first hour should end with the player understanding one thing clearly: Eldoria is alive, and even small choices can echo.

##### Starter NPC Assignment Automation

Starter quests should be assigned to real Eldoria NPCs, but they should not behave like fixed one-time quests. Most starter quests should be generated from NPC needs, inventory, job duties, current wealth, relationships, and local supply.

Core rule:

- NPCs ask for help when they need something.
- Completing the request updates the NPC's inventory or situation.
- That NPC should not ask for the same resource again until their inventory runs low, spoils, is stolen, is consumed, is sold, or a new demand appears.

This means different players can receive different quests from the same NPC depending on what is happening in that server's Eldoria.

Examples:

- If a player brings Mara Kettleblack enough food, Mara does not need more food immediately.
- If another player visits later, Mara may instead need firewood, clean water, bowls, medicine, delivery help, protection, or information about a missing child.
- If the meal hall is well stocked, Mara may offer cheap meals, rumors, relationship quests, or poor district introductions instead of a gathering request.
- If a thief steals her supplies, the next generated quest may become recovery, investigation, or vengeance.

These NPCs should be the first pool used by the starter quest generator.

These NPCs should be enough to start the first hour without overloading the player with every major leader at once.

Major leaders like Gorth Runkis, Count August Silvertongue IV, Robin Quickfoot, Marquis Serville, Merlin Anquist, and Arch Diocese Ranquith should exist nearby as powerful figures, but early players usually meet clerks, runners, apprentices, workers, and ordinary residents first.

##### Starter NPC Inventory Need Profiles

Each starter NPC should have a simple inventory need profile. When inventory falls below a threshold, the NPC can generate a request.

| NPC | Watches Inventory For | Low Threshold Example | Generated Requests |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Lina Wellspring | Local maps, welcome notes, water tokens, guild direction slips | Fewer than 5 starter maps or 10 water tokens | Deliver papers, buy maps, fetch ink, guide a lost newcomer. |
| Pell Inkstep | Blank parchment, ink, local map copies, route notes | Fewer than 3 map copies or 2 ink bottles | Gather route data, buy parchment, verify a road, expose a fake map. |
| Bessa Ovenmere | Flour, honey, wild fruit, herbs, firewood, clean water | Flour below daily bake amount or honey below dessert order | Gather honey, buy flour, chop wood, deliver bread, sell surplus bread. |
| Tollen Reedfield | Seeds, tools, water, scare markers, repaired fences | Seeds below planting need or fence durability low | Gather seeds, fix fence, clear slimes, bring water, recover stolen tools. |
| Saffi Quickleaf | Trail markers, rope, basic wood, arrows, fish, field reports | Arrows below patrol need or trail markers missing | Gather wood, craft markers, fish for camp, scout a route, mark resources. |
| Penn Orwick | Arrows, bandages, confidence, slime gel, repair kit access | Bandages low or weapon durability poor | Help with slimes, bring bandages, repair gear, escort back to town. |
| Yarra Tinveil | Receipts, stall permits, common goods, price reports, small coins | Price report outdated or stall goods below order | Sell item, compare prices, deliver receipt, fill buy order, audit suspicious goods. |
| Mara Kettleblack | Bread, vegetables, fish, water, firewood, bowls, medicine | Meal portions below evening need | Bring food, fetch water, gather herbs, deliver meals, investigate missing supplies. |
| Nix Hollowglass | Safe errands, food, warm clothing, hiding places, trusted adults | Hunger high, cloak damaged, danger nearby | Share food, escort safely, deliver note, hide from Dawnmere agents, find lost toy. |
| Brindle Moss | Broken gear, slime gel, small bones, scrap metal, repair tools | Scrap below repair queue or slime gel low | Bring broken items, collect slime parts, test repaired gear, recover stolen junk. |
| Sister Ameline Voss | Lavender, rosemary, bandages, clean water, holy candles | Healing stock below patient need | Gather herbs, deliver medicine, cleanse wound supplies, quietly question Church orders. |
| Orro Vane | Old map fragments, ink, rumor notes, safe routes, payment | Map stock low or route data outdated | Verify road, compare maps, bring ink, recover stolen map, inspect Verdant Maw rumor. |
| Rovi Mane | Horse feed, horseshoes, saddle straps, road reports | Feed below stable need or horseshoes damaged | Gather feed, bring horseshoe, check road, calm animal, report bandits. |
| Halka Stonejaw | Wanted posters, patrol reports, basic arrows, restraint rope | Patrol report overdue or rope below guard need | Deliver report, identify suspect, escort clerk, warn player about laws. |
| Sable Quill | Blank ledgers, old records, strange ink, missing pages, quiet | Missing page flag active or strange ink low | Find page, ask no questions, inspect rune scratch, deliver sealed note. |

NPCs should also have surplus behavior. If an NPC has too much of something, they may sell it, trade it, donate it, lower prices, create delivery work, or ask the player to move goods elsewhere.

##### Inventory-Driven Quest Logic

Starter quest generation should follow this loop.

1. NPC checks daily needs.
2. NPC checks job duties.
3. NPC checks inventory.
4. NPC checks wealth.
5. NPC checks current market prices.
6. NPC checks safety and travel risk.
7. NPC checks relationships and trusted helpers.
8. If a need cannot be solved internally, NPC creates a request.
9. Request becomes a direct ask, guild notice, market order, rumor, or hidden contract.
10. Player, another player, or another NPC may fulfill it.
11. Fulfillment updates the NPC inventory, wealth, schedule, memory, and relationships.
12. The same request closes until the threshold is reached again.

The system should support partial fulfillment.

Example:

- Mara needs 20 meal portions.
- One player brings 6 fish.
- Another player brings 8 wild vegetables.
- A third player buys bread from Bessa and donates it.
- Mara's need drops as each delivery arrives.
- Once the need is met, Mara stops asking for food and may start asking for delivery help instead.

##### Different Players, Different Quests

Players should not all receive identical quests from every NPC.

Quest variation inputs:

- NPC current inventory
- NPC current wealth
- NPC current schedule
- NPC relationship with the player
- Player race and public reputation
- Player class, job, skills, and tools
- Player inventory
- Local market supply
- Other players' recent actions
- Other NPCs' recent actions
- Crime, theft, spoilage, weather, monster activity, and war events

Examples:

- A player with a pickaxe may be asked for stone or ore.
- A player with a gathering pouch may be asked for herbs, seeds, or fiber.
- A player with a repair kit may be asked to fix broken tools.
- A player with Silver Tongue may be asked to negotiate.
- A player with Beast Calm may be asked to calm a panicked animal or tame a slime.
- A player with a bad reputation may be offered risky, low-trust, or criminal work.
- A Demon player may see prejudice from some NPCs but deeper trust from Demon refugees.
- A Beastfolk player may receive different reactions around labor brokers, stables, and Dawnmere staff.

If one player solves an NPC's shortage, another player may see a different quest from the same NPC.

Example:

- Player A brings Bessa honey.
- Bessa now has enough honey.
- Player B arrives later.
- Instead of asking for honey, Bessa may ask Player B to deliver sweet bread to Mara, sell extra pastries, find missing flour, repair the oven, or investigate why a merchant overcharged her.

| Find Your Feet in Eldoria | Pell Inkstep, Lina Wellspring, Orro Vane | Yarra Tinveil, Saffi Quickleaf | Map copies, ink, route notes, battle fog state. |

| The First Honest Sale | Yarra Tinveil, Bessa Ovenmere, Count August's clerk | Brindle Moss, market stall owners, fences if criminal | Merchant stock, buy orders, price reports, player inventory. |
| Slimes in the Tall Grass | Penn Orwick, Tollen Reedfield, Adventurers Guild board | Saffi Quickleaf, worried child, Brindle Moss | Slime population, crop damage, slime gel/core demand. |

##### Request Cooldowns and Inventory Thresholds

Each NPC request should have a practical cooldown based on the item type.

| Need Type | Example | Cooldown Logic |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Daily food | Bread, fish, vegetables, water | Can recur daily if consumed by meals. |
| Crafting material | Iron, wood, hides, slime gel | Recurs when production consumes stock. |
| Medicine | Herbs, bandages, clean water | Recurs during sickness, injury waves, or poor district pressure. |
| Tools | Hammer, knife, pickaxe, repair kit | Recurs slowly unless broken, stolen, or lost. |
| Maps | Parchment, ink, route notes | Recurs when sold, copied, stolen, outdated, or demand rises. |
| Safety | Slimes, wolves, bandits, undead | Recurs when population or threat level rises. |
| Social | Escort, apology, debt help, witness | Recurs through relationships and events, not item stock alone. |

NPCs should remember who helped, who refused, who stole, who lied, and who solved the problem efficiently.

##### Starter NPC Memory Updates

Every starter quest should leave a memory.

Memory examples:

- "Player fed me when I was hungry."
- "Player charged me fairly."
- "Player exploited my desperation."
- "Player brought clean herbs on time."
- "Player sold me stolen goods."
- "Player cleared slimes without killing the tamed one."
- "Player asked why Dawnmere wanted the children moved."
- "Player noticed the impossible rune and did not panic."

These memories should feed relationship values, future prices, trust, faction interest, rumors, and future quest offers.

##### World Clock and Time Scale

Huwan should run on an accelerated in-game clock.

Core time rule:

- **24 hours of real time equals 1 full week in Huwan.**

This means:

- 1 real hour equals 7 in-game hours.
- 1 real minute equals 7 in-game minutes.
- 1 in-game hour lasts about 8 minutes and 34 seconds in real time.
- 1 in-game day lasts about 3 hours and 26 minutes in real time.
- 1 in-game week lasts 24 real hours.

This time scale makes the world feel alive without forcing players to wait real days for NPC schedules, market changes, crop growth, guild requests, rent, wages, or inventory demand.

Time should affect:

- NPC daily routines
- Shop opening and closing
- Work shifts
- Sleep schedules
- Meal times
- Market restocks
- Quest expiration
- Resource respawn checks
- Crop growth
- Hunger and thirst
- Animal movement
- Monster activity
- Crime patterns
- Guard patrols
- Guild board updates
- Church services
- Tavern traffic
- Brothel traffic
- Auction timers
- Rent, wages, taxes, and debt
- Weekly events

The world clock should keep running on the server. If a player logs out, the world still advances. When the player returns, NPCs may have moved, sold goods, consumed inventory, completed routines, generated new needs, or had their problems solved by someone else.

##### No Time Skipping

Under no circumstance can players speed up, skip, rewind, pause, or force-advance server time through normal gameplay.

This includes:

- Sleeping in a bed
- Resting at a camp
- Waiting at an inn
- Using a chair, bench, bath, tavern, or brothel room
- Casting time magic
- Using divine blessings
- Using artifacts or relics
- Entering pocket worlds
- Entering dungeons
- Logging out and back in
- Using admin-style character powers during normal player progression

Huwan runs on a living server clock. All players, NPCs, markets, farms, dungeons, guild boards, auctions, resource respawns, food spoilage, wages, taxes, debts, monster populations, and story events share that same time flow.

Beds, camps, inns, and other rest locations can still matter, but they do not skip time.

Resting can provide:

- Health recovery over time
- Mana recovery over time
- Fatigue reduction
- Injury stabilization
- Disease recovery support
- Comfort bonuses
- Social or romance opportunities
- Safety from weather
- Protection from some monsters
- Save or logout safety, if needed

Resting should mean the player spends time safely. It does not mean the server jumps forward.

Forbidden time-acceleration rule:

- No player-accessible spell, item, bed, ritual, relic, deity, class, job, dungeon, pocket world, or UI option can accelerate the shared server clock.

Matrena, forbidden time magic, and deity-level time effects may exist in lore, but they cannot be used to advance the shared server economy or bypass waiting rules in normal gameplay. If a story ever appears to manipulate time, it must be isolated, carefully controlled, and unable to duplicate resources, skip market cycles, bypass cooldowns, or desync players from the living server.

##### In-Game Day Structure

NPC schedules should use time blocks instead of exact minute-by-minute scripts unless the NPC is fully simulated or quest-critical.

| Time Block | In-Game Time | Common World Behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dawn | 05:00 to 07:00 | Bakers, farmers, guards, and travelers begin work. |
| Morning | 07:00 to 11:00 | Shops open, guild boards update, workers travel, markets fill. |
| Midday | 11:00 to 14:00 | Meals, trade peak, public errands, Church and guild traffic. |
| Afternoon | 14:00 to 18:00 | Crafting, deliveries, patrols, gathering returns, market negotiation. |
| Evening | 18:00 to 22:00 | Taverns fill, meal halls serve, workers return, social quests rise. |
| Night | 22:00 to 02:00 | Taverns, thieves, guards, brothels, smugglers, late travelers. |
| Deep Night | 02:00 to 05:00 | Most citizens sleep; criminals, monsters, guards, and secret magic move. |

Essential services should use shifts. A player should not be completely stuck because one important NPC is asleep, but meeting the right person at the right time should still matter.

Examples:

- A bakery may be closed at night, but leftover bread might be available through a tavern.
- A guard office may always have someone present, but Halka Stonejaw may only work certain shifts.
- A Church healer may be awake for emergencies, but official blessings may require daytime staff.
- A Thieves Guild contact may be easier to find at night.

##### Weekly Time Structure

Because 24 real hours equals one in-game week, weekly cycles can matter every real day.

Weekly systems:

- Guild boards refresh.
- Market prices adjust.
- Auctions close.
- Rent is due.
- Wages are paid.
- Taxes are collected.
- Crops grow or fail.
- NPC inventories are consumed or restocked.
- Factions spread influence.
- Noble houses host meetings.
- Churches hold services.
- Caravans arrive or fail to arrive.
- Monster populations shift.
- Bandit activity changes trade routes.
- Poor district needs rise or stabilize.

Exact weekday names can be added later. For now, the week should support seven in-game days with different rhythms.

Possible weekly rhythm:

| Day Role | World Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Market Day | More merchants, better trade, more pickpockets, more maps for sale. |
| Guild Day | Guild boards refresh heavily, recruitment rises, rank tests appear. |
| Labor Day | Building, farming, repair, and contractor work increases. |
| Faith Day | Church services, blessings, funerals, resurrections, and religious quests rise. |
| Patrol Day | Guard patrols increase, bounty checks rise, wanted players feel pressure. |
| Rest Day | Social quests, taverns, brothels, family visits, romance, and leisure rise. |
| Shadow Day | Thieves Guild, black market, secret contracts, and hidden magic hooks rise. |

##### Starter NPC Daily Schedule Seeds

Starter NPCs should move through the city based on work, needs, relationships, and danger.

| NPC | Dawn | Morning | Midday | Afternoon | Evening | Night |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
