| Sable Vey Rook | Sealed delivery, route scouting, decoy identification, witness extraction. | Rogue reputation, black-route hints, anti-scrying tools. | Courier hunt, spell leak, Dawnspire warning. |
| Ormak Wolfbanner | Escort duty, treaty proof, war-road patrol, intimidation backup. | Barbarian training, guard favor, caravan discounts. | Border violence, treaty collapse, raid defense. |
| Mallow Crown-Gel | Slime rescue, illegal farm shutdown, gel trade, wetland defense. | Slime-taming progress, slime gear recipes, alchemy discounts. | Slime strike, hostile wetlands, price spikes. |
| Saelira Mosswhisper | Nonlethal capture, habitat repair, companion care, poacher tracking. | Taming progress, companion leads, forest reputation. | Monster migration, farm attacks, hunter conflict. |
| Lethian Reedmark | Scout marks, ferry defense, arrow crafting, purist tracking. | Archer progress, river maps, mixed-village trust. | Village raids, false patrols, evacuation event. |
| Fenroth Moonharp | Witness escort, lyric recovery, rumor verification, performance security. | Bard progress, reputation repair, hidden history. | Noble cover-up, public scandal, assassination attempt. |
| Caerion Silverroot | Spell correction, framework testing, student rescue, rune dampening. | Mage training, safer spell variants, academy access. | Academy lockdown, spell mutation, caster injuries. |
| Yumi Longwhisker | Shipment audit, price check, contract review, shortage prevention. | Merchant progress, trade discounts, auction perks. | Cartel growth, inflated prices, supply collapse. |
| Kadrim Gearwise | Machine capture, spare part recovery, repair trial, control-core testing. | Artificer progress, companion schematics, repair discounts. | Machine outbreak, guild panic, containment raid. |

### Legendary NPC Lifecycle And Escalation Layer V0.1

Legendary Status NPCs should have lifecycles. They can move from rumor to active presence, crisis, resolution, retirement, death, corruption, redemption, or future promotion. The world should continue adapting around them.

Lifecycle states:

- **Dormant:** Exists in lore, books, gossip, old songs, or remote regions.
- **Rumored:** Players begin hearing about the legend through hints and soft map markers.
- **Verified:** Proof confirms the legend is real and currently active.
- **Contacted:** The player can meet, message, hire, help, or be tested by the legend.
- **Trusted:** The legend offers training, recipes, private quests, discounts, or event access.
- **Crisis:** The legend is threatened or causing a regional event.
- **Resolved:** The current crisis is settled, but consequences remain.
- **Changed:** The legend's status, route, faction ties, market impact, or relationships shift.
- **Retired, Missing, Dead, Or Corrupted:** Extreme outcomes that should require major events.
- **Successor Active:** A student, rival, heir, construct, slime split, bardic apprentice, or party member continues part of the legacy.

Escalation rules:

- Repeated failures should raise urgency rather than instantly delete the chain.
- Ignored crises can become harder, more expensive, more public, or more dangerous.
- Player success can reduce costs, prevent raids, open markets, or change laws.
- Player cruelty can turn a mentor into a rival or enemy.
- Legendary NPC death should be rare and meaningful, not casual background loss.
- If a legend dies, their memory, gear, students, enemies, debts, and unfinished quests should remain in the world.

### Legendary NPC Game Data Handoff Layer V0.1

When these legends move from world bible into the game systems, each should have a consistent profile so quests, simulation, guild boards, markets, memories, and event triggers can read the same information.

Suggested profile fields:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Display Name | The name players see. |
| Hidden Name Or Alias | Secret identity, old name, courier code, title, or disguise. |
| Race Or Lineage | Race, sub-race, monster type, undead type, or construct type. |
| Rank | A, S, SS, SSS, or other world rank. |
| Home Region | Main territory, kingdom, city, road, dungeon, pocket world, or hidden base. |
| Current Location State | Home, traveling, hidden, event, captured, missing, dead, or unknown. |
| Primary Role | Blacksmith, rogue, barbarian, slime monarch, tamer, archer, bard, mage, merchant, artificer. |
| Simulation Depth | Rumor, event, background, full, or protected. |
| Discovery Requirements | Rumor, proof, reputation, skill, guild, class, job, or item needed. |
| Trust Tier | Current player or party access level. |
| Relationship Role | Mentor, rival, ally, patron, enemy, debtor, contractor, protector. |
| Quest Chain State | Not started, discovery, trust, legend, resolved, failed, escalated. |
| Repeatable Request Pool | The smaller jobs this legend can generate. |
| Reward Pool | Skills, abilities, recipes, discounts, contacts, maps, summons, titles, or gear access. |
| Market Impact | Prices, shortages, supply routes, auctions, resource demand, or trade restrictions. |
| Faction Links | Guilds, kingdoms, cults, noble houses, monsters, dragons, or hidden factions. |
| Memory Log | Important player, party, faction, and world events remembered by the legend. |
| Failure Consequences | What changes when players fail, betray, exploit, or ignore the legend. |
| Successor Logic | Who or what carries the legend forward if they retire, vanish, or die. |

Final legendary NPC rules:

- Legendary Status NPCs should add depth to the world without stealing the player's story.
- Their systems should generate choices, not chores.
- They should react to player reputation, not blindly hand out content.
- They should make professions, classes, relationships, and world politics feel connected.
- They can become beloved allies, terrifying enemies, permanent rivals, or future legends in player history.

### Legendary Random Encounter Lottery Layer V0.1

Legendary Status NPCs should not be placed so predictably that players can simply look up a location and farm them. They have homes, routes, duties, and systems, but players should also have a rare lottery chance of running into one through normal play.

Core rule:

- **Legendary encounters are rare, contextual, and partly random.**
- A player can stumble into a legendary while gathering, traveling, shopping, crafting, escorting, dungeon delving, attending festivals, reading maps, following rumors, or helping ordinary NPCs.
- Context can improve the odds, but it should never guarantee the encounter unless the player has already earned a direct summon, quest meeting, or scripted story event.

Encounter lottery stages:

| Roll Result | Encounter Type | Example |
|---|---|---|
| No Hit | Normal world activity continues. | The player gathers herbs, shops, hunts, crafts, or travels as expected. |
| Soft Clue | The player finds a trace, rumor, mark, song, or unusual object. | A riverbent arrow, a black-route token, a strange cog-core, or a slime royal seal. |
| Indirect Contact | The legend affects the scene without appearing directly. | Sable's decoy package appears, Hilda's forge-mark is found, Fenroth's banned verse is heard. |
| Witness Contact | The player sees the legend briefly but cannot yet interact deeply. | Ormak passes with a treaty caravan, Yumi audits a stall, Saelira calms a beast. |
| Direct Contact | The legend speaks, tests, hires, warns, helps, or challenges the player. | The player is offered a small job, warning, or trial. |
| Crisis Contact | The legend appears during an urgent event. | A machine outbreak, slime strike, forge raid, ambush, or spell mutation begins nearby. |
| Fate Contact | Extremely rare. The encounter starts a major chain or server-notable event. | A private summon, legendary apprenticeship chance, relic crisis, or world announcement path begins. |

Lottery modifiers:

- **Luck Stat:** Slightly increases rare encounter odds.
- **World Popularity:** Increases the chance that legends have heard of the player.
- **Relevant Class Or Job:** A blacksmith is more likely to find Hilda clues; a rogue is more likely to cross Sable's routes.
- **Guild Reputation:** Guild activity can raise the chance of legend-related notices.
- **Regional Context:** Being in a relevant biome, city, market, ruin, dungeon, academy, road, or festival can improve odds.
- **NPC Relationships:** Ordinary NPCs can accidentally introduce the player to legendary traces through their own needs.
- **Map Quality:** Purchased maps, old maps, and NPC-made maps can reveal rumor zones or clue routes.
- **Danger Level:** Higher-risk areas can carry better encounter odds, but also worse outcomes.
- **Recent Behavior:** Saving people, exploiting people, over-harvesting, trading fairly, or breaking promises can attract different legends.

Anti-farming rules:

- Legendary lottery rolls should not be grindable through rapid zone entry, logging in and out, sleeping, or repeated menu actions.
- Encounter checks should respect living server time.
- The same player or party should have cooldowns after direct, crisis, or fate contact.
- Failed encounters can leave clues, but repeated failure should not guarantee a meeting.
- Players should not be able to force a specific legend without completing discovery, proof, trust, or direct quest requirements.
- If too many players gather in one place trying to force a legend, the system can shift to rumor-only, decoy, or crowd-event outcomes.

Party and raid rules:

- A party uses a shared encounter roll when acting together.
- If one party member triggers a legendary clue, the whole party can see it if they are present and eligible.
- Reward credit depends on contribution, quest state, and party-leader split rules.
- Raid-scale legendary encounters should be rare and usually tied to crisis, server, dungeon, relic, or faction triggers.

Design goal:

- Players should feel like, "I cannot believe we just ran into them."
- Legendary encounters should create stories, not chores.
- The lottery should reward exploration, reputation, profession identity, and strange luck.
- Even a failed legendary encounter should leave the player with a rumor, clue, suspicion, or future goal.

### Legendary Encounter Rewards Table V0.1

Random legendary encounters should feel exciting without flooding the world with overpowered rewards. The reward should match the encounter tier, the player's contribution, their existing relationship with the legend, and whether the encounter was solo, party, raid, or world scale.

Reward rules:

- **Soft rewards first:** Most random encounters should give clues, access, reputation, knowledge, or opportunity rather than raw power.
- **Power requires trust:** Skills, abilities, unique recipes, legendary gear paths, and direct training should require proof, trust, or a quest chain.
- **No free legendary gear:** A random meeting can reveal a path to legendary equipment, but should not simply hand it over.
- **Profession relevance matters:** A blacksmith gets better smithing rewards from Hilda than a random untrained player.
- **Failure can still reward information:** Even a lost encounter can create a rumor, warning, map note, enemy flag, or future chance.
- **Party rewards split by contribution:** Parties can share discovery, but material rewards, titles, and training access should consider participation.

| Encounter Type | Common Rewards | Rare Rewards | Should Not Give |
|---|---|---|---|
| No Hit | Normal gathering, travel, shopping, combat, or crafting results. | Tiny chance of unrelated rumor from nearby NPCs. | Legendary access, unique skills, rare recipes. |
| Soft Clue | Rumor, journal note, vague map zone, strange object, witness account. | Clue item tied to a future discovery quest. | Direct meeting, major reputation, powerful gear. |
| Indirect Contact | Hidden quest flag, faction attention, market hint, altered NPC dialogue. | A timed lead or private board notice. | Signature training or legendary crafting rights. |
| Witness Contact | Confirmed sighting, reputation bump, clearer map marker, future meeting chance. | A short warning, tossed item, coded phrase, or one-time small favor. | Full trust tier, permanent unlock, relic path. |
| Direct Contact | Small quest, trial, discount, lesson, warning, temporary buff, minor item. | First trust-tier unlock or direct chain invitation. | Final signature ability, legendary weapon, full mentor status. |
| Crisis Contact | Emergency quest, party objective, rare material chance, faction reputation, raid seed. | Event-limited recipe, rescue debt, major trust gain, unique title progress. | Guaranteed top-tier loot without contribution. |
| Fate Contact | Major quest-chain start, legendary apprenticeship chance, relic path, world-event seed. | Signature lesson path, server announcement, permanent relationship role, hidden class/job clue. | Instant demi-god progress, SSS status, unearned legendary item ownership. |

Legend-specific reward examples:

| Legend | Soft Reward | Direct Reward | Crisis Or Fate Reward |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hilda Hammerwake | Forge-mark clue or memorial ingot rumor. | Smithing lesson, repair discount, alloy test quest. | Oath-bound forging path or memorial weapon commission. |
| Sable Vey Rook | Black-route token or courier code fragment. | Stealth route, decoy lesson, sealed delivery contract. | Emergency extraction favor or Dawnspire courier chain. |
| Ormak Wolfbanner | Peace-banner thread or war-road warning. | Rage-control drill, escort contract, guard respect. | Treaty defense event or War-Rest Rage training path. |
| Mallow Crown-Gel | Royal gel trace or cleaner slime rumor. | Slime diplomacy quest, slime care lesson, slime trade access. | Slime charter event or evolved slime companion path. |
| Saelira Mosswhisper | Animal sign, migration warning, nonlethal trap clue. | Taming lesson, companion lead, habitat repair request. | First Language Listening path or monster-herd peace event. |
| Lethian Reedmark | Riverbent arrow clue or hidden ford mark. | Archery drill, river map, village defense request. | Riverbend Shot path or mixed-village rescue event. |
| Fenroth Moonharp | Banned verse, old song fragment, noble hush rumor. | Performance lesson, witness escort, reputation repair song. | Public truth event or bardic memory magic path. |
| Caerion Silverroot | Corrected spell margin or framework clue. | Cantrip refinement, spell safety lesson, academy request. | Merciful Geometry path or dangerous spell rewrite event. |
| Yumi Longwhisker | Odd contract clause or price anomaly. | Trade discount, contract lesson, shipment audit. | Market stabilization event or Soft Ledger Trap path. |
| Kadrim Gearwise | Apology-marked cog or strange repair tag. | Repair lesson, machine capture request, part discount. | Cog-Soul rewrite path or clockwork containment event. |

Reward cooldowns:

- **Soft Clue:** Short cooldown. Players can find multiple different clues, but duplicates should convert to small knowledge or minor coin value.
- **Indirect Contact:** Moderate cooldown. Repeated indirect contact should deepen one lead instead of spawning many unrelated leads.
- **Witness Contact:** Longer cooldown. A legend should not constantly appear in the same area.
- **Direct Contact:** Long cooldown unless the player is on an active chain.
- **Crisis Contact:** Event cooldown. The region should recover before another crisis of the same type.
- **Fate Contact:** Extremely long cooldown. This should feel like a story people remember.

Reward dice interaction:

- Legendary encounters can use loot dice only when there is a clear loot moment, such as rescue reward, crisis reward, raid reward, rare material recovery, or event chest.
- Loot dice should improve reward quality or add bonus rolls, not replace trust requirements.
- Black market or crafted loot dice can improve chances, but cannot force a fate encounter or bypass relationship gates.
- High rolls can reveal better clues, extra materials, stronger discounts, rare titles, or future meeting chances.
- Very low rolls can still give story value, such as a warning, funny failure, damaged item, suspicious witness, or rival attention.

### Legendary Encounter Failure Surge Table V0.1

When a player fails, ignores, betrays, or wildly mishandles a legendary encounter, the result should feel unpredictable, a little dangerous, and sometimes hilarious. This table works like a wild surge of consequences: failure does not simply end the quest, it bends the story.

Use this table when:

- A player fails a legendary encounter roll badly.
- A party botches a direct, crisis, or fate contact.
- A player betrays a legend, breaks an oath, steals from them, or exploits their trust.
- A legend-related event is ignored until it worsens.
- A loot dice roll around a legendary encounter goes extremely low.
- The game needs a strange consequence that makes the world feel alive.

Failure surge rules:

- Roll d100 or let the encounter system choose an outcome that fits the moment.
- Mild outcomes are common; world-changing outcomes should be rare.
- If a result does not fit the current legend, reroll or reinterpret it through that legend's theme.
- Funny outcomes should embarrass, complicate, or redirect the player without ruining long-term progress.
- Severe outcomes should create recovery paths whenever possible.
- Party failures can affect the whole party, but personal betrayals should follow the responsible player.

| d100 | Failure Surge Result |
|---:|---|
| 1 | **Lucky Disaster:** The player fails the task, but finds a soft clue pointing to a safer second chance. |
| 2 | **Embarrassing Witness:** A nearby NPC sees the failure and spreads a slightly exaggerated version. |
| 3 | **Bent Map Marker:** The player's map gains a marker that is close, but annoyingly wrong. |
| 4 | **Damaged Proof:** The clue survives, but it is cracked, wet, burned, sticky, or hard to verify. |
| 5 | **Wrong Legend Rumor:** The player is now chasing a rumor tied to a different legendary NPC. |
| 6 | **Guild Clerk Error:** The quest is filed under the wrong guild board for one in-game week. |
| 7 | **Polite Rejection:** The legend hears about the failure and refuses contact until a small apology task is completed. |
| 8 | **Bad First Impression:** Trust starts lower than normal if the player later meets the legend. |
| 9 | **Rival Gets Credit:** A rival NPC completes part of the objective and gains local praise. |
| 10 | **Minor Price Sting:** Related vendors raise prices slightly until the player repairs their reputation. |
| 11 | **Bardic Mockery:** A short tavern song about the failure appears in nearby inns. |
| 12 | **Suspicious Package:** The failed clue becomes a decoy delivery that attracts rogue attention. |
| 13 | **Slime Cleanup Fee:** Cleaner slimes arrive and charge the player for the mess. |
| 14 | **Lost Component:** One useful quest component is lost and must be replaced. |
| 15 | **Awkward Title:** The player gains a temporary local nickname that is not flattering. |
| 16 | **Mistranslated Rune:** A rune clue is translated badly, creating a wrong but funny objective. |
| 17 | **Nervous Witness:** A witness refuses to talk unless the player brings food, safety, or proof of honesty. |
| 18 | **Delayed Courier:** A message arrives late, shifting the quest timer forward. |
| 19 | **Overhelpful NPC:** A background NPC tries to help and makes the situation more complicated. |
| 20 | **Sticky Evidence:** The proof item cannot be removed from the player's hand for a short time. |
| 21 | **Guild Fine:** The player receives a small fine for mishandling an official request. |
| 22 | **Broken Tool:** A relevant tool loses durability or needs repair before the quest can continue. |
| 23 | **False Confidence:** The next clue looks certain but requires verification before it can be trusted. |
| 24 | **Small Crowd:** Bystanders gather, making stealth or subtle negotiation harder. |
| 25 | **Misplaced Respect:** A strange NPC admires the failure and begins following the player's career. |
| 26 | **Contract Smudge:** A merchant contract becomes legally ambiguous and needs review. |
| 27 | **Animal Disapproval:** Nearby animals avoid the player until they complete a gentle action. |
| 28 | **Forge Shame:** Smiths laugh quietly unless the player repairs or replaces what was damaged. |
| 29 | **Rogue Tax:** A local thief claims the failed job created extra risk and demands payment. |
| 30 | **Soft Lockout:** The direct meeting path closes temporarily, but a longer alternate route opens. |
| 31 | **Faction Whisper:** A minor faction starts watching the player. |
| 32 | **Oath Bruise:** Broken promises reduce trust gains with honorable NPCs for a short period. |
| 33 | **Slippery Trail:** Tracking takes longer because the route is muddied, slimed, crowded, or magically blurred. |
| 34 | **Wrong Audience:** The player explains the problem to someone who absolutely should not have heard it. |
| 35 | **Leaking Secret:** A private clue becomes public rumor before the player can act. |
| 36 | **Hostile Discount:** A vendor offers a discount only if the player admits fault publicly. |
| 37 | **Suspicion Spark:** The legend's suspicion rises, even if no permanent harm is done. |
| 38 | **Training Delay:** A lesson or unlock is delayed until one extra trust action is completed. |
| 39 | **Companion Concern:** A companion, tamed creature, or party NPC questions the player's judgment. |
| 40 | **Unwanted Escort:** A guild sends an observer to follow the player during the next related task. |
| 41 | **Moral Stain:** If the failure involved cruelty, local good-aligned NPCs react negatively. |
| 42 | **Criminal Interest:** If the failure involved theft or lies, the Thieves Guild becomes curious. |
| 43 | **Holy Complaint:** If the failure involved the dead, the Church demands an explanation. |
| 44 | **Merchant Flag:** If the failure involved trade, the Merchants Guild flags the player as risky. |
| 45 | **Knight Notice:** If the failure threatened public safety, the Knights Guild opens a minor report. |
| 46 | **Adventurer Snickering:** Adventurers hear about it and the player takes a reputation bruise. |
| 47 | **Archers' Bet:** The Archers Guild starts betting on whether the player can fix the problem. |
| 48 | **Mage Warning:** Magic-related failure triggers a quiet warning from hidden mage channels. |
| 49 | **Debt Marker:** Someone helps clean up the failure, but now the player owes them. |
| 50 | **Clue Split:** The party receives two conflicting leads and must choose which one to trust. |
| 51 | **Rival Invitation:** A rival offers to help, clearly planning to profit from the player's embarrassment. |
| 52 | **Public Apology Option:** The fastest recovery path is a public apology or restitution. |
| 53 | **Resource Drain:** The related region consumes extra resources to cover the failure. |
| 54 | **Market Twitch:** A small price shift appears in related goods for several in-game days. |
| 55 | **Weather Insult:** Local weather turns inconvenient at the worst possible moment. |
| 56 | **Unstable Spell Echo:** If magic was involved, a harmless but visible spell echo follows the player briefly. |
| 57 | **Machine Misread:** Clockwork devices misidentify the player as maintenance staff, suspect, or owner. |
| 58 | **Slime Vote:** Local slimes hold a tiny court and issue a judgment the player may appeal. |
| 59 | **Animal Jury:** A tamer-related failure causes animals to watch the player silently for a while. |
| 60 | **Song Fragment:** Fenroth-style rumor magic turns the failure into half a song, but nobody knows the ending yet. |
| 61 | **Lost Time Window:** A route, shop, ferry, meeting, or event moves to a later real-time window. |
| 62 | **Evidence Theft:** A minor thief steals damaged proof, forcing the player to recover it. |
| 63 | **Witness Bribed:** Someone pays a witness to change their story. |
| 64 | **Wrongful Blame:** A background NPC is blamed for the player's failure unless the player intervenes. |
| 65 | **Monster Opportunity:** Nearby monsters exploit the distraction and move into a resource area. |
| 66 | **Bandit Interest:** Bandits hear that a legendary clue is loose and begin hunting it. |
| 67 | **Noble Interference:** A noble house attempts to control the narrative. |
| 68 | **Purist Exploitation:** Race-purity extremists twist the failure into propaganda. |
| 69 | **Poor District Cost:** The poor district pays a small price unless the player repairs the damage. |
| 70 | **Guild Rivalry:** Two guilds argue over who should control the cleanup quest. |
| 71 | **Quest Timer Starts:** A timed recovery quest begins immediately. |
| 72 | **Recovery Ingredient:** The player must gather a rare but not impossible ingredient to fix the failure. |
| 73 | **Harder Negotiation:** The next negotiation tied to this legend begins at a disadvantage. |
| 74 | **Proof Requires Expert:** The damaged evidence now requires a specialist to confirm it. |
| 75 | **Legend Sends A Warning:** The legend does not appear, but sends a message that they noticed. |
| 76 | **Legend Tests Again:** A harder but fair second test appears later. |
| 77 | **Legend Laughs:** The legend finds the failure amusing and lowers hostility, but raises expectations. |
| 78 | **Legend Offended:** The legend is insulted and requires restitution before further contact. |
| 79 | **Legend Concerned:** The legend suspects the player is in over their head and sends safer work first. |
| 80 | **Legendary Decoy:** A fake version of the legend appears, trying to exploit the player's confusion. |
| 81 | **Minor Raid Seed:** The failure adds progress toward a future local raid or defense event. |
| 82 | **Cartel Movement:** A market-related failure lets a cartel gain ground. |
| 83 | **Spell Mutation:** A magic-related failure mutates a local spell effect into a small hazard. |
| 84 | **Machine Escalation:** A clockwork-related failure lets a machine learn one bad habit. |
| 85 | **Slime Strike Warning:** Slime-related failure creates a warning before a local slime strike. |
| 86 | **Beast Migration Shift:** Tamer-related failure nudges creatures toward farms, roads, or camps. |
| 87 | **Border Tension:** Barbarian, knight, or archer-related failure raises conflict near a border. |
| 88 | **Hidden Enemy Flag:** A secret enemy marks the player as useful, dangerous, or easy to manipulate. |
| 89 | **Server Rumor:** A wider rumor spreads, but not yet a full announcement. |
| 90 | **Reputation Split:** Some NPCs admire the player's attempt while others condemn the outcome. |
| 91 | **Rare Second Chance:** The player gets one clean recovery opportunity, but the reward cap is lowered. |
| 92 | **Dangerous Favor:** A powerful NPC offers to fix the problem for a troubling price. |
| 93 | **Betrayal Echo:** If betrayal occurred, linked legends hear a distorted version. |
| 94 | **Emergency Summons:** The player is summoned to explain themselves before a guild, court, or faction. |
| 95 | **Regional Event Spark:** The failure starts a small regional event that other players can notice. |
| 96 | **Raid Escalation:** The failure advances a raid clock tied to monsters, machines, factions, or markets. |
| 97 | **Relic Disturbance:** A nearby relic, artifact, or sealed item reacts badly. |
| 98 | **Fate Reversal:** The failure blocks the original path but opens a stranger, rarer path. |
| 99 | **Legendary Intervention:** The legend appears personally, saves the worst outcome, and demands repayment. |
| 100 | **Catastrophic Story Seed:** The failure becomes a server-notable tale, future event, or long-term rivalry. |

Legend-flavored failure twists:

- **Hilda Hammerwake:** Failed work can create forge shame, damaged memorial steel, angry dwarven tribunals, or a chance to prove respect for the dead.
- **Sable Vey Rook:** Failed stealth can create decoys, false routes, courier debts, blackmail, or a chase through places that were not on the map yesterday.
- **Ormak Wolfbanner:** Failed restraint can raise border tension, trigger escort danger, or force the player to learn that rage without discipline is just noise.
- **Mallow Crown-Gel:** Failed slime diplomacy can start slime strikes, sanitation problems, alchemy shortages, or a tiny legal hearing conducted in wetland bubbles.
- **Saelira Mosswhisper:** Failed taming can shift migrations, hurt trust with animals, or reveal that the monster was protecting something.
- **Lethian Reedmark:** Failed scouting can expose villages, create false patrol reports, or let purists claim moral high ground.
- **Fenroth Moonharp:** Failed truth quests can become mocking songs, dangerous rumors, or a half-sung scandal nobody can stop repeating.
- **Caerion Silverroot:** Failed spellwork can mutate frameworks, injure students, cause academy lockdowns, or create visible magical side effects.
- **Yumi Longwhisker:** Failed trade quests can shift prices, empower cartels, lock discounts, or create contract clauses that bite later.
- **Kadrim Gearwise:** Failed artificer work can teach machines bad habits, trigger containment events, or make clockwork creatures afraid of the player.

### Legendary Encounter Critical Success Deck V0.1

When a player handles a legendary encounter perfectly, rolls extremely high, makes a brilliant choice, or creates a moment the world should remember, the system can draw from the **Legendary Many-Fates Deck**. This is a dramatic success deck inspired by the feeling of drawing a powerful fate card, but built specifically for Huwan.

Core rule:

- Critical success should open a meaningful door, not hand over the whole kingdom.
- A card can grant access, reputation, clues, rare opportunities, discounts, temporary blessings, relationship jumps, or future event paths.
- The strongest cards should start legendary questlines or unlock the right to pursue power, not instantly award final legendary gear.
- A card draw should feel rare enough that players remember where they were when it happened.

When to draw:

- A natural critical success during a legendary encounter.
- A perfect solution to a legend's test.
- A flawless party performance in a crisis contact.
- A fate-level encounter resolved with exceptional skill, mercy, cleverness, or courage.
- A legendary loot dice roll reaches its highest outcome.
- A player makes a choice that strongly matches the legend's values.

Draw rules:

- **Soft Clue or Indirect Contact:** Draw from Common or Uncommon cards.
- **Witness or Direct Contact:** Draw from Uncommon or Rare cards.
- **Crisis Contact:** Draw from Rare or Epic cards.
- **Fate Contact:** Draw from Epic or Mythic cards.
- **One Draw Limit:** Most encounters allow only one card.
- **Party Draws:** A party draws one shared card unless the event specifically rewards individual roles.
- **Cooldown:** Rare, Epic, and Mythic cards should have long cooldowns.
- **No Forced Farming:** Cards cannot be forced through relogging, rapid zone entry, sleep, or repeated menu actions.

| Card | Rarity | Critical Success Effect |
|---|---|---|
| **The Open Door** | Common | A hidden route, back entrance, safe road, or alternate meeting path becomes available. |
| **The Clear Bell** | Common | Nearby NPCs confirm the truth of a rumor, removing one false lead. |
| **The Warm Hand** | Common | The legend starts with a small trust bonus during the next meeting. |
| **The Honest Coin** | Common | A merchant, guild clerk, or witness waives a fee connected to the encounter. |
| **The Steady Tool** | Common | A relevant tool avoids durability loss and gains a temporary quality bonus. |
| **The Good Witness** | Common | A background NPC becomes willing to testify, guide, or verify proof. |
| **The Clean Map** | Common | A vague map marker becomes more accurate, clearing local battle fog around the clue. |
| **The Second Voice** | Common | A party member notices a detail the player missed, adding an optional objective. |
| **The Bright Thread** | Uncommon | The next step of the quest chain becomes easier to discover. |
| **The Quiet Key** | Uncommon | A locked social, physical, or magical barrier can be bypassed once. |
| **The Respectful Bow** | Uncommon | The legend hears of the player's conduct and respects them before meeting. |
| **The Polished Ledger** | Uncommon | A market, auction, or contract clue becomes clear without extra cost. |
| **The Gentle Snare** | Uncommon | A nonlethal solution becomes available in a hunt, capture, or defense event. |
| **The Hidden Seat** | Uncommon | The player receives quiet access to a restricted meeting, class, shop, archive, or performance. |
| **The Returned Favor** | Uncommon | An NPC helped earlier returns at the perfect moment. |
| **The Useful Rumor** | Uncommon | A rumor spreads in the player's favor, slightly improving local reputation. |
| **The Silver Margin** | Rare | A spell, recipe, contract, route, or rune gains a safer alternate version. |
| **The Unbroken Oath** | Rare | An oath, promise, or honorable choice becomes publicly recognized. |
| **The Black Route** | Rare | A hidden courier, smuggler, or rogue route opens for limited use. |
| **The Mercy Strike** | Rare | A combat success resolves without unnecessary death, earning respect from honorable legends. |
| **The Soft Crown** | Rare | A monster, slime, beast, or construct recognizes the player as safe to approach. |
| **The Fine Edge** | Rare | A crafted or repaired item gains a temporary masterwork quality. |
| **The Listening Grove** | Rare | Nature, animals, plants, or local spirits reveal a route, warning, or hidden witness. |
| **The True Verse** | Rare | A bardic truth spreads before enemies can control the story. |
| **The Sealed Spark** | Epic | A hidden quest chain activates but remains private until the player chooses to pursue it. |
| **The Mentor's Glance** | Epic | The legend personally notices the player and may offer a test, lesson, or private summons. |
| **The Debt of Power** | Epic | A powerful NPC owes the player one limited favor, with strict boundaries. |
| **The Lucky Forge** | Epic | The next relevant crafting attempt gains a major quality boost if proper materials are used. |
| **The Silent Arrow** | Epic | The player can bypass one ambush, patrol, or pursuit through perfect timing. |
| **The Living Contract** | Epic | A fair agreement gains extra perks for both sides if honored. |
| **The Tamed Moment** | Epic | A dangerous creature, machine, slime, or spirit pauses long enough for peaceful resolution. |
| **The Festival Flame** | Epic | A public success becomes a local celebration, raising reputation and unlocking event vendors. |
| **The White Stair** | Mythic | A path toward a secret teacher, hidden guild, relic lead, or pocket-world contact appears. |
| **The Dragon's Courtesy** | Mythic | A dragon, ancient beast, or dungeon intelligence chooses observation over hostility once. |
| **The World Takes Note** | Mythic | The server gains a rumor about the player before the full story is known. |
| **The Unwritten Page** | Mythic | The player may choose one of two future quest directions that normally would be random. |
| **The Kind Relic** | Mythic | A relic, artifact, or legendary item reacts positively without becoming owned. |
| **The Crowned Road** | Mythic | A dangerous regional route becomes temporarily safe because the player's success inspired others. |
| **The Fate-Touched Skill** | Mythic | The player gains progress toward a rare skill, but must still complete training to unlock it. |
| **The Many-Fates Smile** | Mythic | Draw two lower-rarity cards and choose one. The rejected card becomes a future rumor. |

Legend-flavored critical success twists:

- **Hilda Hammerwake:** Cards appear as forge sparks, memorial names, cleaner steel, oath heat, or a rare chance to work beside her.
- **Sable Vey Rook:** Cards appear as black-route doors, coded notes, vanishing witnesses, decoy swaps, or a package that chooses not to betray the player.
- **Ormak Wolfbanner:** Cards appear as controlled rage, peaceful intimidation, caravan respect, treaty leverage, or warbands choosing restraint.
- **Mallow Crown-Gel:** Cards appear as slime votes, royal gel seals, cleaner slime help, sanitation miracles, or evolved slimes recognizing the player as a person too.
- **Saelira Mosswhisper:** Cards appear as calm beasts, safe tracks, animal witnesses, migration openings, or a creature allowing touch for the first time.
- **Lethian Reedmark:** Cards appear as impossible arrow paths, river routes, hidden fords, village trust, or purist lies breaking at the perfect moment.
- **Fenroth Moonharp:** Cards appear as true verses, public courage, witness songs, reputation waves, or a crowd going silent at exactly the right line.
- **Caerion Silverroot:** Cards appear as corrected geometry, safer spellwork, calm mana, helpful margins, or a framework that finally behaves.
- **Yumi Longwhisker:** Cards appear as balanced ledgers, honest contracts, price stability, auction advantage, or trade partners realizing fairness can still profit.
- **Kadrim Gearwise:** Cards appear as cooperative machines, apology cogs, stable cores, harmless sparks, or clockwork creatures choosing curiosity over panic.

Critical success limits:

- A card cannot instantly grant SSS rank, demi-god progress, full legendary ownership, or godlike authority.
- A card can reveal a path to power, but the player must still earn the path.
- A card cannot override another player's protected story, guest login, or moderator-level role.
- A card should never erase consequences that already happened; it can create a way to repair, redirect, or rise above them.
- The best cards create stories other players hear about.

### Legendary Encounter Probability Table V0.1

Legendary encounters should feel possible, but never routine. The player should believe the world can surprise them, while still understanding that most legendary meetings require luck, reputation, context, or effort.

Probability is checked only during **eligible moments**, not constantly. Eligible moments include completing a quest, entering a meaningful region, following a rumor, gathering in a rare area, finishing a difficult craft, surviving an ambush, opening a strange map, completing an escort, discovering a ruin, triggering a market anomaly, or helping an NPC tied to a legend's theme.

Base odds per eligible encounter check:

| Encounter Result | Base Chance | Intended Feeling |
|---|---:|---|
| No Hit | 93.000% | Most world activity remains normal. |
| Soft Clue | 5.000% | Rare but believable clue, rumor, mark, or strange object. |
| Indirect Contact | 1.200% | The legend affects the scene without appearing directly. |
| Witness Contact | 0.550% | Memorable sighting or brief near-meeting. |
| Direct Contact | 0.200% | Very rare direct interaction, test, warning, or small request. |
| Crisis Contact | 0.045% | Extremely rare urgent legendary event. |
| Fate Contact | 0.005% | Almost unbelievable moment that players talk about later. |

Modifier categories:

| Modifier | Low Bonus | High Bonus | Notes |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Luck Stat | +0.10% | +1.50% | Adds to the non-No Hit pool, mostly improving Soft Clue and Indirect Contact. |
| World Popularity | +0.10% | +2.00% | Increases the chance that legends, rivals, or informants notice the player. |
| Relevant Class | +0.25% | +1.50% | Blacksmiths find smithing legends, rogues find rogue routes, tamers find beast clues. |
| Relevant Job | +0.25% | +1.25% | Merchant, artificer, hunter, dungeon explorer, bard, and similar jobs affect context. |
| Guild Reputation | +0.25% | +1.75% | Higher guild trust creates more notices, introductions, and named requests. |
| Active Rumor | +1.00% | +4.00% | Strongest normal modifier, but mostly improves clues and indirect contact. |
| Danger Zone | +0.25% | +2.50% | More danger means more chance, but worse consequences on failure. |
| Rare Map Or Proof | +1.00% | +5.00% | Can improve quality of encounter, but cannot force a specific legend. |
| Recent Heroic Action | +0.25% | +2.00% | Mercy, rescue, protection, and fair trade attract certain legends. |
| Recent Cruel Action | +0.25% | +2.00% | Betrayal, exploitation, over-harvesting, and violence can attract darker attention. |

Probability caps:

- Normal exploration legendary hit chance should usually cap at **15%** per eligible check.
- Strong rumor or proof-based checks can cap at **25%**.
- Active quest-chain checks can cap at **40%**, but only for the appropriate legend pool.
- Fate Contact should normally cap at **0.05%**.
- Earned Fate Contact during a major quest, raid, relic crisis, or server event can cap at **0.20%**.
- Direct summons, scripted meetings, and completed proof chains do not use the random lottery.

Roll order:

1. Confirm the moment is eligible.
2. Check cooldowns and anti-farm rules.
3. Build a context pool from region, biome, guild, class, job, reputation, party state, and recent behavior.
4. Apply modifiers and caps.
5. Roll encounter tier.
6. Select a legend or legend-related clue from the weighted pool.
7. Generate reward, failure, or critical success effects if needed.
8. Record memory, cooldown, journal, and world-state changes.

### Legendary Progress Step 1: Eligibility Gate Layer V0.1

The lottery should only roll when the player does something meaningful. This prevents farming and keeps legendary encounters tied to real play.

Eligible triggers include:

- Finishing a quest or quest step.
- Discovering a new area, ruin, dungeon floor, hidden room, route, or map section.
- Completing a rare gather, high-quality craft, successful trade, difficult taming attempt, or dangerous escort.
- Helping an NPC with a real simulated need.
- Surviving a battle above the player's expected difficulty.
- Entering a festival, auction, tribunal, peace summit, raid zone, or faction event.
- Handling a rumor, proof object, sealed package, broken relic, strange machine core, or unusual monster behavior.

Non-eligible triggers include:

- Opening and closing menus.
- Rapidly entering and leaving the same zone.
- Logging in and out.
- Sleeping.
- Repeating the same trivial action.
- Standing near a known rumor spot without doing anything meaningful.

### Legendary Progress Step 2: Context Tag Layer V0.1

Every eligible check should carry context tags. These tags tell the system which legends make sense without making outcomes predictable.

Context tag examples:

| Tag Type | Examples |
|---|---|
| Region | Nanira, Aulend, Egand, Ifai, Eabus, Emea, Drizis, Aurelith, Dawnspire, Alseaia. |
| Biome | Forest, mountain, river, lake, dungeon, ruin, city, market, road, wetland, academy, forge. |
| Activity | Crafting, gathering, escorting, trading, sneaking, singing, taming, spellwork, repairing, scouting. |
| Social | Noble, poor district, guild, faction, mixed-race settlement, church, black market, refugee route. |
| Risk | Safe, guarded, dangerous, cursed, contested, dungeon-born, raid-adjacent, relic-adjacent. |
| Behavior | Merciful, greedy, honorable, cruel, clever, reckless, patient, exploitative, protective. |

The system should use tags to weight the legend pool. A player repairing a strange clockwork core on Aurelith should be more likely to find Kadrim-related content, while a player calming beasts in Aulend should be more likely to find Saelira-related content.

### Legendary Progress Step 3: Weighted Legend Selection Layer V0.1

After the encounter tier is rolled, the system chooses a legend, clue, faction, or event from a weighted pool.

Selection rules:

- Directly relevant legends receive high weight.
- Regionally nearby legends receive medium weight.
- Random distant legends receive low weight so the world can still surprise players.
- A legend on cooldown can still generate soft clues, but not repeated direct meetings.
- A legend involved in an active event becomes more likely to appear indirectly.
- Protected Legendary True characters are not pulled by this lottery unless the user creates a specific moderator or guest event.

Weight example:

| Match Type | Weight |
|---|---:|
| Same legend quest already active | 100 |
| Same profession, guild, and region | 80 |
| Same profession or guild | 60 |
| Same region or route | 45 |
| Same faction theme | 35 |
| Same biome or activity | 25 |
| Distant but plausible | 10 |
| Pure world surprise | 3 |

### Legendary Progress Step 4: Cooldown And Anti-Farm Layer V0.1

Cooldowns preserve wonder. Once a player or party receives a meaningful legendary result, the system should prevent immediate repeats.

Suggested cooldowns:

| Encounter Type | Player Cooldown | Party Cooldown | Region Cooldown |
|---|---|---|---|
| Soft Clue | Short | Short | None or short. |
| Indirect Contact | Moderate | Moderate | Short. |
| Witness Contact | Long | Long | Moderate. |
| Direct Contact | Very long | Very long | Moderate to long. |
| Crisis Contact | Event-based | Event-based | Long recovery window. |
| Fate Contact | Extremely long | Extremely long | Server-notable cooldown. |

Anti-farm responses:

- Repeated suspicious behavior can convert possible hits into No Hit or Soft Clue only.
- Crowding a rumor area can spawn decoys, false rumors, guild warnings, or crowd events.
- Using multiple accounts or parties to camp a location should not multiply fate odds.
- Cooldowns should follow the player, party, and relevant region.

### Legendary Progress Step 5: Party, Raid, And Contribution Layer V0.1

Legendary encounters should support groups without letting parties multiply odds unfairly.

Group rules:

- Parties roll once as a group when acting together.
- The highest relevant Luck, reputation, class, or job can help, but bonuses should be capped.
- Members not present for discovery do not automatically gain full proof.
- Party members can share quest visibility, but training unlocks may require personal eligibility.
- Raid encounters use contribution scoring: most damage, last hit, healing, tanking, utility, objective completion, rescue actions, and leadership.
- Raid rewards can use loot dice, but trust and relationship unlocks still depend on behavior and eligibility.

Contribution categories:

| Category | Examples |
|---|---|
| Combat | Damage, tanking, interrupts, control, survival. |
| Support | Healing, buffs, cleansing, rescue, reviving. |
| Utility | Scouting, trap removal, map reading, crafting, repairs. |
| Social | Negotiation, intimidation, persuasion, leadership. |
| Moral | Mercy, restraint, oathkeeping, avoiding unnecessary deaths. |

### Legendary Progress Step 6: Encounter Director Flow Layer V0.1

The encounter director is the hidden system that decides how a legendary encounter appears.

Director flow:

1. Identify the player's current action and context tags.
2. Check whether the action is eligible.
3. Read player, party, guild, faction, reputation, and cooldown state.
4. Roll probability and encounter tier.
5. Select a legend or legend-adjacent event from the weighted pool.
6. Choose presentation: rumor, object, NPC witness, map clue, direct scene, emergency event, or fate moment.
7. Attach reward, failure, or critical success handling.
8. Update memories, world state, cooldowns, map, journal, and possible guild boards.

Presentation rules:

- Soft Clues should be subtle.
- Direct Contact should feel authored, even when randomly triggered.
- Crisis Contact should immediately create urgency.
- Fate Contact should use special music, HUD treatment, server rumor behavior, or cinematic framing later in development.

### Legendary Progress Step 7: HUD, Journal, And Map Layer V0.1

Legendary encounters need UI support without revealing too much.

HUD rules:

- Soft clues should appear as subtle journal pulses, not giant alerts.
- Confirmed proof can create map markers.
- Unverified rumors create vague zones, not exact locations.
- Party-shared clues should identify who discovered the clue.
- Dangerous legendary events should show urgency, time limits, and party visibility.
- Fate Contact can trigger a unique notification style, but should not spoil the reward path.

Journal states:

| State | Player-Facing Meaning |
|---|---|
| Rumor | "Someone mentioned something strange." |
| Lead | "There may be a real trail to follow." |
| Proof | "This legend or event is confirmed." |
| Contact | "A meeting or response is possible." |
| Trust | "This legend may offer deeper work." |
| Crisis | "This situation can worsen if ignored." |
| Fate | "This may change more than one quest." |

### Legendary Progress Step 8: Dialogue, Rumor, And Witness Layer V0.1

Legendary encounters should often reach players through people before systems.

Dialogue rules:

- Common NPCs can mention rumors they only half understand.
- Guild leaders can verify certain clues.
- Merchants can notice price oddities before they understand the cause.
- Bards can turn encounters into songs, warnings, mockery, or reputation waves.
- Poor district NPCs may know hidden truths because they see who comes and goes.
- Children, workers, guards, and background NPCs can become accidental witnesses.

Rumor quality:

| Quality | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Dirty Rumor | Mostly wrong, but points toward a real theme. |
| Useful Rumor | Contains one actionable clue. |
| Verified Rumor | Confirmed by proof, guild record, or reliable witness. |
| Planted Rumor | Created by enemies, rivals, nobles, cartels, cults, or factions. |
| Living Rumor | Changes as the world state changes. |

### Legendary Progress Step 9: Economy, Market, And Resource Layer V0.1

Legendary encounters should connect to the living economy whenever relevant.

Economy rules:

- A forge crisis can raise metal, charcoal, weapon repair, and mercenary prices.
- A slime strike can raise sanitation, alchemy reagent, and potion prices.
- A cloth cartel can raise clothing, pouch, quiver, and uniform prices.
- A machine outbreak can raise repair-kit, component, and artificer service prices.
- A monster migration can lower road safety and raise escort fees.
- A bardic scandal can affect noble donations, festival spending, and faction reputation.

Market effects should usually be local first, then regional, then server-wide only if the event escalates.

### Legendary Progress Step 10: Data, Testing, And Balance Handoff Layer V0.1

When implemented, legendary encounter logic should be data-driven so designers can tune it without rewriting code.

Recommended data fields:

| Field | Purpose |
|---|---|
| EncounterId | Unique encounter or clue id. |
| LegendId | Linked legend, if any. |
| Tier | Soft Clue, Indirect, Witness, Direct, Crisis, Fate. |
| Tags | Region, biome, activity, guild, class, job, risk, behavior. |
| BaseWeight | How likely this entry is within its pool. |
| Requirements | Reputation, proof, skill, job, guild, item, map, or relationship needed. |
| CooldownScope | Player, party, region, server, legend, or faction. |
| RewardPool | Normal rewards, critical cards, loot dice tables, training paths. |
| FailurePool | Failure surge entries, recovery paths, faction reactions. |
| WorldEffects | Market shifts, event clocks, raid seeds, faction movement. |
| JournalText | Player-facing rumor, lead, proof, or quest text. |
| DebugNotes | Designer-only notes for testing and balance. |

Testing checklist:

- Verify players cannot farm encounters by relogging, zoning, sleeping, or menu actions.
- Verify Luck helps, but does not make legendary meetings common.
- Verify parties do not multiply odds unfairly.
- Verify false rumors are annoying in a fun way, not confusing in a broken way.
- Verify Soft Clues happen often enough to make the world feel alive.
- Verify Direct and Fate Contact remain rare.
- Verify failures create recovery paths.
- Verify rewards open progression without giving away legendary power for free.
- Verify economy and faction effects revert, stabilize, or evolve after events.
- Verify the system creates stories players want to tell.

### Legendary Encounter System Completion V1.0

This section closes the Legendary Encounter design as a complete gameplay framework. It combines random lottery chance, rumor discovery, proof gathering, direct meetings, quest chains, rewards, failure surges, critical success cards, memory, economy, guild boards, party handling, raid escalation, and world-state changes into one usable system.

Design status:

- **World Bible Status:** Complete enough for systems design.
- **Content Status:** Ready for expansion into data tables, quests, dialogue, maps, and prototypes.
- **Implementation Status:** Needs engine-side data structures, encounter director logic, UI hooks, save/state tracking, and simulation integration.

Core promise:

- A player can live an ordinary day in Huwan and unexpectedly brush against legend.
- Most legendary encounters begin as clues, rumors, witnesses, or consequences.
- Direct meetings are rare.
- Fate-level encounters are extremely rare.
- No random encounter gives unearned ultimate power.
- Every success, failure, betrayal, and strange accident can become story.

#### Complete Legendary Encounter Flow

1. **Player Action:** The player does something meaningful, such as questing, crafting, gathering, escorting, trading, exploring, fighting, taming, repairing, or helping an NPC.
2. **Eligibility Gate:** The system checks whether the action qualifies for a legendary roll.
3. **Context Tags:** Region, biome, class, job, guild, risk, behavior, and current world state are collected.
4. **Cooldown Check:** Player, party, region, legend, faction, and server cooldowns are checked.
5. **Probability Roll:** The system rolls No Hit, Soft Clue, Indirect Contact, Witness Contact, Direct Contact, Crisis Contact, or Fate Contact.
6. **Weighted Selection:** The system chooses a legend, clue, rumor, object, witness, event, or faction thread from the weighted pool.
7. **Presentation:** The encounter appears through HUD, map, journal, NPC dialogue, object discovery, direct scene, emergency event, or server rumor.
8. **Player Response:** The player ignores, investigates, succeeds, fails, betrays, shares with party, or escalates the situation.
9. **Outcome Roll:** Rewards, failure surge, critical success deck, loot dice, or relationship changes are resolved.
10. **World Update:** NPC memory, guild boards, markets, region state, event clocks, quest chains, and player reputation update.
11. **Follow-Up:** The encounter becomes a rumor, quest, relationship, repeatable request, raid seed, or future fate path.

#### Complete Encounter Tier Matrix

| Tier | How It Appears | Player Risk | Reward Ceiling | Failure Ceiling | Follow-Up |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No Hit | Normal world activity. | None beyond normal play. | Normal rewards only. | Normal failure only. | None or tiny unrelated rumor. |
| Soft Clue | Mark, rumor, object, witness, song, map smudge. | Low. | Better clue, vague marker, small knowledge. | Wrong clue, damaged proof, funny rumor. | Rumor or Lead journal state. |
| Indirect Contact | Legend affects the scene without appearing. | Low to moderate. | Hidden flag, market hint, private board notice. | Decoy, suspicion, rival attention. | Lead or Proof journal state. |
| Witness Contact | Player briefly sees or nearly meets a legend. | Moderate. | Reputation bump, future meeting chance. | Bad impression, missed window, local gossip. | Proof or Contact journal state. |
| Direct Contact | Legend speaks, tests, warns, hires, or challenges. | Moderate to high. | Trust Tier 1, small quest, lesson, discount, contact. | Soft lockout, apology quest, distrust. | Contact or Trust journal state. |
| Crisis Contact | Urgent event involving a legend. | High. | Major trust gain, rare material, party event, raid seed. | Regional damage, raid clock, faction anger. | Crisis journal state. |
| Fate Contact | Extremely rare story-defining moment. | Very high. | Hidden chain, apprenticeship path, relic lead, server rumor. | Rivalry, world event, permanent reputation shift. | Fate journal state. |

#### Featured Legend Complete Encounter Packs

| Legend | Soft Clue | Direct Test | Crisis Contact | Fate Path | Final Unlock Direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hilda Hammerwake | A memorial ingot bears a grief-mark that hums near broken weapons. | Prove the dead named on a weapon mattered more than the weapon itself. | Her forge is attacked during an oath-bound craft. | The player is invited to help create a legendary weapon that remembers every life it defends. | Oath-bound forging, memorial steel, rare smithing authority. |
| Sable Vey Rook | A black-route token crawls across the table when moonlight touches it. | Deliver a sealed package without opening it, losing it, or asking the wrong question. | A courier route collapses and a living package begins waking up. | Sable offers access to a hidden courier war tied to Dawnspire. | Black-route access, anti-scrying delivery, rogue courier mastery. |
| Ormak Wolfbanner | A peace-banner thread is found wrapped around a broken arrow. | Stand between anger and violence without starting a fight. | A treaty caravan is ambushed and both sides blame each other. | Ormak calls the player to prevent a border war before the first horn sounds. | War-Rest Rage, treaty defense, barbarian restraint mastery. |
| Mallow Crown-Gel | A royal gel seal replays the touch of a frightened alchemist. | Prove an evolved slime is a person under local law. | Illegal harvesting causes a slime strike across sanitation routes. | Mallow asks the player to help establish the first slime-recognized settlement charter. | Slime diplomacy, slime taming, evolved slime rights, slime gear paths. |
| Saelira Mosswhisper | A wounded animal leaves tracks that circle back to help the player. | Calm a beast without killing, trapping, or humiliating it. | A monster migration threatens farms because its old habitat was destroyed. | Saelira asks the player to broker peace between settlement growth and wild survival. | First Language Listening, advanced taming, nonlethal monster handling. |
| Lethian Reedmark | A riverbent arrow is found lodged behind impossible cover. | Scout a crossing without exposing a mixed village. | Purist raiders disguise themselves as lawful patrols. | Lethian asks the player to save several villages without letting war ignite. | Riverbend Shot, hidden ford maps, mixed-village defense network. |
| Fenroth Moonharp | A banned verse matches a hidden archive record word for word. | Protect a witness long enough for truth to be sung. | Nobles attempt to stop a performance by force, bribery, and murder. | Fenroth lets the player decide whether a truth should save people now or burn history open. | Truth-song magic, bardic memory, reputation wave control. |
| Caerion Silverroot | A spell diagram corrects itself in silver-green ink. | Rewrite a dangerous framework without weakening its intended purpose. | A copied spell mutates and locks down part of an academy. | Caerion offers access to a hidden debate over whether forbidden magic should be taught safely. | Merciful Geometry, spell dampening, advanced wizard framework study. |
| Yumi Longwhisker | A contract clause exposes a shortage before the market notices. | Find the dishonest trade route without crashing innocent shops. | A cloth cartel threatens pouches, uniforms, robes, quivers, and poor-district clothing. | Yumi invites the player into a three-continent market correction. | Soft Ledger Trap, auction timing, merchant empire access. |
| Kadrim Gearwise | An apology-marked cog rolls back to the broken machine it came from. | Capture a clockwork creature alive and prove what instruction broke it. | Machines begin copying player tactics after a bad control-core design spreads. | Kadrim asks the player to decide whether clockwork beings deserve legal protection. | Cog-Soul rewriting, machine companion crafting, ethical artificing. |

#### Complete Resolution States

Every legendary encounter should end in one of these states:

| State | Meaning | World Result |
|---|---|---|
| Ignored | The player walks away or misses the moment. | The clue may decay, move, or become someone else's story. |
| Failed | The player tries and fails without betrayal. | Failure surge, reduced reward, recovery path, or rival progress. |
| Botched | The player fails badly or creates a public mess. | Stronger failure surge, reputation bruise, guild notice, or event clock. |
| Partial Success | The player solves one part but misses another. | Smaller reward, incomplete proof, alternate route, or delayed trust. |
| Clean Success | The player completes the objective properly. | Reward, memory update, quest progress, and possible trust gain. |
| Critical Success | The player handles the moment exceptionally. | Critical success deck draw, stronger trust, rare clue, or improved future path. |
| Betrayal | The player lies, steals, exploits, murders, or breaks trust. | Betrayal memory, lockout, enemy flag, faction consequence, or recovery trial. |
| Restitution | The player repairs harm from failure or betrayal. | Trust may recover, but memory remains. |
| Escalated | The event grows beyond the player. | Party, raid, regional, or server-level event. |
| Legacy Shift | The encounter changes the legend's long-term state. | New route, market change, faction shift, successor, retirement, rivalry, or public story. |

#### Server And Public Message Rules

Not every legendary encounter should broadcast. Most should remain personal, party-level, or regional.

| Message Scope | Used For | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Personal | Soft clues, small failures, private warnings. | "A strange forge-mark has been added to your journal." |
| Party | Shared discoveries, escort danger, group tests. | "Your party found proof of Sable's black-route token." |
| Guild | Board updates, named requests, guild reputation changes. | "The Merchants Guild has posted an emergency cloth audit." |
| Regional | Crisis events, market shifts, public songs, village defense. | "A slime strike has begun in the western wetlands." |
| Server Rumor | Fate contact, major crisis, rare critical success, legendary rivalry. | "Rumors spread that a player caught the attention of a hidden legend." |
| Server Announcement | Major world-state changes only. | Dragon dungeon death, legal status change, relic awakening, raid victory, or demi-god-level milestone. |

Server announcement limits:

- Soft Clue, Indirect Contact, and Witness Contact should never create full server announcements.
- Direct Contact can create local rumors, not global announcements.
- Crisis Contact can create regional or server rumors if enough players are affected.
- Fate Contact can create a server rumor, but should not reveal private details.
- Full server announcements should be reserved for events that change Huwan.

#### Complete Reward And Power Boundaries

Legendary encounters can reward:

- Rumors
- Proof
- Map hints
- Reputation
- Trust
- Discounts
- Training access
- Recipe access
- Rare material opportunities
- Temporary buffs
- Private summons
- Repeatable request access
- Companion leads
- Hidden class or job clues
- Relic paths
- Raid seeds
- Market opportunities
- Public story presence

Legendary encounters cannot randomly grant:

- SSS rank
- Demi-god status
- Moderator authority
- Protected guest characters
- Full legendary gear ownership without the required path
- Pantheon approval without the required path
- Permanent server control
- Instant class mastery
- Instant job mastery
- Forced relationship outcomes

#### Complete Failure Recovery Paths

Failure should hurt, but it should usually create a way forward.

Recovery methods:

- Pay restitution.
- Replace damaged goods.
- Publicly apologize.
- Complete a harder proof quest.
- Protect the harmed NPC.
- Expose the real culprit.
- Accept a lower reward cap.
- Let a rival gain temporary advantage.
- Serve the affected guild, region, or legend.
- Complete a noncombat trust repair task.
- Admit fault to the party or public.
- Wait for a living server cooldown to expire.

Betrayal recovery should be harder than normal failure recovery and should never fully erase memory.

Example data entry:

| Field | Example |
|---|---|
| EncounterId | LEG_HILDA_SOFT_INGOT_001 |
| LegendId | HILDA_HAMMERWAKE |
| Tier | Soft Clue |
| Tags | Egand, forge, smithing, memorial, weapon, honorable |
| BaseWeight | 80 |
| Requirements | Smithing interest or broken weapon clue. |
| CooldownScope | Player and region. |
| RewardPool | Forge-mark clue, smithing rumor, memorial ingot journal entry. |
| FailurePool | Damaged proof, forge shame, wrong apprentice rumor. |
| WorldEffects | None unless followed. |
| JournalText | "This ingot bears a grief-mark from a forge that remembers names." |
| DebugNotes | Good early Hilda clue for smithing players. |

Example fate entry:

| Field | Example |

| EncounterId | LEG_MALLOW_FATE_CHARTER_001 |

| Tags | wetland, slime, alchemy, law, mercy, monster-rights |

| CooldownScope | Player, party, region, legend, server rumor. |

| FailurePool | Slime strike, alchemy shortage, monster-rights backlash. |
| WorldEffects | Possible local legal status change for evolved slimes. |
